Pending orders contribute only scalar candidates, not full CONFIG/CONTRACT records. Unsaved input is not enrolled. Old order snapshots are never rewritten. In the 货号与包装 editor, new paper rows and previously unrecorded dimension units default to cm; explicitly recorded units remain unchanged. This is an editable form default, saved only by the normal revision-checked master action, and never rewrites original order or provenance snapshots. Counting units and packing-count defaults retain their prior behavior. Multiple configurations remain separate choices. CONFIG is not customer-bound: new signatures omit customer, identical formal configurations from different customers reuse one record with customer-tagged provenance; legacy IDs/customer metadata remain compatible and do not restrict reuse. Contracts and customer identifier/date rules retain customer scope. CONFIG maintenance does not require a customer; old semantic duplicates may still be deactivated or have notes edited. The 货号与包装 list defaults to active configurations; its Delete action uses revision-checked, audited deactivation with preferred cleared, preserving historical orders and provenance. Managers can select 停用 and restore that exact configuration for future orders. Server-calculated packing_name uses the first recorded configuration as 标准装 and compares one unambiguous outer-box products-per-carton count with matching product, paper-type composition and units: larger/smaller counts become 大包装/小包装 with count, otherwise 其他包装. Existing unnamed history chooses the earliest available immutable source timestamp (updated_at fallback; equal times use deterministic ID order, not proof of true chronology). The designated standard record remains the baseline even if inactive; privileged changes to its configuration recalculate other labels/revisions atomically without altering historical orders. New/different order configurations trigger a nonblocking possible-wrong-packaging reminder and are added only after formal confirmation, preserving the standard record. Factory/item identity and whole multi-paper sets include counting/dimension units and products-per-carton; different sets remain selectable with non-blocking reminders, and selecting one copies fields into the order without defaulting a price. Original fingerprints, current canonical content and recorded source aliases deduplicate evidence across privileged corrections, without overwriting edits, recommendation or inactive status. Configuration selection is revision-checked when creating or structurally editing an order; confirmation uses the saved paper snapshot rather than reloading an altered master configuration. Factory-scoped `carton_procurement:master_manage` is required for master edits and also permits existing customer maintenance. Warehouse and carton warehouse operators, and their related supervisors/managers, receive it through their default role definitions; no per-warehouse authorization is needed. Existing legacy operator roles receive the grant once with authorization revision invalidation; fixed positions reconcile through catalog fixed-v32. The warehouse keeper/supervisor/manager templates and legacy warehouse_keeper default also include carton_mark:read and carton_mark:template_upload for the scoped original-file library and uploads. Existing legacy warehouse_keeper grants are backfilled once with role/user authorization revision invalidation; later revocations, explicit denies and factory/department bindings remain intact. Pinned V2 role versions retain their frozen permissions and require the existing deliberate package-upgrade workflow to adopt these additions. User bindings, factory/department scopes and explicit denies remain authoritative. Inventory-write permission alone does not bypass master permission, and unrelated department roles and external carton warehouse readers gain no new access. Changes retain reasons and before/after audit evidence. Routine UI actions prefill editable reasons: master creation/correction, date rules, workshop/bin maintenance and enable/disable, unlocked-order edits/cancellation, receipt void/reversal, inventory reversal and stocktake cancellation (append/reduction/outbound already have defaults). Authorization, any change involving a hard format check, posted-order returns, closing unlock, missing-price/free-material evidence, manual inventory adjustment, stocktake discrepancies/recount returns and Other outbound purpose require explicit input. Stocktake cancellation and return reasons are separate. Backend reason requirements and permission checks remain enforced. Contract/item identities are immutable (replace and deactivate instead), while workshop names can change under their stable ID. Stopped contracts cannot be used for new order creation/confirmation; routine date/note edits of existing orders do not reapply new format rules. Identifier format checks and relationship/different-configuration reminders are separate. The order form shows each active customer format directly below its contract/item/customer-PO input (blank optional PO skips checks); mismatches use inline alerts with the expected format, missing rules explicitly say not configured, and OFF stays distinguishable from a passed check. These hints use the existing saved-template matcher and never learn or widen formats while typing. Contract/item/customer-PO format recognition prioritizes pasted complete identifiers; when examples are blank, the rule editor can infer from factory/customer-scoped CONTRACT and CONFIG provenance, including item/PO sources from historical orders whose incomplete packaging prevented CONFIG creation. Shared CONFIG sources are filtered by their actual source customer; blank historical PO never falls back to a contract number. Recognition errors and sample hints are independent per field. Each identifier rule can be deleted immediately with audited revision control, retaining historical orders; deletion clears only that rule. Deleting an item format returns it to OFF without automatic recapture; contract and optional PO resets remain automatic, and the next confirmed and locked order with a parseable identifier records a new frozen format while that rule remains in automatic mode. Deleting an existing hard check requires an explicit explanation. A cleared rule does not re-infer from old history in the editor. Item-number formats default to OFF in schemas, the master editor, customer summaries and order hints. Old unconfigured AUTO/HISTORY item formats (and unrestricted legacy WARN defaults) are projected as OFF while their stored JSON/templates remain unchanged; automatic contract/PO capture does not rewrite that item snapshot. Existing MANUAL, nontrivial legacy WARN and explicit BLOCK checks are preserved. An explicit customer-rule save enabling item checks records user_configured in RULE JSON, allowing the chosen AUTO/WARN/BLOCK behavior to remain effective. Formal confirmation records missing contract and nonempty customer PO formats from the first eligible identifier as frozen customer RULE snapshots; item capture runs only after explicit opt-in. Automatic capture requires a parseable identifier containing ASCII digits; other identifiers need manual format entry. Later orders do not widen frozen templates; manually disabled or nontrivial existing rules are preserved. Drafts and ordinary edits do not learn formats. A single example can seed a candidate; no evidence leaves segmented checks unconfigured. Templates use literal prefixes/separators and discrete ASCII-digit widths (SC{9}/{3,4} accepts nine digits before the slash and either three or four after it). Multiple templates are allowed; inference merges widths only when at most one numeric segment varies, avoiding unobserved cross-products. Users explicitly re-identify or edit the template to change it. AUTO/WARN remains advisory; only explicit BLOCK rejects mismatches in the backend. OFF disables checking. Old nontrivial prefix/length/character rules remain compatible; unrestricted legacy WARN without templates is treated as AUTO. Legacy contract/PO AUTO without a saved snapshot can acquire one on the next formally confirmed order; item AUTO requires explicit opt-in. The editor can still identify and save a format explicitly. Turning a hard check on or off requires a specific explanation. Current contracts remain factory/customer/contract scoped without year/batch. Orders additionally carry optional customer_po (migration 20260912_0108, empty by default for existing rows). Within a factory/customer, the same contract/item with different nonempty POs identifies separate orders; a repeated active nonempty PO/contract/item is rejected. Blank PO retains legacy compatibility, including legacy same-code orders. Explicit PO matching preserves Chinese and punctuation and ignores surrounding whitespace/case; ambiguous same-contract/item matches without PO require human selection. PO is retained by both historical import layouts, formal source evidence, immutable supplier document snapshots and cumulative exports. Customer RULE JSON adds customer_po_rule using the existing optional OFF/AUTO/WARN/BLOCK format mechanism; even BLOCK never requires a blank PO. An omitted PO in older update requests preserves the current value; changing PO is a structural edit subject to existing locks. Inventory continues to separate formal order-line IDs and is never merged by printed contract/item/PO text. Import matching_version invalidates pre-PO cached match results. Historical values never automatically become strict format rules. Master/provenance reads currently scan/return full history; large-history performance remains unverified. Published PostgreSQL upgrade acceptance is maintained in the canonical deployment section. The construction design in `docs/business/纸箱模块-基础资料建设方案.md` distinguishes implemented first-version behavior from expansion ideas.

Master-data paper options, 货号与包装 and 仓库与仓位 provide privileged Excel template download and preview/confirm import, all requiring factory-scoped master_manage without warehouse-specific overrides. Templates accept only `.xlsx`/`.xlsm` up to 5 MB and 1000 data rows, read only the `导入数据` sheet, and isolate examples in `说明与示例`. Paper type/quality cells each contain one value; length/width/height are separate positive-number columns joined into canonical `长*宽*高` specifications (all three may be empty only for scalar paper-option imports). Formulas, incomplete dimensions, conflicting group headers and duplicate paper lines are rejected. Paper options append with normalized deduplication, explicitly imported hidden values become visible, and unrelated factory RULE fields/status remain intact. Packaging imports require actual Excel text cells for item numbers; numeric cells are rejected even with zero-padding display formats, preserving leading zeroes and preventing silent precision loss. They group by item plus explicit group (blank means 默认), require repeated group headers to agree, and create complete multi-paper CONFIG variants without customer/price binding; an existing identical semantic configuration is skipped without changing its status, recommendation or notes. Location templates contain 仓库 and 仓位或范围 as actual text cells; one warehouse per cell, and single bins or comma/顿号/semicolon/newline-separated lists with same-prefix ascending ranges such as A1-A25、B01-B03. Single bins use Chinese/ASCII letters, digits or underscores, also accepting existing A-00-style names; ranges require two complete endpoints (including A-01-A-03), and padded endpoints must have equal digit widths. All rows together may expand to at most 1000 bins, including existing bins. Warehouse/bin names follow create_location trim+uppercase semantics; file duplicates are errors, while existing ACTIVE/INACTIVE bins only skip without changing status, inventory or history. 待核仓位 and CL-UNKNOWN identifiers cannot be imported. New ACTIVE bins establish their warehouse through the first bin without creating empty warehouses, and each has an INVENTORY_LOCATION_CREATED audit with reason 模板导入仓库仓位. Preview is read-only. Confirmation resubmits and reparses the same file under the factory transaction lock and compares a digest binding factory, import kind, file fingerprint and the complete master snapshot; location imports additionally include every factory location ID, warehouse, bin code, status and revision. Any changed input or relevant snapshot requires a new preview. Existing packing-name, preferred-single-choice, revision and audit rules are reused, and the whole batch, including location audits, commits atomically. Import dialogs block backdrop dismissal, reset on factory/file changes, and refresh master suggestions after successful confirmation. A successful master import stays completed and cannot be submitted again if the follow-up list refresh fails; its dialog explains the refresh failure separately.

Carton bulk-selection summaries remain mounted as a compact toolbar row at zero selection so checking rows does not shift the table. The exception workspace supports individual selection and select-all within the current filters, retains and displays hidden selections, and clears selection on factory change. Selected exceptions in the same state can be started, resolved or closed in an atomic batch of up to 500 records under the factory lock and expected revisions; handling explanations are optional for both single and bulk start/resolve/close actions, while state, scope and revision checks remain enforced. Exception listing loads every API page. Each batch item is flushed before the shared lock helper can expire ORM state, while commit happens only after all items succeed. The selected-item list opens only on explicit 查看已选 and floats over the page; clearing all selections closes it. Carton data-entry dialogs must not dismiss on backdrop clicks: order creation/editing, append/reduction, cancellation/return and movement reversal retain their entered values until explicit close/cancel or successful submission. Receipt, outbound/transfer and master-data editors likewise block outside dismissal; read-only previews retain their existing behavior. Order creation/editing and receipt posting report the failed step within the active dialog, with server error detail and the entered draft preserved. If the server write succeeded but local refresh or audit loading fails, feedback reports the saved/posted result and asks the operator to refresh; after an uncertain timeout, it directs the operator to check the ledger or receipt history before retrying.

The order ledger's paper-item total and arrival progress aggregate required and received quantities across every paper-item detail row, use generic `件` for the combined total, and retain each detail type's own unit in the breakdown. Multi-select supplier issuance accepts both initial and subsequent append/reduce/adjustment documents. Orders with an unissued net change create a new immutable issue, while selected orders with no new change reuse their latest issued snapshot in the batch without creating a duplicate number; orders having neither are skipped. The frontend uses an in-page accessible confirmation dialog whenever a batch contains a non-initial pending issue or a reused historical snapshot; it does not depend on native window.confirm, which may be unavailable in embedded browsers. Confirming downloads one batch workbook; cancellation, leaving the page or changing factory/selection dismisses the pending confirmation without issuing. The request retains the original factory, selection and order revisions, and duplicate clicks are blocked while processing.

Replacement procurement is separate from increasing original demand: More → 补单 requires order_write plus inventory_write, an already received active order, an issued original/baseline procurement document and no unissued ordinary quantity/date change. Responsibility is explicitly OWN (我方问题) or SUPPLIER (供应商问题); the extra note is optional. Submission immediately issues the selected quantities from exact formal paper-line/bin stock under the factory lock and creates an immutable B01/B02 replacement procurement snapshot in the same transaction. Original product/paper demand stays unchanged. Later normal receipt restores the replacement shortage: effective arrival is posted receipt minus cumulative replacement outbounds; receipt capacity, progress and completion use that net amount. Reductions protect pre-replacement fulfillment without counting replacement receipts twice; each B snapshot records its receipt-line evidence so authorized receipt reversal releases only invalidated protection. Frontend reduction limits use the server allowance. The replacement source is ORDER_REPLENISHMENT with OTHER purpose, not normal workshop usage or an inferred supplier credit, and cannot be reversed independently from its linked procurement. Current physical balance, valuation, locked months, factory scope, revisions and persistent request replay protect posting. B quantities export separately with responsibility, while ordinary A/R/C baselines and C numbering remain unchanged; ordinary and replacement issuance share the factory lock. Replacement receipt lines link to an exact B issue and freeze responsibility, inventory cost and supplier payable treatment in immutable RECEIPT_REPLENISHMENT_LINKED audit evidence. OWN receipts use their actual positive receipt price and accrue once by acceptance month; SUPPLIER receipts restore the original replacement outbound carrying cost automatically, require no chargeable-price entry and are excluded from supplier monthly payable sources. Verified zero inventory cost remains explicit price evidence; unknown original costs block free receipt until verified, never silently become zero. New ordinary formal receipts are classified separately in RECEIPT_ORDINARY_CLASSIFIED audit evidence. Pending and posted linked receipts reserve each B/paper quota; void/reversal releases it, and factory locking plus existing request replay prevents duplicate posting. Ordinary receipts cannot consume outstanding B quotas; multiple sources or mixed ordinary/replacement demand require explicit selection and separate receipt registrations for the same paper. A sole unambiguous B can be selected automatically. Saved receipt responses expose B number, responsibility and settlement_unit_price separately from the existing inventory unit_price. Receipts predating this linkage with unclassified posted evidence after B issuance are flagged replenishment_review_required, expose no reusable B quota and block further receipt/replacement allocation until existing correction/reentry resolves the ambiguity; supplier source rows also expose the unresolved linkage as a reconciliation issue, and old bills are never silently rewritten. Confirmed supplier periods and inventory locks retain their existing posting/reversal guards, including free receipts. New B document snapshots state the fee policy. No additional compensation or automatic supplier-return credit is created.

Carton-order schedule semantics distinguish the customer deadline from the supplier commitment. New orders require a customer due date and snapshot the customer-specific or factory-default safety lead (fallback three calendar days); the authoritative planned supplier due date is `max(order date, customer due date - snapshotted lead days)`, so urgent orders never produce a plan earlier than the order date and the UI warns when the full safety lead is unavailable. The server always recomputes this planned date instead of trusting a client-supplied value. Already stored historical rows retain their existing dates without silent backfill. Newly confirmed historical imports preserve the source planned due date; an explicit source customer due date is retained, while a missing customer due date is filled as source planned due date plus the active customer procurement safety lead, then the active factory default, then three calendar days (explicit zero is valid). The preview shows each fallback and freezes the effective rule/customer revision before atomic confirmation. This applies to both calculated legacy layouts and explicit wide demand layouts; historical import must not run the normal new-order planned-date recomputation. Supervisor append and ordinary date edits may provide a new customer due date but continue using the original order safety-lead snapshot. Optional suggested customer dates use a separately configured day interval, support explicit customer override/disable, and require active acceptance in the order form. Supplier documents, urgency reminders and due-range filters continue to use the planned supplier date, while the order ledger displays both dates. Advanced master-data maintainers configure defaults; these changes never rewrite existing order dates or safety-lead snapshots. Factory/customer RULE JSON also exposes production_days (0–365 calendar days, blank inherits the factory, system fallback seven days) for the supplier production-and-delivery interval. Active customer rules override active factory rules, including an explicit zero; inactive rules do not apply. It uses the existing master permission, revision and audit checks and requires no schema migration. This interval recalculates timing for unplaced business-schedule rows without changing historical order dates or inventory. Migration `20260907_0101` adds nullable customer due dates and non-null three-day safety-lead snapshots after injection V3 revision `20260905_0100`, indexes customer due dates by factory, and deliberately preserves every historical planned due date.

The order ledger keeps its filters, bulk actions, selection summary, table header and rows in one bordered card, with a divider below the toolbar rather than a gap between cards. The order ledger and pending-receipt ledger use contiguous rows within one shared table surface: a light header, thin horizontal separators, and selected/hover row backgrounds, without per-order card gaps, borders, rounded corners or shadows. The order ledger combines customer, status, relative due-window and inclusive order-date range filters with planned due-date and order-date sorting in its list toolbar; the workspace-header customer selector is hidden on the orders tab. The default `交期由近到远` consolidates the former urgency option: open orders sort by planned due date ascending, unresolved dates follow dated open orders, and completed/cancelled orders stay last. The remaining choices are `交期由远到近` and `下单日期最新`. Order and pending-receipt filters have a single clear action including the shared keyword; order search includes product names. The order ledger requests 50 records per page, with server-side filtering, sorting, translated-status/literal keyword search and totals. Internal order status and current supplier acceptance status are separate filters. The supplier filter uses the canonical current ordinary-issue/commitment projection before totals and paging; old or replacement acceptances cannot satisfy the current issue. Clearing filters preserves sort, with an explicit restore-default-sort action. Its request-local batch projection preserves the original receipt, replenishment, supplier-acceptance, usage and deletion rules without per-order SQL loops. Cross-page selected orders are refreshed by explicit order number before use. Weekly/inspection tables render 50 rows at a time; the weekly workspace fetches a lightweight order identity/status/split index and obtains full details on demand with stale-response protection. The dashboard uses a dedicated factory-authorized `/dashboard/workspace` read model: server-side customer/literal-search/status/kind filtering and full totals precede eight-row pagination; summary reminders, recent orders, priority exceptions and pending split previews are bounded, and inventory is summed separately by unit with decimal precision. Cold dashboard entry loads this projection and customer options rather than complete orders, import evidence, receipts, movements, audit and master ledgers. Source-to-exception matching is indexed once per batch in both the extracted frontend fallback and the backend projection, preserving exact source coordinates, ambiguous matches, newest effective identity, current marks/order links, splits and reminder semantics; resolved mismatch status alone never suppresses an unplaced-order reminder. Effective weekly batches are streamed without the former 50-batch cutoff for dashboard totals. Action context fetches current source evidence and a unique full order before the original permission/revision/quantity guards; reading never creates business records. Factory, viewer, filter/page and action generations discard stale responses; Shanghai midnight refreshes timing, and reminder navigation restores the exact source batch even beyond loaded weekly history. Server projection still scans effective history and authoritative inventory/split sources on each request; it is not a materialized constant-cost summary. Other workspaces retain their existing full-ledger business reads and master/provenance scans. These dashboard changes are locally implemented and regression/build verified, but remain undeployed; the production release still has the former cubic frontend matching path. Synthetic scale verification does not replace an actual production-browser profile and deployed latency check. Order, receiving, inventory and stocktake selections persist across filters but explicitly show hidden selection counts, an expandable complete selection list, individual deselection and clear-all. Shared search/customer controls appear only where wired to the current content; master data, stocktake and audit use local controls. The order-date filter shares the receipt planned-due range calendar: open once, click start and end consecutively to apply and close; selecting the same day twice filters a single day, and clearing restores all dates. Incomplete selections preserve the applied range, and changing factory resets it.

Formal-order corrections check expected revisions and require a reason. Ordinary update/cancel/bulk-cancel acquire the shared factory transaction lock before reading orders and checking revisions; concurrent requests carrying the same revision can accept only one change, and the other must refresh before retrying. Ordinary new orders enter `CONFIRMED`, which the UI presents as `待下单`; before confirmation and locking they may be revised, appended or cancelled. The formal order ledger More menu includes 复制订单信息 for users with current-factory order_write permission in carton or pmc-warehouse. It opens an editable new-order draft from any source status, including completed and cancelled orders, carrying customer, supplier, contract, customer PO, item/product, notes, quantity basis and all paper details including dimension/unit/price metadata. Order date defaults to today and both due dates must be reconfirmed; EXPLICIT demand and unknown product/packing quantities remain explicit/null. Source IDs, revisions, master configuration identity, fulfillment, receipt/shipment and supplier execution records are not reused. Only normal create submission saves a separate 待下单 order, which still requires confirmation and locking; changing factory closes the copied draft. The former manual offline_placed action/API remains retired (distinct from the warehouse replacement procurement described above); all manually entered orders follow ordinary confirmation and supplier issuance. Previously stored business/audit evidence is retained. A dedicated, audited `确认订单并锁定` transition changes `CONFIRMED` to `PENDING_SUPPLIER` (`已确认锁定`), increments the revision and locks ordinary structural edits and cancellation. Receipt creation and confirmation both require every referenced order to be `PENDING_SUPPLIER` or `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, so an order cannot enter inventory before confirmation and locking and a stale pending receipt cannot bypass the gate. The dedicated return action remains separate from order reduction: it posts outbound movements for positive on-hand balances tied to partially or fully received orders. The receipt-entry UI (manual and human-reviewed import/ad hoc) submits once with post_immediately=true and a durable client request_id. Creation, confirmation, physical allocations, inventory movements and order progress commit atomically under the factory lock; failures roll back the entire submission. Factory/actor/request-scoped audit evidence replays identical requests without posting again and rejects changed content. Successful responses lock the form before refreshing the ledger. The legacy API draft mode remains compatible, and existing pending receipts are explicitly confirmed or voided through history; they are never automatically posted or discarded. Direct posting, legacy receipt save and confirmation enforce posted plus all pending effective quantities against each exact order line requirement under the shared factory lock. Pending documents reserve their effective quantities; confirmation counts its own pending quantity only once, and void/reversal releases the corresponding reservation/posted quantity. Defects reduce the effective reservation, while standalone ad hoc receipts do not consume formal-order capacity. Old overbooked pending documents are blocked at confirmation until excess drafts are explicitly voided; they are never silently truncated or deleted. Concurrent saves cannot over-reserve. No implicit over-receipt tolerance is allowed.

Carton receipt posting, order-return stock deductions, outbound/adjustment/bulk movements, reversals, historical inventory import, relocation, stocktaking, closing review and missing-price confirmation share a factory-scoped database transaction lock before reading balances. When both transaction and supplier-row locks are needed, the transaction lock is always acquired first. Manual inventory operations are immutable ledger entries: outbound and adjustment movements can reference either a formal order line or an existing standalone historical balance, adjustments and reversals must never make the exact inventory balance negative, and a reversal appends an opposite movement rather than changing or deleting the original record. New manual and bulk RETURN outbounds must resolve one supplier from their existing order/receipt source before posting; unassigned standalone opening stock cannot be returned until its supplier provenance is established. This precheck leaves ordinary usage and legacy reversal paths intact and prevents an unassigned return from blocking later supplier closing. Manual movement and bulk outbound requests require a client-generated request_id. A factory/actor/request-scoped unique ID on the existing business audit stores the normalized request fingerprint and original response atomically with the movements, under the factory write lock. Identical retries return the original result without rechecking a now-depleted balance or adding movements; reuse with different content or a different operation kind returns 409. Failed transactions leave neither partial movements nor a committed request receipt. A genuinely new request may reuse a source document number, because documents can contain several lines or batches. Retry metadata is omitted from the public operation-log detail. The frontend shares identical in-flight inventory requests and retains unresolved request IDs with their submission identity in tab session storage, allowing unchanged retries after timeout or refresh when browser storage is available; successful responses release the ID for subsequent intentional operations. Both single and bulk outbound forms close immediately after successful posting and before refreshing balances, returning to the original page with success feedback; refresh failure is reported separately and never reopens a successful submission. Failed submissions retain their entered values. These guards are active on the local development backend and do not require a database migration; other deployments must update frontend and backend request contracts together. Inventory amounts use a separate full-history moving-weighted-average projection by factory, customer, inventory identity, unit and normalized currency. Live inventory positions/balances and movement GET responses expose read-only cost_status, cost_currency, cost_amount and cost_unit_price from this same complete projection under the factory transaction lock. Inventory and dashboard stock totals group filtered positions by unit and retain four decimal places; different units must never be summed into one displayed quantity. Inventory closing summary counts month/customer/currency records as 份 rather than customer 家. Real-time inventory also filters exact paper type and unit while preserving selected physical positions and full-ledger balances. Exception queries include owner department and created-time ordering; master configuration supports code/customer sorting. These query controls do not alter workflow states or totals. The inventory page defaults money columns off and offers 显示金额 for stock/ledger values plus currency-separated totals of filtered priced positions; unknown records are excluded with an explicit count. Single and bulk outbound show read-only current-cost estimates, with actual posted ledger amounts authoritative. Missing-price dependencies follow average-cost consumption and original reversals; known-price positive receipts remain individually priced even in an uncertain pool. Position display amounts allocate rounded pool cents by largest remainder with stable position-key ties, preserving nonnegative stock values and the total. Multiple active currency pools under one physical identity, negative positions, unmatched quantities or valuation errors show 待核算; missing prices show 待核价 rather than zero. These fields do not edit source prices, quantities or locked closing snapshots. Supplier monthly reconciliation is implemented separately from inventory closings in the closing workspace (default bilateral reconciliation page, secondary inventory records). Migration 20260909_0105 adds nullable receipt acceptance_date and immutable/versioned carton_supplier_settlements, guarded before startup create_all; the primary local backend/data database includes this migration, with original rows preserved and no guessed historical dates. Receipts accrue to supplier months by explicit actual acceptance_date, not order/delivery/entry dates; split deliveries accrue separately. Legacy missing dates require audited verification and block confirmation. Supplier sources include effective posted receipt lines except explicitly linked SUPPLIER-responsible free replacements, plus ORDER_RETURN and manual OUTBOUND issue_kind RETURN, excluding internal issues, stocktakes, transfers and opening stock. Returns accrue by movement posting month normalized to Asia/Shanghai; UTC/offset timestamps are converted before deriving dates or checking confirmed supplier periods. Legacy receipt confirmation-time fallbacks use the same normalization. Independent actual-return-date entry is not implemented. Receipt/order supplier consistency and uniquely provable legacy supplier attribution are enforced. Supplier comparison uses receipt prices and independently approved return prices/credit evidence, never average inventory carrying cost; unknown totals are null. Dongkang collaborative supplier monthly reconciliation automatically suggests vendor quantities, independent original CNY delivery prices and amounts through the exact supplier-shipment/receipt link; warehouse receipt prices are never substituted for absent vendor evidence. Original price text is retained; missing, out-of-range or greater-than-six-decimal prices leave suggested values blank for evidenced correction. Legacy unlinked receipts and return credits require explicit entry; existing manual versions and suppliers not supported by the dedicated Dongkang portal retain the manual path. The unified monthly page is named 双方月结对账 and places vendor original delivery, effective warehouse acceptance and proposed settlement alongside each other. Complete linked lines show read-only proposed values until explicitly expanded for adjustment; missing prices, unlinked historical receipts and return evidence use inline credential supplement fields in the same page. Existing manual versions are clearly identified, with draft conversion available and previous confirmations/superseded versions preserved. Three current-version indicators reflect internal save, supplier acknowledgement and internal completion; unsaved or stale evidence must not display an effective supplier acknowledgement. Inventory monthly records remain a separate tab. Both reconciliation views can filter keywords, differences, unlinked sources and version stages for display; totals, saves and confirmations always use the complete immutable version, including hidden lines. Unsaved changes are visibly warned and do not become a filtered partial submission. Each vendor line omitted from current payable sources remains visible with acceptance/discrepancy evidence, including wholly rejected or unreceived papers and cross-month acceptance, without adding payable amounts. Exact per-source quantity/price/amount comparison and explicit same currency/tax basis remain required. Changes from original delivery values require a reconciliation note. Saved collaborative drafts are visible through dedicated supplier read permission, scoped to the fixed active supplier and authorized factory; supplier approval permission acknowledges or disputes the exact version, without exposing internal sample purposes/requesters, locations or inventory carrying costs. Only ended months with no unresolved dates/prices/source mismatches/differences may be confirmed; collaborative versions first require the supplier acknowledgement of current source and statement evidence, followed by internal closing_manage plus order_adjust confirmation. Drafts and reasoned disputes are available during the month. Saving edits, updating evidence or reopening invalidates the new drafts supplier acknowledgement while retaining prior version evidence. Collaborative versions cannot downgrade to manual to bypass acknowledgement. Factory locks, optimistic revision, source and acknowledgement fingerprints protect saves/confirmation; original vendor snapshots also participate in stale-source detection. The flow uses the existing settlement version table JSON and requires no new schema migration. No independent monthly-bill import/OCR, guessed/split matching, extra-fee reconciliation, tolerance bypass or payment records are implemented. Confirmed month source changes require reasoned reopening that preserves prior evidence as SUPERSEDED and creates a new DRAFT; normal next-month receipts/returns remain available. Stale drafts refresh source evidence explicitly while retaining vendor-only rows for resolution. Inventory closings retain their existing posting-time/cost rules and do not represent supplier payable approval. Current operation and limitations are documented in docs/business/纸箱月结与供应商对账方案.md. Reads scan full history and hold the existing factory transaction lock, so large-history latency and contention remain unmeasured. The audited price-confirmation path only fills an original zero-priced positive receipt/opening movement once; there is no dedicated correction for a wrong nonzero or already-confirmed price. Whole-receipt reversal and reentry remain available subject to stock, valuation and lock constraints, so correcting only the price after consumption can require undoing dependent outbound transactions. The user has explicitly deferred improvements to the wrong-price correction workflow until they confirm the actual business process. New positive-effective receipt posting requires a finite unit price greater than zero on every line, including immediate posting and confirmation of old pending receipts; validation precedes all inventory writes and any failure rejects the whole posting. Existing positive order prices may supply the default; blank/zero values cannot post. Drafts can retain missing prices, and zero-effective lines do not require a positive price. Legacy posted missing-price receipts and opening-stock import retain their existing audited pricing/correction behavior. Do not implement a new wrong-price correction path or infer its approval/revaluation rules before that confirmation. Positive receipts/opening adjustments contribute their recorded price (or audited missing-price confirmation); outbound, negative adjustment and order return consume the current average carrying cost, with full depletion consuming the exact remaining amount rather than a rounded display price. New issue movements record the six-decimal average price while month-end valuation carries higher-precision Decimal amounts. Reversals restore the original projected amount; a reversal that leaves negative value or value at zero quantity is rejected for explicit reconciliation. Same-second transaction ordering uses persisted audit sequence; unsequenced legacy mixed inbound/outbound records are reported as valuation issues instead of guessing their order. Weekly-schedule and inspection-schedule import batches remain queryable with their parsed results for historical review. Receipt history retains explicit customer, status and inclusive selected-date filters, defaulting to actual acceptance date with a separate delivery-date option and ascending/descending sort, and uses the shared header search for receipt/delivery-note numbers, contracts, items and paper materials without a duplicate local search field. Clearing history filters also clears the header keyword. Both orders and receipts load all API pages so older receipts remain available for correction. The inventory movement table displays business document numbers for inbound, outbound and adjustment sources; internal movement IDs are hidden from row text but available as a hover title on posting time. The frontend inventory movement list still requests only the newest 500 entries without paging, limiting normal historical movement search and correction access. Balance and inventory-report APIs independently use the full ledger, so this UI limit does not truncate their totals. Receipt history shows the supplier delivery-note number as its visible document identifier; the internal receipt number remains in whole-receipt details and search. Customer, contract number, packaging type, paper quality and specification have separate columns, aligned with the inventory/outbound ledger presentation. Receipt-history actions use shared outlined buttons: the single applicable correction action is on the left and `明细` is on the right; there is no redundant `更多` menu. `明细` previews a read-only whole-receipt overlay on hover/focus, dismisses on leave/blur, and pins on click for scrolling; close, backdrop and Escape dismiss it. Previewing does not replace the active receipt-entry draft. Pinned pending receipts retain an explicit continue-confirmation entry for authorized operators. The permission-gated correction button directly opens reason-required whole-document reversal for posted receipts (`冲销`, light red outline), voiding for pending receipts (`作废`, same red outline), or explicit reentry for reversed receipts (`重新登记`, teal outline). Reasons and explicit confirmation remain mandatory for void/reversal. The dedicated receipt reversal requires receipt_write plus inventory_write, optimistic receipt revisions and the same factory lock used by receipt creation/confirmation and dependent append/reduce/return operations. It preserves original headers, lines and confirmation evidence, sets REVERSED, creates a linked audited opposite movement for every positive inbound line, cumulatively checks exact balance keys and rejects missing/inconsistent source movements, duplicate reversal, locked original/current posting periods, negative stock or valuation errors atomically. Posted received quantities are recalculated; affected open/completed orders recover their current pending/partial state and revision, while cancelled orders stay cancelled. Reversed receipts are excluded from legacy effective-receipt rollups but remain visible in history. The inventory-summary ledger report instead keeps the original inbound and the opposite reversal on their actual posting dates. Pending voids only release pending receipt quantities. Reentry copies editable values into a new draft with an explicit “更正” registration-number suffix and a note naming the original receipt/delivery note, because the original delivery-note uniqueness key remains occupied; the original document is never overwritten. This workflow has isolated SQLite/API concurrency and frontend tests. The local backend now exposes the receipt reversal endpoint after the verified database repair described below; live reversal of business receipts has not been exercised by the basic receive/issue flow test.

The PMC / warehouse module catalog exposes `/modules/pmc-warehouse/carton-procurement` as `纸箱采购协同`, with a dedicated workspace for the dashboard, human-created orders, weekly reconciliation and delivery reminders, receipt feedback, inventory movements, inventory summaries, customer-scoped month-end settlement, exceptions and a unified audit/reminder ledger. A carton order is one contract header with multiple paper-item detail rows for the same item number; packaging type (for example outer carton, sliding paper or card paper), paper quality and specification are separate fields rather than flattened into separate contract orders. Orders declare quantity_basis. CALCULATED orders derive paper demand from product quantity divided by products per carton, rounding upward. EXPLICIT historical orders use positive per-paper required_quantity as the authoritative demand; product_order_quantity and usage_quantity may be null (unknown), never inferred from carton demand. Migration 20260909_0106 adds the basis and nullable fields, including immutable procurement snapshot quantities; old records remain CALCULATED. The SQLite migration must run with foreign_keys disabled on its dedicated connection to avoid cascaded child deletion during table rebuild, followed by foreign_key_check and business-row preservation verification. The order form no longer defaults product quantity to 3600, validates contract/item identifiers on both client and server, and starts new paper type/quality/specification fields blank while offering explicit historical suggestions. Imported historical orders and later formal orders share the authoritative order/header-line tables. The downloadable historical workbook has blank warehouse-shaped main and extra-paper sheets, a separate example sheet and instructions. The main sheet begins with eight everyday inputs: customer, contract, item, order date, planned due date, outer demand, inner demand and card-paper demand; other fields are optional to the right. Main rows accept separate inner/outer/card-paper demand and material fields; 0/12 means no inner packing and twelve products per outer, not a receipt quantity. Explicit demand wins over packing calculations with a visible mismatch warning. Missing demand is calculated only from explicitly provided positive product quantity and packing, never the reverse. Dates require a year; the source planned date is required and preserved. Missing customer deadlines use the shared historical-date rule above; explicit customer deadlines remain unchanged. Company maps to customer, not factory. A numeric card-paper/card/卡类 column represents 卡纸 in 张 by default and does not require an auxiliary row or historical number; blank/zero creates no paper demand and at least one positive demand is required. Duplicate card-quantity columns are rejected rather than silently dropping quantities. Other card types, mixed specifications and additional papers use explicit type/quantity plus a unique historical-order reference on the extra-paper sheet. New wide imports require a read-only preview and a reviewed fingerprint before atomic confirmation; file, factory-scoped customer/master/order or supplier changes require re-preview. Errors block the entire import, exact duplicates are reported/skipped, ambiguous duplicate source rows and mixed wide/legacy worksheets are rejected rather than silently merged. A unique ACTIVE supplier is bound at preview. Only uniquely provable active master matches may fill missing materials, visibly in preview. Unknown customers must first be maintained explicitly. Legacy long templates remain compatible. History import confirmation acknowledges orders already placed outside the system: imported orders enter PENDING_SUPPLIER directly with an audited LEGACY_BASELINE, bypassing normal submission and preventing duplicate initial procurement issuance. Order deletion requires both factory-scoped carton_procurement:order_write and direct carton_procurement:order_adjust, using the existing supervisor entitlement without the warehouse adjustment fallback. Supervisors may remove any CANCELLED order from the ledger, regardless of receipt, inventory, supplier or split history. Migration 20260929_0126 adds nullable deleted_at; cancelled deletion sets it and advances the revision while keeping the cancelled header, paper lines, purchase issues, receipts, stock movements, supplier commitments/shipments/attachments and split evidence. Both order workspaces hide these removed headers; original document and accounting history remains readable and original order numbers remain reserved. Direct order actions and split context reject a removed header. Directly owned open work items are closed and cannot reopen; immutable deletion audits keep full before snapshots and history_retained=true. No business history or stock/payable is erased by this cancelled-order action. Other statuses retain the existing rule: only unused ordinary or historical-import orders can be physically deleted, and receipt-line evidence (including pending, voided or reversed), inventory movements, supplier execution/attachments, split history and received/completed states block deletion. Net-zero stock does not make these non-cancelled used orders deletable. Members of an issued merged purchase batch also cannot be physically deleted; normal full reduction or return followed by cancelled-header removal retains every batch snapshot and its original document identity. Up to 100 selected orders share one operation ID; all revisions and eligibility checks run under the factory transaction lock before any writes, so mixed invalid batches never partially delete. Legacy history-only endpoints keep their historical-import restriction and the same supervisor gate. Physical deletion keeps complete immutable snapshots of the order/lines, purchase issues and directly owned exceptions, while preserving master data and unrelated schedule exceptions. Per-order deletion stays in More, including cancelled orders; batch deletion requires the same scoped permissions. The frontend requires an explicit in-page confirmation and a trimmed reason of at least four characters, clears successful selections and supersedes older in-flight refreshes. Ordinary deletion uses ORDER_DELETED and legacy history deletion uses HISTORY_ORDER_DELETED. Later quantity/date changes remain delta issues. Missing paper quality/specification may be filled only while receiving that historical paper line; existing values are never overwritten. Receipt material completion is audited and atomic with receipt creation/posting, requires complete identity and a positive inbound price, and does not require unreceived sibling papers to be completed. Ordinary new orders still require normal confirmation and locking. Unknown product/packing quantities remain visible in editing and procurement exports; incomplete or unknown-packing rows cannot become reusable standard packaging. Importing demand never creates receipts, inventory, supplier payable or old completion progress. Historical Excel imports without an explicit historical order number group by customer name, contract and item; persisted and within-batch duplicate checks use resolved customer code plus contract/item inside the shared factory transaction lock. Historical order numbers remain unique within a factory. Different customers can therefore import identical printed contract/item values without merging or skipping each other; concurrent retries cannot create duplicate demand. Historical import restores order demand, not prior receipt/usage/completion progress. The new master-data selector supersedes the legacy history dropdown when available and only lists the selected customer’s configurations, with no copied prices. The legacy fallback API remains a factory-scoped history-item library: typing at least two item-number characters returns exact, prefix, contains and typo-tolerant suggestions ranked by match quality and recency, limited to active customers. Selecting a suggestion fills the authoritative current customer plus the latest historical product name, paper lines, units-per-carton and price metadata, while deliberately preserving the new contract number, product quantity, order date and due date. The order ledger presents the pre-supplier `CONFIRMED` state as `待下单`, supports explicit status, due-range and inclusive order-date range filters, checkbox selection, mixed-status bulk supplier submission, batch issuance of pending immutable supplier documents, cumulative-reconciliation export and atomic bulk cancellation. Bulk supplier submission changes only `待下单` orders to `PENDING_SUPPLIER`, atomically issues each first immutable `P00` supplier snapshot in the same transaction, skips other selected states, checks revisions before one commit and writes submission and issue audit evidence per changed order. New confirmations with at least two eligible orders for the same factory and supplier also persist one purchase-batch ID, combined document number and ordered member-issue manifest inside those initial immutable snapshots. The warehouse downloads one combined workbook per supplier group, retaining each contract, item, PO, paper identity, quantity and unit separately; its purchase history offers the same original combined download. Single-order confirmations remain ordinary P00 documents. Audit-refresh or workbook-download failures remain separate from a successful confirmation, and retries never issue the same orders again. Batch identity never comes from timestamps; this contract adds no schema migration or historical backfill. Submission locks direct edits to customer, contract, item and paper-line data. Appending always requires factory-scoped `carton_procurement:order_write`. While an order is `PENDING_SUPPLIER`, `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` or `COMPLETED`, append additionally requires either `carton_procurement:order_adjust` or both `order_write` and `inventory_write`, allowing warehouse operators without granting supervisor-only stocktake/closing rights. Each permission is evaluated in the current factory and carton/pmc-warehouse department scopes, preserving explicit denies and factory isolation; the frontend mirrors these checks. Existing receipt and inventory history remains immutable. Appending recalculates every paper requirement and compares it with posted receipts: a completed order stays completed when all new requirements are already satisfied (95 to 96 products at ten products/carton still requires ten cartons), and reopens as `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` only when an actual paper shortage appears. The same `order_adjust` OR (`order_write` AND `inventory_write`) rule allows reducing product quantity while an order is `PENDING_SUPPLIER` or `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, but the new product quantity must protect both posted receipts and effective quantities in pending-confirmation receipts. For an order reopened by appending after completion, the exact completed product quantity before that append is the locked baseline; carton rounding slack from the completed balance is never exposed as reducible, and later received cartons extend the protected quantity from that baseline. Orders without a completed-append baseline conservatively protect the product capacity represented by each received or pending carton. Reducing part of the unreceived balance keeps the current open status; reducing all remaining unreceived demand on a partially received order changes it to `COMPLETED`, while reducing an entirely unreceived supplier-submitted order to zero changes it to `CANCELLED`. Completed orders cannot be reduced. For CALCULATED orders, each adjustment recalculates paper requirements. For EXPLICIT orders, append/reduce sends complete per-line target demands without a product delta; each target protects posted plus pending receipt quantities, and a fully reduced paper remains as a zero-demand historical row. Existing receipt/inventory evidence never changes. Creation and adjustment audits freeze paper-specific quantities and units, and timeline text uses these snapshots rather than inventing product changes. Explicit planned dates remain independent during edit/append. Append and reduction dialogs default their auditable reasons to `客人追加订单` and `客人退单`; users may replace those defaults when another reason applies, and the backend applies the same defaults when API callers omit or clear the reason. Supplier purchase documents are immutable issued snapshots rather than views of the mutable cumulative order. The first issued document is `P00`, append documents use `A01...`, reductions use `R01...`, and schedule-only adjustments use `C01...`; every line's executable quantity is its current authoritative paper demand minus the latest issued snapshot's paper demand. Re-downloading an issued document always uses its original snapshot. Selected-order batch issuance creates documents for unissued net changes in one transaction and combines their independent document numbers into one workbook; already confirmed initial orders reuse the automatically issued `P00` snapshot when there is no change, and only orders lacking both change and history are skipped. Orders already supplier-submitted/open/completed before automatic first issuance receive an idempotent `LEGACY_BASELINE` backfill in migration `20260923_0121`; it preserves current cumulative demand for supplier visibility and later deltas without falsely creating a new first purchase document. Cancelled orders and orders with any issue are skipped. Receipts accept only supplier-submitted or partially received orders. The ledger also derives a live due-date countdown, summarizes overdue/today/next-three-day orders, and sorts urgent open orders ahead of future and closed records. The weekly workspace deliberately separates two business checks: customer schedules are imported to detect missing or quantity-mismatched formal orders, while next-week inspection contracts are imported to derive the carton required-delivery date from the first inspection date minus a configurable advance-day value and create paper-department reminders for open orders. Weekly checks offer product-quantity increase/decrease shortcuts based only on an unambiguous linked order product quantity, without converting paper demand; clearing filters preserves the chosen sort. They also offer an attention-only filter and searchable expandable loaded import history (the API currently loads the newest 50 batches). Users with the factory-scoped import permission may undo either a weekly-schedule or inspection-reminder import only as one whole batch, never by individual result. Undo uses the existing REJECTED batch state, preserves the complete original batch and immutable audit snapshot, closes and hides every related exception, blocks reopening or a second undo, and does not change formal orders or inventory. The identical withdrawn weekly or inspection file may be reimported as a fresh batch. The shared factory transaction lock preserves the original REJECTED batch, immutable undo audits and closed exceptions, while the new import records its predecessor in the import profile and parse summary without a schema change. Repeated same-file requests reuse the latest active batch and do not duplicate exceptions; undoing that new batch allows another fresh import. Factory, selected master customer, parser version and inspection advance-day profile remain independent import identities. The frontend marks withdrawn history, removes its active checks/reminders and supersedes older in-flight refreshes so stale results cannot reappear. The dashboard's business-order collaboration panel uses the persisted customer-schedule batches and their exception work items as its source, shows the actual uploader, file and time, retains exception status for review while replacing the business panel's Start Processing action with Mark Already Ordered or a prefilled ordinary-order entry, suppresses stale missing-order alerts when the source is manually marked, currently linked to an order, cancelled or shipped, scopes alert counts to the same customer/search/type filters, and exposes authorized append, reduction of unreceived demand and cancellation; received quantities remain immutable and later corrections to those quantities must use the inventory correction workflow. The weekly order-gap workflow accepts each customer's own file format through header aliases and also provides an optional system XLSX template whose blank `导入数据` sheet uses the exact recognized Reference, customer-PO, PO, customer, item, product, quantity, packing and inspection-window headers; its separate explanation/example sheet is deliberately not parsed as schedule detail. The inspection-reminder workflow continues to accept customer files through aliases without a shared template. Neither workflow itself creates formal orders or inventory. The inventory workspace is the real-time balance and immutable movement surface, while month-end closing is a customer-, period- and currency-scoped reconciliation snapshot rather than a duplicate movement ledger.

Migration `20260805_0050` adds the persistent carton supplier, order/header-line, import-batch, receipt/header-line, inventory-movement, month-end-closing and audit tables plus the initial six `carton_procurement:*` permissions. The authenticated `/api/carton-procurement` API enforces factory scope and pagination, uses the fixed supplier record for each factory, keeps formal order creation human-triggered, and creates exactly one inbound inventory movement per receipt line only after a human confirms the receipt. Effective receipt equals received minus damaged, rejected and other unusable quantities; partial receipts leave an order incomplete. Inventory history is append-only through normal API flows, with corrections recorded as adjustment or reversal movements, and locked closing periods reject further inventory postings. Month-end closing stores a customer-period-currency snapshot and separates ongoing review (DRAFT → PENDING → CONFIRMED, displayed as 核对确认) from final locking. Review confirmation does not block normal inventory postings. Final LOCKED is allowed only after the period has ended according to Asia/Shanghai business time and requires both factory-scoped closing_manage and the supervisor-only order_adjust permission; the UI requires a separate final confirmation. The same permissions allow audited 解锁重核: a reason of at least four trimmed characters and the current revision are mandatory, the complete original snapshot and confirmation/lock evidence are preserved in CLOSING_UNLOCKED, and the record returns to DRAFT without changing movements or quantities. A later locked month for the same factory/customer/currency must be unlocked first. Generation, status changes and unlocking share the factory write lock and reread current state before revision checks. Missing-price or valuation issues remain visible on unlocked closing responses and block confirmation and locking. Zero-valued positive receipts and opening adjustments mean unverified price unless an explicit audited free-material confirmation exists; consumed missing-price stock cannot disappear from review merely because its current quantity is zero or its source was later reversed. A completely untouched reversed opening is excluded. Users with factory-scoped closing_manage may confirm a missing source price with a mandatory reason, including an explicit acknowledgment for a legitimate zero price. Confirmation appends INVENTORY_PRICE_CONFIRMED evidence without overwriting the original movement, rejects duplicate confirmations and any change affecting a locked period, and fully rebuilds affected unlocked closing quantities/amounts as drafts for renewed review. Every unlocked snapshot, including CONFIRMED, may be regenerated as a draft for renewed review; generation preserves LOCKED rows while refreshing other customers/currencies and audits the replaced snapshots. Confirmation and final locking recheck opening, inbound, outbound, adjustment and ending quantities plus ending amount against current movements and valuation; unlocked responses expose snapshot_stale to prevent acting on changed figures. Snapshots remain immutable while locked; disagreement between an earlier locked amount and current weighted valuation blocks subsequent closing confirmation. Posting guards reject changes in or before any later locked month for the same factory/customer, including historical opening imports. Locked responses also disclose existing quantity/valuation disagreement without changing frozen evidence. Generation and missing-price rebuild events freeze quantities by counting unit in `closing_unit_snapshots`; the closing API/UI use `quantities_by_unit`, while legacy scalar columns remain for compatibility and are not displayed as a physical total. Confirmation/locking compare both scalar amount/quantity and per-unit evidence, so equal-total cross-unit changes cannot bypass review. Old snapshots without unit evidence are explicitly marked missing; unlocked ones need regeneration, and locked ones retain their original amounts until authorized unlock/review. Closing list and single responses share the factory transaction lock while reading refreshed headers, audit evidence and ledger rows, and reuse per-period valuations within that response. Ledger ordering, moving-average valuation, closing boundaries and legacy daily summaries normalize timestamps to Asia/Shanghai rather than comparing raw strings; persisted audit sequence resolves same-instant ordering. Source timestamps and historical movements remain unchanged. These rules are active on the local backend after the verified revision-0102 database repair. Live receive/issue verification confirms new outbound movements record moving-average cost and regenerated unlocked closing drafts deduct the carrying amount correctly. Legacy zero-priced issue records remain immutable; valuation reconstructs their carrying cost from the full ledger rather than rewriting source prices. Existing locks are not automatically removed, and local activation does not establish production deployment. Migration `20260810_0064` adds the persisted closing currency, backfills single-currency legacy closings from their source inventory movements without changing locked quantities, amounts or status, rejects ambiguous mixed-currency legacy snapshots, and makes future closing generation aggregate each customer and currency separately. Migration `20260904_0097` adds immutable supplier purchase-order issue snapshots and legacy cumulative baselines; downgrade is allowed only while no formal supplier document has been issued, because deleting issued evidence is not reversible.

Migration `20260805_0051` adds factory-scoped persisted import exceptions and the seventh permission, `carton_procurement:exception_manage`. Weekly schedule and delivery-note imports now parse supported `.xlsx`, `.xlsm` and `.xls` header variants into structured previews. Delivery-note images use the local OCR pipeline only; parser failures remain explicit batch warnings and never trigger an external provider call. Dongkang delivery-note photos use the same four-column business contract regardless of engine: the first column is split into contract number plus item number, the second into paper-item type plus paper quality, the third is the specification, and the fourth is delivered carton quantity; the page-level DN number remains receipt metadata and never becomes an order row or matching identifier. The local profile accepts `.heic`/`.heif` phone originals through `pillow-heif`, corrects sideways photos, keeps rows with an unreadable order cell as explicit review rows, and narrows multi-line contracts by paper quality and normalized dimensions before paper type. Import batches retain file identity metadata, SHA-256 fingerprint and structured parse summary rather than treating the uploaded source file as authoritative business data. Rows are matched to human-created carton orders using normalized contract and item identifiers and one shared material-identity validator across both delivery import paths. Every explicitly provided paper type, quality, normalized specification and dimension unit is checked even for a single candidate; conflicting provided values never fall back to a match. Internal previews retain source_material separately from resolved order fields and list material_candidates with the conflicting fields for human review. Legacy delivery previews without the current material-validation parser version cannot seed new receipts and must be reimported from the original file; stored historical evidence and posted receipts remain intact. In Dongkang supplier export files, 客户单号 maps to our contract_no and 客户料号 maps to our item_no; neither field is customer_po. The Dongkang spreadsheet adapter recognizes these exact headers in `.xls` and `.xlsx`, preserves each source delivery-note number, date, material, specification, quantity and source price, and checks its `客户` destination against the selected factory. A single recognized destination different from the current factory redirects the authorized warehouse user to import into that factory before any batch is saved; mixed or unknown destinations are rejected without creating a batch. A blank customer item requires explicit review; contract-only or item-only fallback is disabled for this format. A matched row also carries the internal order unit price; warehouse receipt review defaults to a positive supplier-file unit price only for CNY purchase lines, falls back to the order price when the file price is missing, nonpositive or the order currency differs, and shows price/currency differences. The warehouse still confirms or edits the final receipt price before posting. The receipt UI switches between all delivery-note numbers in a batch, including notes with no matched orders, and starts every matched paper unselected with zero actual received; the warehouse may select lines or mark one note fully arrived, then still confirms actual quantity, differences, price and bin before posting. Missing orders, quantity differences and ambiguous matches create persisted exception work items. Duplicate fingerprints restore the original batch and do not duplicate exceptions, but the delivery parser version is part of that identity so a parser upgrade reprocesses the same source instead of restoring a stale zero-row batch. The receipt page always keeps the recognized structured rows visible even when no order matches and exposes OCR raw text, engine, parser version and every warning in an expanded diagnostic panel when parsing or matching fails. Weekly imports never create formal orders, and importing a delivery file alone never creates a receipt or inventory. A user may explicitly convert a `MISSING_ORDER` non-supplier delivery row into an ad hoc receipt review line; Dongkang ERP delivery rows require a formal matching order and are not posted as ad hoc inventory, while ambiguous rows must first be resolved. A delivery-note import may be deleted only while it still requires review, has zero matched formal-order rows and is not referenced by any receipt; deletion removes its derived exception work items, retains an audit event, and makes the same source fingerprint eligible for a clean re-import.

Unified ITEM weekly imports recognize the pending-order, cancellation and shipped sections on each ITEM sheet; the receipt/inspection adapters remain separate. Each new weekly import requires an explicitly selected active carton customer from the current factory master. The backend validates and canonicalizes customer_code before parsing, stores schedule_customer metadata on the batch and schedule_customer_code/name on every row, and scopes formal-order matching to that customer. Raw workbook buyer names remain source evidence. A business row is identified within its factory by selected master customer code, contract, item and ITEM SO#/Reference, preserving leading zeros; source P/O#: remains separate evidence. Repeated contract/item/SO rows receive a soft manual-review warning and independent source-row operation keys rather than automatic merging. Manual already-ordered decisions are factory-scoped audit events and remain effective across import batches. Section-aware batches compare each customer/order with its last seen row from earlier non-rejected section-aware imports: a new pending identity is flagged as a new order, and an explicit move into the cancellation section becomes a review item, with a high-priority work-board alert when previously marked or linked to a formal order. Missing rows never imply cancellation, repeated SO identities require a visible manual-review warning without blocking valid row actions, and no import or mark changes a formal purchase order. Customer tabs use the current factory carton master, including labelled inactive customers for viewing, and open that customer's latest effective batch; a customer with no import has an empty workspace. The customer chooser permits only active same-factory customers and requires a choice before file selection. Historical batches without explicit master-customer metadata appear as 未绑定客户 and retain their original raw-buyer identity, marks and snapshots; they are not guessed, reassigned or used as the baseline for newly bound customer identities. Select the correct master customer and reimport to establish a bound baseline. Batch display filters include source section, source order type (always-visible 正单/备料单/样板单/正式PO chips plus any additional current-batch types, including unrecorded types; 正单 includes 加单 rows, whose original type remains visible in an emphasized badge), current order/manual-mark status, change type, text search and inclusive customer-due or inspection-date ranges, plus original/date sorting. Source order type resets to all types when changing customer, batch or factory or clearing filters; filtering does not change procurement eligibility. Unknown, yearless and staged dates do not enter date-range results; invalid ranges show feedback. Selection persists across filters but clears on batch/factory changes, and stale upload responses are discarded after a factory change. The legacy schedule-template download entry is removed from the UI while parser compatibility remains. Older section-unaware imports do not serve as a comparison baseline.

The unified business-input template uses the BUZZ BEE production-schedule ITEM layout across all ITEM worksheets, excluding the duplicate order-summary sheet. Headers are matched by name rather than column position. Only explicit 正单 rows participate in automatic procurement matching; 备料单, 样板单, 正式PO, blank types and invalid/nonpositive quantities remain visible for human review. Repeated contract/item/SO rows stay separate with a soft 待人工确认 warning. Review-only business type, field, date and duplicate-SO warnings remain in weekly/inspection reconciliation instead of creating exception-center work items; older persisted review-only items are hidden from its list and dashboard count without erasing import or audit evidence. Delivery-note row reviews remain separate receipt work items with receipt-specific labels. Contract No., SO#/Reference and P/O#: are retained as separate source fields; selected master customer plus contract and item are narrowed by SO#/Reference as the business customer PO when it is present, falling back to P/O#: only when SO is blank. There is no item-only fallback or neighboring-row fill. Source sheet/row, business customer, production identifier, packing, inspection date and requested shipment date are preserved. Date cells retain their original text separately from a normalized complete date; yearless, staged or invalid text remains visible for manual review and never generates a guessed delivery reminder. Quantity matching may still link the order independently of date review. The UI separates needs-order/ordered/completed procurement status from match results, quantity differences and customer-date differences. Completion refers to procurement receipt completion, not settlement. Source matching and quantity/date comparisons remain import-time snapshots, while current linked order state and explicit manual order marks are overlaid in the UI. Pending 正单, 正式PO and 加单 with a complete selected-master-customer/contract/item identity (raw business customer for unbound legacy batches) may be manually marked already ordered, individually or in a selection of up to 100 source rows; repeated SO identities use independent batch/sheet/row operation keys. The factory-scoped import permission gates marking; all selected rows are validated before one atomic audited commit. Unambiguous marks persist by normalized customer/contract/item/SO identity across later imports, without creating formal orders or inventory. A repeated SO row remains actionable with a soft warning, but its row-specific mark does not automatically propagate to another row or batch. Each customer is compared with its latest effective evidence per identity: new pending identities retain new-order evidence, and an explicit move into the cancellation section creates a cancellation reminder, prominently identifying already ordered records. Pending 正单, 加单 and 正式PO use current ordering state plus a latest-order deadline for timing reminders, independently of the import matching snapshot. The needed carton arrival date is the earlier complete inspection/customer-shipment date minus the effective safety lead; latest order date subtracts the effective supplier production-and-delivery interval (default seven days). Deadlines are never clamped to today. More than three days away displays 待下单, one to three days 即将到期, the deadline 今天必须下单, and only an overdue unplaced row 疑似漏单 with overdue days. Current formally confirmed live orders or explicit manual ordered marks stop ordering-time reminders; DRAFT/CONFIRMED drafts remain unplaced until the normal confirm/lock step. Dates requiring review, invalid/incomplete dates and ambiguous identities never generate a guessed deadline. The schedule and work board share this current projection, refresh on the Asia/Shanghai day boundary and window focus, and use latest non-rejected evidence for board reminders; future waiting records remain available in the collaboration list but do not count as urgent pending work. Date-rule loading failures require review rather than assuming an override. Import-time missing-order/new-order evidence is medium priority and does not itself establish overdue work; closing its exception does not prove an order was placed.
