Timing reminders may exist without a persisted exception, including 加单, and marking/creation still use the exact source batch with existing permissions. Quantity/date differences and explicit cancellation reminders remain separate. Already marked missing orders do not produce another missing-order reminder. A pending unmarked source row can prefill the ordinary new-order form with its selected active same-factory master customer (or a uniquely resolved customer for legacy unbound rows), contract, SO#/Reference as customer PO when present (otherwise source P/O#:), item, product, positive product quantity, normalized complete customer due date and explicit numeric outer or inner/outer packing. Ambiguous customers stay unselected; raw staged/yearless dates, unknown packing, paper quality and specification require human review. File, sheet, row, business SO and original packing/date evidence are retained in the order note. A source 加单 shows an explicit prompt in the new-order form and prefills the order note with its source order type, so the saved order details retain that information. Existing noncancelled orders are checked by customer/contract/item and SO-based customer PO before opening a new form; same-contract/item orders with another PO remain visible as soft candidates for manual comparison. Source-based creation also atomically records an immutable factory-scoped source-identity/order association, so an explicitly selected carton customer remains linked after refresh and subsequent imports even when the workbook buyer or customer display name differs. New bound-source orders must use the same selected master customer; legacy unbound source associations retain their original semantics. A linked live order blocks replay of that exact source row under the factory transaction lock; a separate repeated SO row may create its own order after a soft warning, while stale duplicate-row sources, manually marked rows, withdrawn batches, cancelled and shipped latest sources remain blocked. This association does not itself mark a draft already ordered. Later imports reuse the explicit association for matching and already-ordered cancellation review without changing the order or inventory. A saved CONFIRMED order is shown as created but awaiting confirmation and still requires normal confirm/lock before supplier issuance. Cancellation and shipped rows cannot be used to create new orders. Same-file retries restore the latest active batch; a whole-batch withdrawal permits a new batch from the identical file while retaining the withdrawn history. Absence from a later file or a zero quantity never automatically cancels a purchase. Schedule parser version and selected customer code participate in import identity; old ITEM batches are projected on read to the new SO identity without rewriting stored evidence, ambiguous old shared marks are not reassigned, and old P/O#-based order matches require renewed review; the customer display name is excluded so a rename does not duplicate the same-file batch, and importing identical bytes for another selected customer stays isolated. The requested paper 子单 still means the stable paper-type detail of the original order. Customer quantity splitting uses the separate audited allocation workflow below and never duplicates supplier procurement headers.

Supplier collaboration uses `/carton-supplier` for accounts with the three dedicated IAM permissions. Authorized internal staff review supplier delivery notes inside carton procurement → 收料入库 → 供应商发货待收; `/carton-supplier-management` redirects there and preserves factory/shipment deep links; migration `20260921_0118` added five factory-scoped tables. `carton_supplier:read` opens the entire supplier portal, including issued-order details, supplier documents and the contract-based carton-mark source library; `carton_supplier:edit` previews and confirms supplier Excel delivery-note imports, batch-uploads carton-mark original Excel/PDF/photos against current own issued orders, and runs Excel/PDF checks using authorized library sources or a new print PDF; `carton_supplier:approve` confirms purchase-order acceptance and promised delivery dates. Edit and approve operations also require read. The three permissions are granted in the IAM catalog at all-factory/all-department scope, while the server independently limits supplier data to active Dongkang supplier records and service factories with issued purchase orders; internal factory roles do not enlarge that supplier data scope. The same account may retain a local QC or other internal position, and supplier permissions grant no internal carton procurement or warehouse receipt rights. These three permissions use canonical IAM decisions and a narrowly scoped IAM preview/commit path even while other modules remain in legacy authorization mode; only a group superadministrator may change them, and changing an internal position preserves their overrides. The former `SupplierMember` binding UI and API are retired and historical rows no longer authorize access; the table remains for preserved history. The current portal is tied to supplier code `HEYUAN-DONGKANG`, so supporting another supplier would require an explicit supplier-identity dimension before assigning these permissions more broadly. The PMC/warehouse portal routes accounts with canonical global supplier read permission to their collaboration and supplier carton-mark workspace without carrying the internal active-factory context, including accounts that also hold internal supervisor/manager roles; the collaboration page combines those service factories, while the carton-mark source library retains a service-factory selector. Internal carton procurement remains available only to authorized staff. The legacy approved-template supplier API remains restricted server-side to the selected service factory and authorized supplier, non-cancelled supplier-visible procurement snapshots (including historical baselines), and the latest non-archived template that is approved or explicitly released. Association requires exact whitespace/case-normalized customer, PO (or contract fallback), item and contract identity from the issued snapshot; mismatches remain hidden. Incomplete template pairs are hidden and document access is rechecked on every request. The supplier page aggregates orders and shipment feedback from all authorized service factories in one view, including when an old factory query parameter remains in the URL. Every order and shipment identifies its destination factory. The supplier order ledger shows the business order_date from the issued procurement snapshot (falling back to the formal order for older snapshots) and omits the internal order number from visible ledger rows. Order details use a separate read-only dialog with hover preview and click-to-pin; they show the issued carton order header, customer PO exactly as recorded, paper type/quality/specification, quantities and delivery progress. A missing customer PO is explicitly attributed to the upstream carton order and is never inferred from its contract number. Individual or multi-selected order acceptance confirms every positive-demand paper against its current issue and revision; a batch is atomic per destination factory, while cross-factory selection submits factories separately and reports partial success. Internal procurement order lists and read-only details expose supplier_acceptance from the latest ordinary purchase issue and same-factory commitments, separately from internal order locking, receiving and usage states. The order-ledger status cell displays the internal order state and supplier acknowledgement without a usage-status subline. They show not issued, awaiting supplier acceptance, partial acceptance, fully accepted, unissued changes, cancelled and no-demand states, plus accepted/positive-demand paper counts and the latest valid acceptance time. Replenishment issues do not replace the ordinary acceptance version; old-version confirmations and zero-demand lines cannot make the current order accepted. The existing order-ledger refresh reloads this projection; factory-scoped procurement read permission suffices and no supplier permission is granted to internal readers. The supplier portal no longer offers manual paper-by-paper shipment entry. Supplier delivery import and internal warehouse shipment-review drafts survive same-account, unchanged-authorization session refreshes (including returning focus to the window): file/preview selections, receipt quantities, prices, bins, dates and pending request outcomes remain intact. Actual account/logout, authorization version or mode, identity context or employment epoch, relevant capability changes, and internal factory/responsibility-scope switches invalidate their drafts and obsolete responses. This is in-memory continuity, not persistence across page reloads or navigation; the local fix awaits frontend release. Supplier delivery preview/confirmation must retain the Excel and reviewed hash/selections as multipart/form-data under the shared JSON HTTP defaults. The local transport fix and strict numeric 总数量/总金额 footer exclusion are verified but require a separately coordinated frontend/backend release; rows with business identity or malformed footer text remain explicit review rows. It accepts the original Dongkang `.xls`/`.xlsx` delivery detail file, previews every recognized row grouped by source delivery-note number and destination factory, and confirms selected notes with uniquely matched issued/fully accepted paper rows within remaining unshipped quantity and/or complete unmatched paper rows explicitly staged for warehouse review. Unmatched rows with item, paper type, quality, specification and valid quantity remain shipment evidence only; they never become formal orders or inventory at supplier confirmation. Matching requires authorized service factory plus exact contract and item; paper quality, normalized dimensions and explicit dimension units narrow the paper identity, blank item/material keys, an existing contract/item with conflicting or ambiguous paper identity, conflicting dates, duplicate formal paper rows, already-used note numbers, and unsupported/oversized files block the affected note rather than creating a guess. Confirmation rechecks the same file hash and current database state, creates factory-scoped SENT SupplierShipment records atomically across selected notes, and uses the vendor note number/date and source filename/hash as shipment evidence. The same file can be retried idempotently. The factory dropdown filters the combined list locally; acceptance and warehouse routing use each order's destination factory. Customer, search, business-stage and inclusive business order-date range filters remain available. Order selection persists across filters, while 查看已选 shows all selected orders regardless of the current filter so hidden selections can be inspected and removed. Completed and cancelled orders remain visible for details but cannot be selected individually or by select-all; refresh removes any previously selected order that has become terminal. Unsaved promised dates survive same-issue refreshes and partial batch submission; a new issue resets them. The ledger sorts pending acceptance, pending next shipment after a partial receipt, accepted/ready to ship, shipment awaiting warehouse confirmation, pending issuance, completed and cancelled, and shows per-paper received/required and in-transit progress without combining unlike units. Beside planned delivery, it shows an Asia/Shanghai business-date reminder using the internal carton order wording for overdue, today, tomorrow, within three days and later; invalid or missing dates say delivery confirmation is needed, while completed and cancelled orders show closed labels instead of day counts. Stages are pending acceptance, pending next shipment after a partial receipt, accepted, shipment awaiting warehouse confirmation when paper is in transit, completed, cancelled, and separately pending issuance for unreleased changes. Zero-demand historical paper rows remain visible but do not require acceptance; supplier file confirmation still requires every positive-demand paper on the matched order to be accepted. Paper-type children retain the original order and stable order-line IDs for acceptance, promised dates, partial shipments, receipts and reconciliation. The supplier document desk lists issued purchase documents and supplier delivery notes across authorized factories, supports factory/type/status/date/search filters, and shows contained orders and paper lines. The purchase document desk separately shows current supplier acceptance and positive-paper counts; combined documents are fully accepted only when every member is accepted. Old ordinary issues show a historical-version warning and B replacements require separate confirmation, without borrowing the parent acceptance. Dongkang ERP template export of any document not fully accepted requires explicit acknowledgement of the unaccepted-export warning, rechecked under the factory lock and retained in export audit; cancelling the reminder produces no export. Ordinary purchase document download remains available for review. Export never creates a supplier commitment. Current valid supplier promises differing from the internal planned date are shown per paper in the internal ledger/details and supplier ledger; individual and bulk acceptance forms preview earlier/later days as dates are entered. Stale/zero-demand commitments are excluded, and the planned date is never rewritten. UI wording uses 生成采购单; API routes, issue IDs and audit event codes remain stable. These changes are local and require a coordinated frontend/backend release before online activation. Selected issued purchase snapshots can also be exported as a single-factory Dongkang ERP import workbook with the exact 21-column supplier template: 客户单号 is the internal contract number, 客户料号 is the internal item number, joined paper specifications split into length/width/height, and quantities are positive issue deltas rather than cumulative after-issue totals. Recorded dimension units are retained; a unit written in the specification is used when the separate field is blank, and otherwise legacy blank units use the user-confirmed cm convention. Conflicting units block export. Malformed dimensions, missing keys/due dates, negative or due-only changes and replenishments with unknown responsibility block this importer export with a clear error; the original issued purchase workbook remains available. B replacement purchase issues export only their positive issue deltas. Supplier-responsibility free replacements export a zero unit price; own-responsibility replacements retain the issued unit price, with both marked by B document number and responsibility in the delivery note. The B issue retains the original due date, so the note asks the supplier to confirm its actual delivery date. Successful template exports count in the same per-document audit counter. Document details preview on hover and pin on click. Up to 100 selected documents can be previewed as distinct factory/order groups, with each source document and its paper lines retained separately rather than summed, and exported to one Excel workbook after server-side ownership revalidation. Each successful workbook generation records one audit event per selected document, so the supplier desk displays a persistent per-document export count scoped by factory, type and ID; historical exports before this recording began are not backfilled. Warehouse confirmation batches with a persisted manifest appear as one combined purchase document per factory and supplier, with direct whole-document export and original per-order purchase numbers in details and general exports. Dongkang ERP export expands the authoritative member snapshots, retaining original issue numbers and recording the combined number in remarks. A successful combined export counts once for that selected batch. Earlier issues without batch metadata remain independent; matching dates or issue times never reconstruct historical groups. The supplier activity log projects only supplier-relevant issue, commitment, shipment and warehouse feedback audit events for authorized orders, with no raw internal audit detail. Supplier /activity-page and internal audit queries apply factory/ownership/entity/event, literal keyword and inclusive date filters before full totals and 50-row UI pagination. Supplier keyword matching uses only scrubbed reference/action/actor data, never internal JSON details. Event types and actors for internal filters come from the authorized full audit history, with translated labels for carton-mark, feedback and update events. Supplier-side Dongkang ERP delivery imports now create SupplierShipment records and appear in the supplier delivery document desk and each target factory's internal pending shipment queue. The internal receipt-import API and existing import evidence remain for compatibility, but the carton receiving UI no longer exposes its upload or empty-review panels. Supplier-confirmed deliveries use 供应商发货待收; pending formal orders still support manual warehouse receipt. The same physical delivery must not be registered through both paths. Supplier import confirmation records the source filename, SHA-256 and row references in shipment snapshots/audit, plus each formal paper line's source delivery unit price separately from the immutable purchase-order price; it does not retain the original binary file. The internal warehouse pending-shipment review prepopulates positive delivery-file prices for CNY purchase lines and falls back to purchase-order prices for missing, legacy or different-currency source prices, shows discrepancies, and requires warehouse confirmation of the final receipt price. Dongkang source prices have no explicit currency column and are treated as CNY only for this prefill. The Dongkang delivery spreadsheet maps 客户单号 to contract, 客户料号 to item and 客户 to destination factory; matching requires the correct factory and exact contract/item identity, including Chinese item names, while blank item numbers require review. The portal exposes automatically issued first procurement snapshots, later issued change snapshots and historical baselines; new confirmations appear immediately, unissued later changes still require internal issuance, and old commitments cannot authorize a changed snapshot. SENT shipments reserve outstanding demand but create no inventory; warehouse confirmation atomically creates and confirms the linked receipt once, while all-zero actual receipt records NOT_RECEIVED without inventory. Whole-receipt reversal atomically reopens its supplier note, increments its revision and returns its notification to pending work. Both portals and supplier documents project RECEIPT_REVERSED with no current acceptance quantity and retain scrubbed acceptance_history; external orders omit internal customer_code. Previously reversed notes are also projected as pending corrections without rewriting their old evidence. The invalidated effective acceptance remains reserved against new dispatches; old rejected or short quantities are not reserved twice. Warehouse correction requires an explicit reason, fresh shipment revision and a new durable request, validates other shipments/receipt drafts and all normal stock and accounting guards, and preserves the original vendor note. A replacement internal receipt uses a distinct #C evidence number under the existing note-uniqueness constraint, with supersedes_receipt_id in supplier acceptance audit linking the prior receipt; request replay across earlier acceptance versions cannot repost inventory. Supplier-origin source_material is preserved in immutable shipment snapshots separately from resolved papers and source delivery prices. A physically received shipment that is entirely rejected can be RECEIVED with zero effective inventory. Each confirmed supplier shipment has one factory-scoped system notification in the existing notification center pending tab for staff who can read carton procurement and confirm receipts and inventory. The standalone internal carton procurement header renders the shared notification bell because AppShell does not render its top bar on full-page routes; the embedded supplier receiving panel does not duplicate it. The notice links to that exact delivery note, remains pending after being read, and is handled automatically when the warehouse confirms receipt or non-arrival. Older SENT shipments without stored notices are projected into the pending feed and materialized if marked read; the notification is only navigation, while the shipment remains the workflow source of truth. The factory warehouse sees supplier-confirmed deliveries in its existing pending queue, with shipped quantities prefilled as proposed actual receipts; staff can mark individual lines or the entire note as not arrived and adjust damaged, rejected or unusable quantities. For each unmatched row actually received, warehouse must explicitly classify a needed sample/no-order receipt or wrong delivery. Sample acceptance requires an active factory customer, requester, positive unit price, quantity, unit and warehouse allocation; purpose is optional in both the browser and API, including omitted, blank or short text. Provided purpose remains in receipt feedback, while blank purpose is recorded as 未填写 rather than inferred. This optional-purpose change is locally verified and awaits coordinated frontend/backend release. Acceptance creates an AD_HOC receipt line with no formal order link, a single inventory inbound and normal acceptance-date supplier month-end source. Wrong delivery requires full rejection and a reason, retaining vendor-note feedback without receipt, inventory or payable. A new exact formal contract/item before warehouse confirmation blocks sample classification. Staff with current-factory read, receipt_write and inventory_write may explicitly select the active master customer and an issued formal paper to link a pending (or wholly reversed) unmatched delivery row before acceptance. Link requires shipment/order revisions, factory/supplier/customer scope, exact contract/item and complete compatible material identity, available demand after posted/pending/in-transit reservations, no replacement quota and no duplicate target in that note. It preserves the original unmatched row and source snapshot plus an immutable reasoned association audit, adds one formal shipment row, reserves demand but creates no receipt/stock/payable, then continues through the same vendor note acceptance; unchanged replay returns the association and changed reuse is rejected. An authorized internal order-adjust user may explicitly link a still-posted supplier-origin sample receipt to a later issued formal paper line of the same factory, supplier, customer, item, paper identity, dimensions and unit when unmet demand covers the sample quantity. The link is an immutable audit event that credits the existing sample receipt toward formal fulfillment without creating another receipt, inventory movement or month-end source; reversing the original receipt removes that credit and refreshes the formal order status. A whole note containing only rejected/wrong rows is resolved with no receipt; the original note and per-line decisions remain auditable. Effective receipt remains gated by explicit active-bin allocations totaling each positive line, in both the browser and backend; old receipt API compatibility is unchanged. In-transit shipments protect reduction, cancellation, returns and duplicate ordinary receipt entry. A Dongkang original-file import may explicitly register EXISTING_RECEIPT evidence for goods already manually posted, including completed issued orders and the original warehouse delivery-note number. This pending document reserves no further demand and cannot be received directly. Factory-scoped read, receipt_write and inventory_write users select one whole, unlinked original receipt with matching supplier, formal paper IDs, frozen material/dimension identity, counting unit/currency and vendor quantity equal to original delivered or gross received quantity. Association is actor/request-idempotent, checks both revisions under the shared factory lock, preserves original quantity, defects, price, bins and acceptance date, and creates no receipt, movement or payable source. Vendor feedback and collaboration settlement reuse the original receipt link and acceptance evidence; confirmed supplier months at the original acceptance or vendor delivery date require reasoned reopening first. Partial association across multiple receipts, mixed ad hoc receipts and replacement receipts remain unsupported and require human review. Ordinary new shipments that match existing receipts require explicit warehouse confirmation that the goods are a new physical batch. Reversal of an associated original reopens its vendor note for the existing reasoned correction flow. If an original is reversed before association, only a candidate saved during late registration with audited previously-POSTED reversal evidence may bind its historical lineage and enter correction; voided drafts and unrelated old reversals are never candidates. Missing legacy acceptance dates use the original confirmed timestamp in Asia/Shanghai, never the upload date. PDF and non-macro Office attachments are authenticated, limited to 10 MB, versioned and immutable. A confirmed order with supplier commitment, shipment or attachment evidence cannot be reassigned to a different supplier, so files from its prior supplier cannot become visible under a later issue to another supplier. Replacement/replenishment shipments continue through the existing replacement workflow and are not shipped through the normal supplier portal. The local business SQLite records Alembic `20261008_0146` as described in the carton schema contract above, including nullable carton paper weight snapshots and preserved legacy order-header weights, factory-scoped customer responsibilities and explicit multi-order carton-mark source links, fabric master data and audited starting-chase confirmation with unknown-baseline receiving, carton-mark photo originals and durable photo groups, the preceding local fabric-receiving schema and audience-isolated employee/supplier feedback updates, private screenshots and the independent carton-mark source repository, fabric purchase-source evidence plus the carton, work-center, UV, quote-alternatives, unmatched-delivery, IAM V2 and 3D telemetry revisions. The supplier revision and upstream spray revision join at no-op migration `20260923_0120`; the supplier branch and UV revision `20260923_0121` join at baseline backfill migration `20260924_0122`. The quote-alternatives branch joins at Alembic `20260924_0123`, and supplier unmatched delivery migration `20260924_0124` validates a pre-existing local table when one is present. Other databases below head require explicit migration. Additive merge revision `20260929_0128` joins the carton `20260929_0126` branch with the upstream work-center/UV branch ending at `20260929_0127`; it performs no DDL and joins the two branches before the later 3D telemetry revisions. The production revision, PostgreSQL head and pinned images are maintained in the canonical deployment section below; the unpublished local SQLite lineage above remains independent. Late-document receipt association uses existing shipment linkage and immutable audit fields and requires no schema migration; production activation and preservation evidence are maintained in the canonical deployment section below.

Migration `20260811_0065` adds `INSPECTION_SCHEDULE` import batches and a persisted import profile so the same source can be recalculated with a different advance-day rule without colliding with an earlier batch. Inspection reminders classify completed orders as ready, open orders as upcoming, due soon or overdue, and unmatched rows as missing or ambiguous; actionable results are copied into the existing exception center with the carton department as owner.

Customer carton splitting stays inside each eligible formal-order More menu as 拆单 / 拆分记录 for supplier-submitted, partially received or completed orders; it has no separate ledger-row button. A missing online entry or failed split requires diagnosis against the deployed build, authenticated account permission snapshot and actual order/API state; relocating the button does not establish that splitting works. Split-dialog request IDs use the shared secure-random UUID helper, which falls back to getRandomValues when native randomUUID is absent on the public HTTP origin; opening, editing and retrying must remain supported without changing replay semantics. This compatibility fix remains local: the published carton split dialog still crashes during initialization on HTTP until released. Procurement readers can open the context and historical records; missing create permissions are explained in the dialog instead of hiding the entry. The original write/adjustment and warehouse-confirmation authorization remains authoritative. It retains the original factory, customer, item, material, procurement issue and receipt evidence, and assigns quantities to new contract/customer-PO destinations. Pending arrivals and received available stock are allocated separately; posted and pending receipts, other pending splits and already used stock protect capacity. CALCULATED splits require each paper demand to equal the rounded child product/packing demand and reject any extra total rounding demand until separately procured. EXPLICIT demand may retain unknown product quantities without inventing them. Creation uses current-factory order_write plus the existing order_adjust OR order_write+inventory_write entitlement; warehouse stock confirmation additionally requires read, receipt_write and inventory_write. Stock-bearing plans remain PENDING_WAREHOUSE in the work board until reviewed; pending-only plans become ACTIVE immediately. Receipt entry and supplier delivery-note acceptance preview active destinations in saved-plan order, allocate only effective accepted quantities and require a current warehouse-confirmation token before atomic posting. Supplier shipment/receipt headers remain original, and allocations create equal opposite ORDER_SPLIT adjustments in the same physical positions using stable CSL- child identities. Exact original carrying amounts transfer with the cost projection, preserving total quantity/value and avoiding another supplier payable; child usage and returns retain supplier provenance. The immutable carton_order_split audit aggregate stores plan, revision, request replay and movement pairs without a schema migration. Order and child inventory timelines retain the complete provenance. Bound business schedules recognize split destinations by selected master customer, contract/item and supplied PO, compare the child quantity or original remainder, block duplicate source/direct creation, and link cancellation reminders to the original order. Unknown or ambiguous quantities/POs require review. Live splits block reduction, cancellation, return and structural changes of the original order until the split is explicitly undone; any split history blocks order deletion. Warehouse-confirmed pairs can be reversed together only before child consumption/correction, with original/current accounting locks enforced. Single-side movement reversal is forbidden. Original receipt correction requires undoing its active split first; consumed child history requires explicit warehouse/supervisor reconciliation rather than deleting allocation evidence.

Migration `20260805_0054` adds the factory-scoped carton customer master and `carton_procurement:customer_manage`; the current master_manage compatibility path also permits the default carton/general warehouse operators to create customers. Customer responsibility restricts business writes within the existing factory/IAM scope and never grants IAM rights. 未认领（没有责任人员）的客户向现有岗位人员开放操作；有本厂 read 加 order_write 或 receipt_write 的员工可以“我来负责”，首次认领成为负责人。负责人可授权同厂有效且具有相应岗位权限的协作人员；协作人员不能转授权。主管、经理和管理员必须仍具备本厂直接 order_adjust 统筹权限，才可跨客户操作、转交负责人或清空责任范围重新开放；order_write + inventory_write 的普通仓管组合不构成统筹。订单管理、待收与收料历史默认“我负责的 + 未认领”，可切换“全部客户”；未获客户授权仍可查看同厂其他客户的订单、收料与采购单原始记录，切换查看范围不授予写权限。库存、收发之外的库存操作、QC 及其他原职责范围不因客户归属缩小。新增/修改/确认、追加/减单/取消/删除、生成采购单、拆单、补单、退单、历史订单导入、业务来源标记及客户专属基础资料写入均在原岗位授权之外校验客户操作范围；收料创建、确认、冲销和供应商实际验收/补关联也检查全部涉及客户，混合单据必须全部获授权且整单原子操作。客户认领/授权与订单及收料写入共享厂区事务锁，按客户 revision 拒绝过期保存与并发抢占，并审计负责人及协作人员变更。负责人必须保留自身授权，转交或解除认领由统筹人员处理；旧多人分配没有可信首次认领记录时保持原范围并由主管指定负责人，旧单人分配迁移为该人员负责。责任按稳定 customer_id、user_id 保存，改名或 IAM 源合并不自动扩权。订单工作区的小型摘要包含 own_customer_codes 与 blocked_customer_codes；仅客户认领管理表单请求可授权人员目录，读取失败时前端不开放写按钮。客户责任管理仅在厂区、账号 ID、授权版本/模式或有效任职上下文变化时重新核验；45 秒常规身份心跳不清空已加载列表或未保存授权。真正的权限边界变化仍中止旧请求并清除旧操作控件后重新读取。此刷新修复已包含在当前正式发布版本。New carton orders resolve the canonical submitted customer code against the current factory master and save the authoritative customer name. Referenced customers cannot be deleted or change their internal code; deactivation retains historical identity and descriptions.

Migration `20260805_0055` historically promoted the then-existing `PENDING_SUPPLIER` rows to `CONFIRMED`. Current semantics use `CONFIRMED` as the editable `待下单` state and `PENDING_SUPPLIER` only after the explicit `确认订单并锁定` action; the latter is displayed as `已确认锁定` and remains active for weekly reconciliation, delivery-note matching, receipt and inventory follow-up. Confirmation and locking is an internal state transition and does not send a file; supplier execution begins only from the separately named `发行供应商采购单` action. A persisted order's current cumulative totals remain downloadable only as an explicitly labeled internal reconciliation reference. Multi-select cumulative export generates one `.xlsx` `纸箱累计对账表` with all selected formal-order headers and paper-item lines, and the workbook states that it is not a new supplier order. Supplier execution uses only the independently numbered immutable issue workbooks and their `本次变化` columns.

The frontend supports weekly-file import and reconciliation, delivery-file import and search, imported-line receipt review, and direct manual receipt entry from one or more selected submitted orders. Receipt feedback defaults to a pending-order list on entry and factory changes, using the order-management ledger’s compact aligned rows: order date, customer, contract, item number/product name together, paper-item totals and breakdown, customer/planned due dates, arrival progress, status and due reminder. Compact pending-order rows and receipt-dialog material/source cells retain the generated order identity for selection and linkage; expanded paper selection explicitly displays the original order number together with customer PO and item to distinguish same-contract orders. Receipt-dialog formal material identities display contract and item numbers separately, and manual source badges display only `人工录入`. The same full-order detail preview exposes paper specifications and per-line required/received/remaining quantities. The pending-order selection toolbar offers the sole visible customer filter in manual receipt mode; the duplicate workspace-header selector is hidden there and on the orders tab, while import mode retains the header selector. Both positions use the same customer state for pending orders and receipt history. The toolbar also offers an inclusive planned supplier due-date range based on `due_date`, independent of customer due dates. Pending orders default to planned due date ascending (overdue first), with descending and `下单日期最新` options (the latter preserves backend order: order date descending, then creation time descending); sorting preserves selections and resets to ascending on factory change. A single range button opens a two-month calendar: the first click starts selection, the second completes an inclusive range and automatically applies/closes it. Partial selection never changes the active filter; ranges can be cleared and reset on factory change. Select-all acts on the currently filtered orders and sits in the pending-order list header directly above the row checkboxes; customer/date/sort filters stay in the toolbar. The shared header search and multi-selection remain supported; the receipt workspace has no separate search box or standalone manual-entry action bar. Manual entry starts by choosing a contract and selecting its arrived paper lines; the expanded selection list has no actual-received quantity input. Quantities, defects, prices and allocations are entered in the receipt dialog. Per-order and selected-order registration actions remain available. The delivery-upload action lives only in import mode, which provides an explicit return to the pending-order list without opening a receipt dialog. Delivery-note number/date and editable receipt feedback stay in a dialog opened for one or more selected orders, with draft values retained when closing and reopening. Reopening the same selection resumes unsaved or PENDING_CONFIRMATION receipt work. A POSTED or REVERSED receipt instead starts a fresh draft from the selected orders that still have remaining quantities, so consecutive partial batches can be registered without reloading. Import review uses the same receipt dialog. The latest delivery import is restored only after switching to import mode; it must not replace the default pending-order list. Confirmed but unsubmitted orders do not expose a receipt entry action, and imported receipt rows referencing one are rejected before save as well as by the backend. Both imported and manual receipt UIs use one explicit confirm-inbound submission, atomically saving and posting as described above; the legacy draft API remains available. Partial effective receipt changes the order to partially received, and every line reaching its required quantity changes the order to completed. Migration `20260830_0086` makes receipt order-line linkage nullable and records each receipt line as `FORMAL_ORDER` or `AD_HOC`. A missing-order delivery row can be explicitly accepted as non-formal/sample-board receipt only after the user selects an active customer and completes the item, packaging type, paper quality, specification, unit, quantity and price snapshot; it never creates a formal order. Direct confirmation creates a standalone inbound movement with no order-line foreign key and includes it in normal closing generation. Only legacy draft-mode saves remain outside inventory until explicitly confirmed. Manual formal-order receipt starts with an explicit customer-and-contract selector, expands the original orders with PO/item/order identities, and lets the warehouse select paper lines independently. New paper lines are unselected and actual quantities are blank; only selected lines participate in validation and atomic posting. Inside the manual receipt dialog, the receipt feedback table lists all pending paper drafts with a left-hand selection checkbox and a single actual-quantity editor per row; there is no duplicated selection table above it. Unchecked rows remain visible and retain draft values, but are disabled and excluded from receipt totals. Adding contracts preserves existing draft quantities, prices, locations and delivery metadata. Unreceived sibling papers remain pending and cannot make the parent order complete. Order-time prices may be unknown. Both manual and imported formal receipts expose an editable per-line inbound unit price, defaulting to the order price and allowing delivery-note overrides with up to six decimal places; formal currency and unit remain the order snapshot. Blank/zero is explicitly pending price verification. Saving freezes the receipt price, and confirmation copies it into that inbound movement without rewriting order prices or earlier receipts. Outbound defaults to the selected balance and the selectable reason `客户要货`, with `补货`, `借出`, `调拨`, `损耗` and `其他` as common alternatives; selected balances can be posted atomically with a separately editable positive outbound quantity per inventory row, initially filled with its current balance. Each row shows the resulting balance; blank, nonpositive, more-than-four-decimal and over-balance quantities are rejected before submission, and the backend rechecks current stock atomically. The inventory workspace defaults to a prominent balance ledger, with customer and latest-inbound date filters in the toolbar, a select-all checkbox in the first table-header cell aligned above the row checkboxes, compact totals, separate customer and contract-number columns, and the latest source document for each balance. Contract/source-document and material searches use the shared header search; there are no duplicate balance search fields. Clearing balance filters clears customer, date range and the shared search. Both balance inbound dates and transaction dates use the shared consecutive-click date-range calendar. The balance API derives nullable `latest_inbound_at` from the complete inbound ledger for each balance key; later outbound, adjustment and reversal movements do not change it. Positive HISTORY_INVENTORY opening adjustments also contribute their document posting time to latest_inbound_at, so opening stock remains visible under recent-inbound date filters; later issues, transfers and stocktakes do not replace that date. Balances without either inbound or opening records are excluded from an active inbound-date range. Per-row outbound and relocation actions open separate dialogs with the selected balance; the selection toolbar exposes inventory stocktaking. Manual quantity-adjustment entry points, including the outbound dialog toggle, are removed; physical/book differences use reviewed stocktaking, while wrong source documents use reversal/reentry and location changes use transfers. Relocation now supports partial/full physical transfers between stable warehouse/bin IDs as described below. The legacy whole-balance relocation endpoint remains compatible and preserves its audit evidence; current authoritative distribution comes from physical allocations, while original receiving locations and inbound dates remain unchanged. The audit log shows the old/new locations, item, contract and actor; selected-row bulk outbound opens a separate editable quantity table with customer/contract, material, location, current balance, this shipment quantity and remaining balance, followed by the shared source document and reason. Operation fields are hidden during normal ledger browsing. Opening-stock management is accessible as the 期初库存 subtab under inventory management, alongside real-time stock, summary, movements and stocktaking. It provides a simplified template and a permission-gated batch-settings → read-only preview → explicit confirmation flow. The file picker is available before settings are complete and retains the selected file; preview explicitly identifies missing settings and confirmation remains gated. Batch settings require only an explicit cm/in dimension unit; the simplified path uses CNY. The UI has no opening baseline date input. Each nonzero row requires a complete 入库时间 (also accepts 原入库时间 / 入库日期 / 日期), normalized to Asia/Shanghai with supplied time retained; it controls that row’s HISTORY_INVENTORY posting time, source identity, duplicate comparison and inventory closing month. Missing, invalid, yearless and future dates block confirmation with source-row feedback. The legacy API snapshot_date remains an optional fallback only when the file supplies no row date, and never overrides an explicit inbound time. Customer and warehouse are optional defaults: explicit per-row fields take precedence and one workbook may contain multiple customers, warehouses and bins. Customer may remain completely absent for opening stock: it uses the stable inventory-only code __OPENING_UNASSIGNED__ and display 未指定客户, without creating customer master data or guessing an order/customer from PO. Explicit customer values still require an active same-factory match. Each nonzero row must resolve a unique active bin; ambiguous bin names require an explicit warehouse. Preview displays the resolved customer and warehouse/bin before atomic confirmation. The main sheet contains PO, item, combined paper/material, length, width, height, bin, actual remaining quantity and optional unit price. The template includes warehouse and required inbound time; normalized row posting time is shown in preview and audit. Opening balances remain adjustments excluded from supplier purchases and arrival progress. Posting current remaining quantities at original inbound dates affects unlocked historical inventory reports and subsequent carry-forward; it does not reconstruct the full original historical receipts/issues. The row month and all later locked inventory periods remain protected. Original inbound/outbound columns are not replayed; zero remaining rows skip, incomplete/negative stock and mismatched remaining amounts block the batch, and missing/zero prices remain pending verification rather than free. Combined material labels such as A33+B外箱 and H5A平卡 split explicitly, flat paper permits zero height, and dimensions are never guessed from numeric size. Active bins must uniquely match the row warehouse (or optional default), with an explicit system unknown-bin option; conflicting legacy row baseline dates and inbound dates, or row unit/currency conflicts, are rejected. Preview displays normalized paper identities, unit/currency-separated totals, missing prices and duplicate/conflict evidence without writing inventory. Confirmation rechecks the file/settings/database fingerprint under the factory transaction lock and posts all or nothing. Simplified duplicate checks group by physical paper type/quality/specification, contract, item, customer, unit and stable bin, then compare normalized inbound time, quantity and price, independent of row order or user source-line labels. Different paper qualities remain separate balances and may have different prices under the same contract/item/bin. Identical already-posted balances skip even after later outbound. The same physical identity with conflicting time/quantity/price still blocks rather than adding or overwriting; same-file errors identify the earlier row, material/bin and actual differing values. This path does not yet establish independently repeatable multiple source-batch opening balances for one physical identity. Ambiguous legacy dimension units remain blocked. Uniquely matched formal lines require the same explicit dimension unit; other stock keeps a unit-marked standalone identity. Row-level source and posting evidence is retained in audit. Old full-template API imports remain compatible with stronger same-source conflict checks. Imports append HISTORY_INVENTORY adjustments and never create receipts, restore historical fulfillment, or enter supplier current-period purchases. Entering the page writes nothing. A real cutover still requires verified physical remaining stock and checked source inbound dates; this is not an overwrite-current-stock operation. The obsolete More menu stays removed. The movement ledger remains below the balances; the former outbound overview has moved to the dedicated inventory-summary tab. The movement ledger shares customer and header keyword filters with balances, without a duplicate local search field, and retains its own movement-type and date-range filters plus clear-all. Its date comparison uses the posting date portion, including the full ending day. The UI explicitly states that warehouse/location/latest-inbound filters affect only the balance table. Confirmed inbound and outbound movements feed the dedicated inventory-summary report described below; the former receipt-page inbound overview has moved there. The exception center defaults to open/in-progress items with explicit status/type and clear filters. Month-end history has independent query-month/status filters; generating snapshots is a separate permission-gated action, preserves other loaded months, and discards responses after factory/context changes. The frontend supports persisted exception status/resolution actions and a unified operation log backed by immutable audit events; supplier submission, order update, append and return store the business order number, actor and timestamp, and the log provides date, actor and action filters. Closing generation and status progression remain permission-protected. The frontend loads formal backend orders, movements, summaries, audit events, closings and exceptions when available and falls back to an explicitly labeled read-only demonstration only when the backend cannot be reached. Imported values remain non-authoritative until a person reviews them; locked closings remain immutable.

The independent `库存汇总` tab (`tab=inventory-summary`) combines the former receipt inbound overview and inventory outbound overview into a read-only report, with an order-based summary, a per-order activity preview and inbound, outbound and adjustment detail views. The summary groups cumulative/selected-period flows by actual formal order line and inventory identity, showing order date, customer, contract, item/product name, paper/specification, unit, opening, inbound, outbound, adjustment and ending quantities. Distinct orders with identical printed contract/item values remain separate, as do paper lines and units; generated order numbers remain hidden. Active formal orders without movements display zero, and standalone stock is explicitly labeled rather than assigned to a fabricated order. Date filters retain rows with period activity or nonzero opening, plus active zero-history orders whose order dates fall within the range. `/api/carton-procurement/inventory/report` requires the existing factory-scoped carton read permission and calculates quantities in Decimal from the complete ledger, never from the movement list's first 500 records. It reports opening + net inbound - net outbound + signed adjustment = ending, retaining prior matching history for opening balances. The API also retains its daily customer/unit rows for compatibility; only those daily rows list activity dates. Date boundaries use Asia/Shanghai posting time, including both endpoints, independently of delivery-note dates. Reversals retain their original flow category with a negative net quantity on the reversal date; opening imports and count gains/losses remain separate adjustments. Customer filters use stable codes, units remain separate, and exact contract/material identities remain intact in detail and search matching. The shared header keyword identifies matching inventory identities through customer, contract, item, material, location or historical document; all history for those identities remains included so balances stay meaningful. Formal-order product names can also locate those identities, including orders without inventory history. The report customer/date/clear toolbar sits below the summary/detail tabs, immediately above the table and below the quantity cards, and remains usable during loading/errors. It has no duplicate search field; clearing filters also clears the header keyword, and statistics follow the same active filters. Frontend total display uses scaled integers to preserve four-decimal quantities without mixing units; stale responses cannot restore data after factory/filter changes. Manual multi-line movements with the same type/document number count as one document per day/customer/unit; receipt documents retain their source IDs. The per-summary-row `订单流水` action reads `/inventory/order-timeline` under the same factory-scoped read permission. It opens the selected order in a modal only when the button is activated by click or keyboard; pointer hover and focus alone do not open it. The modal supports scrolling and filtering; close, backdrop click or Escape dismisses it. There is no separate timeline tab or all-orders entry. The selected order’s business-change history is shown independently of the summary date/search filters; changing the surrounding factory/customer/search/date/view closes the overlay. Exact formal order IDs include all paper lines of that order; standalone rows use their server-derived inventory identity. The timeline combines immutable order audit events (creation/import, modification, locking, append/reduce, cancel/return and supplier document issue), receipt save/void state evidence, actual inbound/outbound/adjustment/reversal movements, relocation and missing-price confirmation. Positive receipt confirmation/reversal is represented by the actual movement once rather than duplicated audit quantities; zero-effective confirmations remain state-only. Quantity changes distinguish order product pieces from paper inventory units; before/after order quantities use saved audit snapshots, and edits describe captured field changes. New order creation also records its original quantity and identity in the audit event; legacy creation records without that snapshot explicitly remain unknown instead of borrowing current quantities. Inventory before/after quantities follow complete history and persisted audit sequence; ambiguous same-second unsequenced legacy entries show only the actual delta. The preview shows only creation/history import, append, reduction, cancellation/return, actual inventory movements (inbound/outbound/adjustment/reversal) and relocation. Procedural saves, confirmation/locking, supplier issue, order-edit and price-confirmation audit events remain available in the operation log and API but are omitted from this concise preview and its filter/count. Contract/item appear once in the overlay title, customer/product once above the table; rows show time, business event, paper/document, quantity change, note and actor. The preview defaults to chronological order and supports reverse order and exact operation-subtype filtering. The API retains customer/date/search filters and cancelled-order history, but the UI only opens the exact selected order or standalone stock identity and never requests all orders. This report does not change posting, relocation, stocktaking, monthly cost projection or lock rules. It introduces no shared-contract stock merging, workshop consumption ledger, fixed safety-stock threshold or new money calculation. Each order row also exposes current usage status/quantity and expandable current physical location balances; these fields are explicitly current even when the quantity report is filtered to a historical period. All matched detail records currently return together without pagination or export; large-history performance has not been established.

Carton physical inventory uses factory-scoped stable warehouse/bin IDs in `carton_locations` and immutable quantity allocations in `carton_position_entries` (migration `20260908_0103`, online only, startup guard before create_all). Each original inventory movement is allocated exactly once across positions, with signed allocations summing to its original quantity; receipts still produce one main INBOUND per effective receipt line. Receipt/relocation location entry searches existing active same-factory catalog labels and emits only the unique matching stable ID; arbitrary or ambiguous text clears the selection and cannot post. The picker does not create locations; creation remains in master data. Receipt drafts freeze location labels and quantities; the browser requires positive effective quantities to be fully assigned, while legacy API submissions without allocations use the unverified/default compatibility path. Single/bulk outbound and adjustments use actual location IDs and may never make a position negative; same material in two positions is a valid bulk selection. Normal receipts, returns, reversals, opening imports and reviewed counts update main and physical ledgers atomically under the factory lock. Partial/full same-factory transfers post zero-sum physical entries only and an audited, revision-checked, idempotent request; they never change cost identity, total quantity, inbound/outbound figures or usage. Reversal restores original location allocations and is blocked if the required source stock has since moved or been used. The physical list reads movement, location and allocation metadata in one joined SQL snapshot to avoid mixing concurrent commits. The realtime inventory table fits its container without a horizontal minimum width: contract and item have separate columns, while paper/quality/specification share a cell, recent movement metadata stays in the row title, and optional money appears under balance. Narrow screens use stacked grid rows. Each visible inventory row separates 累计入库, 累计出库, 当前结余 and 到货情况, with no stock/demand progress bar. Quantity columns use the full joined physical-allocation snapshot for the exact position, never the 500-row movement UI or arrival minus stock. Net inbound/outbound reverse their original categories; opening stock, net transfers and other adjustments are separate fields shown beneath the emphasized balance when nonzero. Opening + inbound - outbound + transfers + adjustments equals balance. Outbound includes supplier returns and other stock deductions. Missing compatibility fields display unknown rather than guessed zero. Order arrivals join only by immutable order-line ID and matching unit; split positions share line arrival facts, while standalone or unmatched lines explicitly lack arrival facts. The display uses current order and inventory responses and does not change posting or order status. Daily inventory filters include warehouse/location and identity-level latest INBOUND time; transfers retain that inbound date. The realtime inventory sorting selector orders the current filtered rows by customer, contract, item, natural warehouse/bin order, numeric balance (ascending/descending) or latest inbound date (newest/oldest); unknown inbound dates stay last, default restores the source order, and sorting preserves selection by stable position identity without writing the ledger. Main movement location text is only a bounded summary; full labels and quantities belong to allocation details. Catalog creation normalizes names and reuses duplicates within factory. Creation, revision-checked rename and deactivation require factory-scoped master_manage, supplied by the default warehouse/carton operator and related supervisor roles. Legacy warehouse-name grants no longer authorize any write. Ordinary free-text receipt/legacy transfer paths cannot create arbitrary locations. Inactive locations reject new positive posting and transfer-in, while outbound, transfer-out and stock-constrained reversals remain possible. Names in earlier receipt/movement snapshots remain unchanged. Standalone quantity/position identities use `STK2:` plus a SHA-256 of the structured seven-field identity; formal order-line IDs remain unchanged. APIs return the authoritative key and the frontend reuses it. Legacy allocation keys are interpreted through their immutable source movement; old transfer keys resolve only when unique in the same factory, without rewriting original records. New posting prechecks prospective aliases so it cannot make a readable old transfer ambiguous. Ambiguous legacy transfers block posting instead of guessing ownership; ambiguous old stocktakes show a reconciliation error with unknown current quantity and can be cancelled by authorized users, but cannot be submitted/approved. Unambiguous old counts retain their original keys, cutoff tokens and revision evidence, and overlap checks include them. Cross-factory transfers and actual workshop-consumption accounting are not implemented. Legacy distribution cannot be reconstructed from old free-text locations: migration allocates old movements to the factory's `待核仓位`, preserving all original evidence and cost, for users to verify and transfer later. Downgrade refuses to delete existing physical ledger evidence. The local SQLite upgrade is active with a pre-upgrade backup under `backend/data/qa/20260908-carton-positions`; production deployment and restored-copy PostgreSQL migration acceptance are maintained in the canonical deployment section.

Carton usage status is independent of procurement progress. `issue_kind` is USAGE / RETURN / LOSS / LOAN / OTHER / UNKNOWN; the browser defaults to normal usage/shipment and offers reasons appropriate to the selected purpose. Only net USAGE outbounds (with reversals inheriting their original purpose) count as normal release; return, loss, loan, transfers and stocktake adjustments do not count as usage. Status is derived from complete current movements per exact paper identity: NOT_RECEIVED, UNUSED, PARTIAL, EXHAUSTED (`当前已领完`), CLEARED (`库存已结清` for mixed non-usage depletion), or UNKNOWN (`领用待核实` for unresolved historical purpose/quantity anomalies). Order aggregation considers actual release facts without summing units. Current stock may be exhausted while procurement remains partially received; later receipt reopens usage progress. Old untyped outbounds remain UNKNOWN instead of being inferred from free-text reasons. Normal release measures warehouse handover, not actual workshop consumption, and no manual completion flag or old-purpose correction workflow has been added. Single/bulk outbound optionally records a separate active factory WORKSHOP id and immutable name; per-line destinations override the shared destination, and reversals inherit the original name/purpose even after catalog changes. Order listings precompute usage once per factory batch.


Inventory stocktaking offers paper-type/unit filters for positions, and creation-date/state filters plus ascending/descending ordering for document history before server pagination. Clearing queries preserves sorting; an explicit restore action resets it. It reuses selected physical inventory keys from the main ledger: clicking stocktaking with a selection creates its draft once and opens actual-count entry directly, without a second selection/generate step. Entry without a selection opens the customer/location/contract/item selector and document history with a server-side status filter applied before pagination; failed document queries clear stale results. select-all sits in the first table header aligned above row checkboxes. Returning to the count list does not automatically create another draft, and stale selections or leaving during initial loading prevent automatic creation. Persisted DRAFT/SUBMITTED/POSTED/CANCELLED documents and the 500-position-per-sheet limit remain unchanged. Counts are scoped to the exact inventory identity and physical location. Overlapping active counts on the same factory position are blocked; distinct locations can be counted independently. Count documents stay attached to their original location after transfers; old whole-balance active documents must be cancelled and recreated before posting under the new rules. Creation snapshots book quantity and material/location metadata; physical quantities distinguish blank from zero and differences require reasons. Draft save and submit use document revisions plus a server ledger token bound to factory/document/lines, every movement ID and effective location/revision, so net-zero intervening transactions and move-away/back also invalidate the cutoff. Saved inputs whose ledger basis changed remain explicitly marked for rechecking; an unacknowledged draft save cannot clear that warning or silently replace the saved counting basis. The current UI lets supervisors and warehouse keepers with factory-scoped inventory_write create their own count, fill actual quantities and reasons, acknowledge the cutoff, then explicitly confirm submission and posting in a second confirmation dialog. CONFIRM requires posting_confirmed=true and a fresh ledger token server-side; drafts additionally require complete counts and cutoff acknowledgment. It freezes actual minus the current book balance and appends fixed differences atomically under the factory lock in one request, moving DRAFT directly to POSTED without another supervisor. The creator may confirm their own count. Existing SUBMITTED documents can also be confirmed by inventory_write users after refreshing current evidence; their original submitted counts/differences/person/time remain unchanged, and intervening receipts/issues are preserved. Confirmation audits the line quantities, reasons, location and confirming actor. The old SUBMIT/APPROVE API actions retain the legacy two-person permissions for compatibility, but the current UI does not use them. Draft editing remains restricted to its creator; return/cancellation permissions retain their existing owner/supervisor rules. Changed effective location/revision, mixed currency/unit pools, negative resulting stock, valuation anomalies or locked posting months block review; zero differences retain evidence without zero movements. Gains use current average carrying cost, with no-cost gains remaining explicitly unpriced; losses consume the same weighted valuation. Posting is versioned and uniquely sourced per stocktake line, and retries cannot post twice. Return and cancellation require reasons. Migration `20260907_0102` adds stocktake headers/lines after 0101, startup verifies readiness before create_all, and downgrade is blocked once any count evidence exists. The carton development database under `D:\rr\backend\data` includes the physical-position and master-data schema at revision 0104; original quantities, prices and business evidence were preserved during its additive upgrade. Separately, the configured business SQLite at `D:\RR\royal-regent-nexus\backend\data\royal_regent_nexus.db` is at merged revision `20260908_0105`, including both document-tool and carton position/master schemas; see the local V3 database state below. These distinct database paths must not be treated as one migration state. Stocktake behavior has isolated verification; the basic live receive/issue test does not establish live stocktake review coverage. The stocktake workspace provides browser entry and explicit confirmation/posting; bulk Excel count input is not implemented.



### Fabric and Semi-Finished Warehouses — Purchase Sources and Navigation

The user-defined direction is to replace independently maintained operational documents with connected, structured records across engineering, Sales, purchasing, the fabric/material warehouse, cutting, outsourcing, semi-finished storage and packaging/finished storage. Master data is a prerequisite: material identity/specification/color/unit, suppliers and subcontractors, products/styles/parts and locations must remain consistently identifiable. Material, supplier, price and quantity evidence must stay in the system. Fabric storage does not own purchasing or have an independent order-management workspace; it receives already-issued purchase information in 收料入库. It has seven main navigation areas, while semi-finished storage retains eight; both reuse carton interaction habits. Fabric purchase-source import/query, confirmed actual receiving with inbound batch inventory, and factory-scoped fabric master-data maintenance are operational. Issuing, opening balances, quality release, returns/corrections/month-end and the semi-finished warehouse remain unconnected. Purchase history is explicitly not an opening-stock migration.

The user primarily owns the carton module and has chosen fabric first, then semi-finished storage, reusing the carton page framework and common capabilities while defining each warehouse's business properties independently. The user has authorized rebuilding the two existing warehouse entries and their framework pages. They now use the carton-style top bar and horizontal navigation through `src/features/warehouse-foundation/WarehouseWorkbench.vue`, retaining existing routes, warehouse navigation counts, purchasing responsibilities and real fabric purchase-source ingestion. The existing carton implementation is not a prerequisite refactor. The shared `WarehouseDocumentPreview.vue` and `WarehousePreviewFields.vue` offer temporary receipt, issue and stock-detail previews, with generic field/repeatable-group types in foundation `documentPreview.ts`. Fabric definitions in `warehouse-operations/fabricDocumentPreviews.ts` distinguish fabric/accessory/thread categories, source and business dates versus accounting month, prior cumulative versus current quantities, repeatable dye-lot/roll/bin details, multiple demand allocations, quality/hold/ownership states and price evidence. Semi-finished definitions remain in `documentPreviews.ts` with their existing process/cycle fields. Preview rows use stable local keys; deleting a filled row or leaving filled scalar/repeatable content requires discard confirmation. Values stay in component memory, preserve explicit zero and original units, and clear when the preview closes or its warehouse/kind changes. No preview saves documents, converts units, sums allocations, calculates amounts or posts stock; option labels do not define approved business state transitions. Business filters and posting actions in unconnected framework views remain disabled until connected. Catalog descriptions identify fabric purchase/receipt capabilities and semi-finished pending processing workflows. `docs/business/warehouse-framework-reuse-plan.md` distinguishes the implemented framework from future domain integration; its remaining opening/issuing, barcode, quality-release, valuation and cutover proposals are not delivered capabilities. Do not copy carton-specific supplier, inventory, pricing or permission rules into these domains.

The Huakang C PMC/warehouse catalog exposes separate fabric and semi-finished warehouse entries at `/modules/pmc-warehouse/fabric-warehouse` and `/modules/pmc-warehouse/semi-finished-warehouse`, defaulting to `/overview`. Both use the shared shell and declarative page definitions in `src/features/warehouse-operations/`: overview, receipts, inventory, exceptions, closing, master and activity, plus orders for semi-finished storage only. Both inventory workspaces separate stock, receipts/issues summary, unified movements, returns, transfers and stocktaking; semi-finished storage additionally separates processing custody and packaging handoffs. Fabric 收料入库 has exactly five tabs in order: 订单导入 (`import-review`), 待收料 (`pending`, the daily default), 送货单扫描 (`delivery-scan`), 入库记录 (`history`) and 采购接口 (`procurement-interface`). Scan and interface pages are explicit pending-integration placeholders, with no OCR, credentials or automatic upstream synchronization. 订单导入 receives purchase documents and provides read-only links with `source=all` / `source=returned` for all imported sources / returned historical references; neither is an actual warehouse receipt. 待收料 defaults to outstanding purchase sources (including partial arrivals), with separate not-arrived/unknown, partial, procurement-reported-arrived and unread-change filters. Procurement reference quantities remain separate from warehouse receipt progress. Unknown references/baselines remain null; recorded system receipt totals never include imported history. Legacy fabric orders URLs redirect to these corresponding source queries or pending receipts. Supplier-return records were an unconnected framework and are removed from fabric receiving; workshop material returns remain a distinct planned inventory view. Other unconnected business views retain disabled filters/actions and explicit not-connected states without simulated quantities; fabric master data is a real operational workspace. Semi-finished fields retain processing state in stock/monthly/stocktake records and distinguish quantities and units before and after processing. Stable view IDs use the `view` query parameter for refresh, sharing and browser history; the mounted shell remembers selection independently per warehouse/section path. Mobile uses a subview selector and reveals the active primary item. Dashboard shortcuts and the searchable usage guide link to exact subviews. Entry and mounted route updates require one explicit `huakang-c`; source/receiving APIs independently enforce canonical `fabric_warehouse:read` / `fabric_warehouse:import` / `fabric_warehouse:receive` for Huakang C and PMC/warehouse. Old fixed V2 role versions need an explicit receive grant/identity upgrade; current position templates include it. Semi-finished small external purchases and outsourced processing show intake views with ordering/approval ownership still unconfirmed; available-stock, conversion, loss and settlement calculation rules remain unimplemented.

Purchase-source ingestion accepts the daily Kingdee-derived material-tracking `.xlsx`, specifically `未回物料`, `已回料`, `补数未回物料` and `补数已回` by sheet name and business headers. Supplements are independently identified (including upstream ID namespace) and retain supplement number, style, workshop group and release date; release date is not order date. Ordinary identities remain compatible. The worker-facing `退货记录` mixes excess returns, quality returns, internal recipients and already/partly replaced goods; `记录罚款` is deduction follow-up. Neither blindly creates pending receipts or stock. Historical return-pending/returned auxiliary sheets contain delivered/cancelled/no-delivery records and also require an explicit replacement contract. The default TRACKING scope adds pending-sheet sources and reads returned-sheet changes only for already-known active identities, excluding unrelated returned history; explicit pending-only/returned/all scopes remain available. A tracked pending source that becomes returned or has reported quantity at least ordered quantity moves out of outstanding into procurement-reported-arrived / warehouse-review, never into actual receipts. UPDATE evidence creates persistent unread reminders; marking them read is version-bound and separately audited in batch result metadata, does not acknowledge physical receipt and does not remove arrival-review items. The visible pending page refreshes every minute; there is no external/push notification or automatic Kingdee fetch. The user confirms that returned material remains historical reference and adds no stock. Partial deliveries in the pending sheet remain pending sources. Original units, prices, reply text, delivery facts and raw cell/formula/number-format evidence are preserved; formulas are never executed, unknown quantities/prices are not replaced with zero, and no unit/currency conversion or master-data creation is inferred. Preview lists new/changed/same/blocked rows, confirmation saves every usable page atomically and explicitly skips blocked rows. Migration `20261005_0131` adds four evidence/source tables only; existing databases require explicit migration, missing schema returns 503 for this domain, and downgrade refuses to remove existing evidence. Supplier delivery-note file parsing is not implemented; manual delivery references accompany actual warehouse-confirmed receipts. Import history supports paginated, previewed withdrawal of the latest active batch only, with a required reason and read+import authority. Withdrawal hides newly introduced lines using WITHDRAWN tombstones, restores prior source facts for updates and preserves identities/evidence; exact re-imports match full source facts to preserve original identities even after row reordering; ambiguous changed multi-line tombstones receive new identity slots rather than reassigning historical evidence. All batches receive a monotonic factory sequence; imports, review acknowledgements and withdrawals share the factory write lock/revision. Legacy tied timestamps block ambiguous rollback. Batch result JSON holds withdrawal/review metadata; no additional schema migration is required. Older code that does not understand WITHDRAWN must not be used after withdrawal. Withdrawal preview/execution blocks any affected source with a receipt or a starting-chase confirmation, including updates; receipt posting and chase confirmation advance the shared factory revision and invalidate old source previews. The receipt guard works on the older receiving schema before the new migration, so a missing new column cannot bypass rollback protection. The contract and procedure are in `docs/business/fabric-procurement-import.md`. The separately supplied actual-receipt, issue, production-release and monthly-stock workbooks are not supported by this purchase-source importer and must not be passed through it as stock migration.

Actual fabric receiving is separate from the temporary previews: `FabricReceiptDialog.vue` posts source-bound receipts and `FabricReceivingRecords.vue` queries history, batch stock and inbound movements. Migration `20261006_0135` adds `fabric_receipts`, `fabric_stock_batches`, `fabric_inventory_movements`; `20261007_0138` additively adds the receipt `baseline_known` flag and fabric master, master-change and starting-chase-resolution evidence tables. Existing stores require backup and explicit upgrade; business startup does not silently create fabric tables. Old receiving records default to a known baseline. Older receiving schemas can still expose recorded progress and enforce import-withdrawal protection, but new writes return 503 until upgraded. Downgrade refuses to delete master/audit/resolution evidence or actual receipts whose starting quantity is unknown.

Daily entry follows the carton receiving document layout: document header, source/remaining summary, physical split table, totals and fixed save footer. Receipt/delivery dates default to the current Asia/Shanghai day; accounting month is generated by the server from receipt date, with no independent input. Material category remains a manual fallback; only an ACTIVE material master matching source code, name and original unit can prefill its confirmed category, with no name-based guesses or engineering integration. Every split needs a location; fabric also requires dye-lot and optionally a roll number (single-roll identifier), while accessories/thread do not carry fabric dye/roll identifiers. Delivery date and notes are optional expandable fields. There is no first-receipt question or manual prior-receipt field. Clicking save-and-receive submits confirmed=true without a duplicate checkbox. The connected receiving page and receipt dialogs use the carton slate background, white cards and teal actions; overdue counts remain red. Subnavigation sits above one work card with compact follow-up filters. General receiving explanations and the temporary sample preview are under the collapsed 收料说明 entry, with no stacked framework-status or preview cards above the daily list. Purchase lists reuse the carton top search, compact inline filters, DateRangeFilter, CartonSelectionSummary and adjacent row actions. Text searches for purchase/production/release/style/material/supplier are unified in the header; the lower advanced area contains only category/unit/quantity-review/import selectors. Selection persists across pages and filters, exposes hidden selected items for removal, and permits at most 50 source lines. Batch entry freshly checks every selected source, shares receipt date and delivery/manual evidence, and retains separate category, physical splits, unit and overage reason for each source; actual quantities remain blank until entered, with no mixed-unit total. The batch receiving API reuses single-receipt validation under one factory lock and transaction, producing one receipt per source and rolling back all items on any failure.
