A batch root UUID binds actor and complete content; deterministic child UUIDs and batch metadata frozen in existing receipt payloads protect replay and prevent single/batch/root/child identity reuse. At most 500 physical splits are accepted per batch. Unknown outcomes freeze the entire submitted batch for retry. No batch schema migration is required. Only current positive physical split quantities post atomically, summed using Decimal in the original unit, maximum six decimals. Dye/roll leading zeros are preserved. An explanation is shown and required only for over-receipt beyond a known chase remaining; ordinary partial/short receipts need no reason, and unknown chase does not invent an over-receipt ceiling. Source facts/revision, actor, master references and batches are frozen; subsequent imports or master edits do not rewrite stock. New batches remain pending inspection, never automatically QC-released or valued. Same source/delivery reference is unique but one note may cover different sources; actor/content-bound UUID retries and source revision/receipt-count checks under the factory lock prevent duplicate or concurrent posting. Unknown frontend outcomes freeze the submitted request for retry, including HTTP 408; HTTP browser UUID fallback and dirty dismissal guards remain, with untouched defaults closing without confirmation.

Chase progress is independent of physical inventory. The first explicit valid reported-received quantity in active import evidence is the starting chase reference: an order of 665 with reference 628 starts owing 37. The first actual receipt freezes that reference and evidence, preventing later procurement reports from subtracting system receipts twice. In pending XLSX sheets (normal and supplement), empty arrival and delivery records mean no prior arrival, so chase starts at the full order quantity. A precise same-row EVALUATE of an empty delivery cell with an empty or #VALUE! cache also establishes zero, including bounded same-column shared formulas with relative row references; no formula is executed. Both quantity columns must exist; delivery formulas, other errors/formulas, unclear delivery text and conflicting totals remain unknown. This default does not apply to returned sheets or upstream feeds. The parser preserves the zero interpretation basis, raw headers, caches, formulas and formula attributes. Legacy duplicate groups may upgrade in place only when the complete original file digest, legacy facts and raw row/position prove unique counterparts and the sole change is this interpretation; other ambiguous multi-line changes still require stable detail IDs. Unknown historical quantities do not block current actual receiving when source/material/unit/supplier identity is clear and quantity facts do not conflict. The first receipt freezes the unknown state; later imports cannot retroactively establish a new baseline. Users with read+import authority can confirm the original starting quantity before all system receipts, with required evidence, source/receipt-count/resolution revision checks, actor/content-bound UUID replay and the shared factory lock. Confirming 37 after system receipts of 10 leaves 27, without changing stock; confirming zero closes chase without posting stock. Each confirmation is immutable and audited with the source and cutoff facts. A reference rollback, reference above order, ambiguous legacy chronology or order quantity changed from frozen facts requires quantity reconciliation; material/name/unit/supplier changes cannot be cleared by numerical confirmation. Worker lists show starting chase, system received and remaining chase, with order total and prior arrival under starting chase; detailed current procurement references remain in source detail. Pending and returned worksheets partition purchase-detail completion states rather than additive copies of an order; one PO can contain distinct material lines in both sheets. Fully received or manually confirmed-zero progress leaves outstanding/arrival-review. Current stock contains only system-recorded inbound batches; no complete opening stock, outgoing movements, return replacement, receipt correction, quality release or locked-month ledger is implemented. Current net inventory plus undelivered chase is not a reliable procurement total after consumption/returns. PostgreSQL real concurrent posting and shop-floor cutover have not been verified.

Purchase lists filter and sort before pagination, supporting unified keyword, supplier, purchase/supplement type, follow-up state, latest promise date range, confirmed category, original unit, quantity review and active import batch; granular order/production/style/material-code API filters remain compatible but do not duplicate text inputs in the daily UI. Sorts cover overdue, promise, order, supplier, updated and remaining quantity; quantity sort requires one original unit and puts unknown quantities last. Promise facts use the latest supplied reply, including text such as 等通知 that supersedes an older dated reply; no promise is inferred from order date. OVERDUE and DUE_TODAY require a known positive warehouse chase remaining. Unknown quantity with an elapsed promise gets an amber date/quantity-review hint, not a supplier-overdue count. Awaiting-date, quantity-review, unread-change and procurement-reported-arrived reminders remain distinct. Minute refresh pauses while a receipt/detail/quantity-confirmation dialog is open; no external reminder automation is configured.

Fabric master data has MATERIAL, SUPPLIER, LOCATION and UNIT kinds scoped to Huakang C, with DRAFT/ACTIVE/INACTIVE status, revision checks, actor/content-bound UUID replay and immutable before/after change evidence. Reads use existing fabric read authority; writes use read+import, without adding or broadening grants. Imported source facts are read-only candidate drafts; human confirmation is required to save/activate, and conflicting material names/units are flagged instead of silently merged. ACTIVE materials require code/name/category/unit; old code, specification, color and composition can be supplemented. Supplier codes are manually confirmed, with optional contact/phone; ACTIVE locations require warehouse identity and populate receipt choices. Fabric master uses carton-style compact setting cards and header search; warehouses group clickable location controls. A first location establishes a warehouse, with bounded range entry and blank XLSX/XLSM template preview/apply (5 MiB, 1000 data rows and expanded locations per batch), rejecting formulas and numeric identifiers. Existing same-warehouse locations retain status; cross-warehouse code reuse blocks the whole operation. Location codes are globally unique case-insensitively and immutable. Receipt input matching uses the confirmed canonical location code before roll/bin duplicate checks; case variations cannot bypass inactive-location validation, and ambiguous legacy case aliases block receiving. Whole-warehouse rename checks a complete group digest derived from the same list snapshot, including hidden locations and groups larger than one import batch, under the existing factory lock. Batch root/child request identities and frozen operation results in existing change evidence protect retries; no new schema, empty-warehouse entity, carton deletion rules or maintenance grants are introduced. Names/status changes retain original receipt snapshots. Standard unit code/name cross-overlap is rejected. Conversion configuration needs a confirmed material, distinct original and price units, positive ratio, evidence, applicable conditions and effective date; it is stored only and does not convert receipts, stock or prices. New receipts snapshot matching confirmed references; known inactive material/supplier/unit/location blocks new matching receipts. Unmatched material names/units are not falsely snapshotted as confirmed master data. Historical receipts and batches retain their original snapshots.

The user's four fabric workbooks establish that the warehouse handles fabric, accessories and thread, with demand tracking, split actual receipts, departmental/processing issues, dye-lot and individual-roll records, returnable thread-spool custody, and daily movements rolled into monthly stock. `docs/business/fabric-workbook-analysis.md` records exact source sheets/cells and proposed framework additions. Issue block dates, material arrival dates and settlement periods can differ; multiple production demands can share one physical issue. Demand allocation and physical batches need separate identities. The release workbook currently concatenates quantity into lookup keys; this is legacy evidence, not an acceptable stable source identity. Its receipt view overlaps the purchase actual-receipt ledger. The monthly file includes several stock versions, earlier-month summaries, a stale pivot cache, a manually overridden balance and missing prices evaluated as zero amounts. None establishes approved opening stock or valuation. Effective worksheet/version, cutoff, stock-allocation and material-readiness meaning, unit conversions, price source and the scope of roll labels remain to be confirmed before importing or posting; no new operational capability is implied by this analysis.

Without a stable upstream purchase-line ID, candidate identity includes order, supplier, production, plan, material code/name, unit and contract; quantity, price, sheet and row number never identify a line. Initial multi-line groups retain separate warehouse IDs, and identical re-import/reordering preserves them. Changed ambiguous multi-line groups, mixed ID/no-ID natural groups and competing aliases targeting one warehouse line are blocked rather than guessed or merged. Single-line updates show before/after evidence; omitted file rows are never deleted. Factory locks, revision-bound previews and actor/file/scope-bound request IDs prevent stale writes and duplicate retries, including unknown HTTP outcomes. Reserved authenticated `/sources/preview` and `/sources/apply` share this ingestion contract and require stable Kingdee IDs. First linking to a document source needs an explicit warehouse line ID/current revision and matching identity; no Kingdee connector, credentials or automatic fetching is configured.

Operational delivery should start with fabric receiving/issuing before connecting semi-finished processing handoffs. In the described Huakang C flow purchasing has already placed orders; the warehouse receives purchase/delivery information rather than inheriting carton warehouse-owned purchasing. Both purchase-data template import and direct upstream data integration are required in the first operational version, alongside supplier delivery-note import/manual entry, unified pending receipts, actual quantity/quality/source recording, pending issues, partial actual issues, inventory, returns, stocktaking and corrections. Workshops/outsourcing submit requisitions through their modules when available; otherwise warehouse staff issue from manually entered requests or handwritten/printed source documents. Upstream system ownership, payloads and stable source identifiers still need validation. Whether outsourced processing is an engineering-origin instruction to execute or a separately created processing order passed to outsourcing remains explicitly unresolved; do not infer that the warehouse selects factories, prices or processing terms.

Normal and exceptional work must both be recordable: the user wants quick standalone intake/receiving and traceable special handling when the normal upstream flow is unavailable, so people do not resume separate document ledgers. Fabric storage receives purchasing's orders rather than placing its own orders. Department-specific summaries should derive from shared source transactions rather than be re-entered as independent totals. The all-department research proposes linked handovers retaining issued quantity, recipient actual quantity and differences separately; quick entries with missing order links or prices would remain visible for completion, and later linking must not post stock a second time. These are research proposals, not implemented or fully approved exception policies. Exact minimum fields, temporary-master governance, missing-price treatment, exception approval/follow-up rules and cutover accounting remain to be established. Existing carton receipt rules and supplier configuration must not be silently generalized or changed for this new domain.

Interview boundaries: engineering supplies BOM and process evidence; Sales confirms and releases demand; purchasing buys materials; the material warehouse counts and issues them under prior instructions, with QC handling quality. Engineering needs versioned process-document distribution, change reminders and recipient confirmation; purchasing needs shared-material aggregation with retained order allocations and separate planned/promised/actual arrival facts. Cutting records actual part-level output and distribution separately from planned order quantities. Outsourcing coordinates assigned factories, material/process-document handoff and returns; one production order may split across factories with distinct prices/tax terms. The all-department research reports that ordinary sewing subcontract dispatches mainly use cutting's paper slips and spreadsheets, while Kingdee is used for selected special subcontract processes; the Kingdee manuals must not be treated as proof that all physical flows already follow that chain. The semi-finished warehouse receives processed output from subcontract sewing/internal workshops/handwork, not directly from cutting; secondary handwork/stuffing may issue and return before packaging. Sewn toys remain semi-finished until packaging, then enter finished storage. A separate cut-piece warehouse is planned, not already operating. MA/order/release/production-number relationships, BOM style composition, loss basis and unit conversions, quality release, custody/quantity treatment of returns and rework, packaging handoff and reliable opening balances still need source-document validation. Existing spreadsheets and interview reports are evidence, not proof of accurate stock or universally applicable loss percentages.

The warehouse-supervisor meeting AI summary records a barcode/QR-first receiving direction instead of relying on AI recognition of supplier paperwork. Source/order data should prefill receiving, while warehouse staff verify actual physical quantities and retain differences before confirmation; structured document import and manual entry remain transitional paths when upstream modules are unavailable. The summary calls for both warehouse-entered issues and downstream-requested issues, traceable operations, nonconflicting location codes and splitting a batch across bins. Phones are to be evaluated before dedicated scanner purchases. Label granularity (order versus delivery/package/roll/bundle), label issuer, actual quantity binding, duplicate-scan/reprint handling, unit conversion and the scope of location-code uniqueness remain unconfirmed; the claimed 99%+ accuracy and lower cost have no measured evidence. The meeting's eight-screen sketch does not replace the current warehouse navigation contract. Its rollout reference to 华新 is not yet mapped to the repository's 华兴 factory identity; group visibility requires explicit cross-factory authorization rather than blanket access.

The meeting requests repeated semi-finished processing/return cycles, separate 皮料、充棉、下发车间 planned/delivered/outstanding progress, and a dedicated defective-return → repair dispatch → repaired-return trail tied to original business records, including across months, without reversing all historical issues. This is a target workflow, not an implemented stock or settlement rule; processing-state identities, QC release, responsibility, period handling and whether rework changes payable/delivery progress require worked examples. The research proposes acceptance through daily normal/exception use, traceable order and stock progress, and a complete month-end plus next-month carryover, followed by retiring the corresponding duplicate manual ledgers. The meeting's statement that warehouse functionality is largely complete must not be generalized to the fabric/semi-finished modules or barcode/rework capabilities; implementation maturity remains as described above.

### Huakang C Cutting Production — Master Data Foundation

The confirmed cutting-module scope is Huakang C's own Customer Order Center intake and dispatch, engineering-provided production BOM, purchasing of fabric/accessories with material-date replies, supervisor-maintained daily plans and clerk-entered actual output. The customer-name field (洋行名) has no internal/external classification; internal versus outsourced refers only to cutting execution resources. The ledger replaces 累计交货欠产 with 累计计划差额 and keeps 未完成套数 as a separate column. The confirmed remaining-set formula is order total sets minus completed sets for the same order, retaining the subtraction result rather than clamping it to zero or substituting task targets; calculation implementation remains in P2. Delivery/output progress is measured in complete matching sets, with the underlying part composition supplied by BOM; the usual preparation interval before first supply is three working days. Scheduling must support a configurable subset of materials being ready, without requiring all accessories to arrive. Current-stage required materials and feasible batch quantities are distinguished from outstanding later-stage accessories; partial cutting readiness does not establish complete-set output. Execution includes internal cutting and outsourced cutting only, not sewing. The six required workspaces are scheduling, daily reporting, material inventory, monthly closing, master data and actual production material costs. Monthly closing includes both internal production/inventory/material-cost reconciliation and subcontractor processing-fee reconciliation, payable amounts and settlement documents. UI and workflow should follow the spray workspace's design approach, adapted to cutting rather than inheriting spray payroll or customer-receivable rules.

The specification and phased delivery boundary are in `docs/business/huakang-c-cutting-module-plan.md`. P1a provides the Huakang-C-only production portal card and authenticated six-section workspace at `/modules/production/cutting`, defaulting to `/planning`; direct entry and same-parent query changes require a single explicit `huakang-c`. P1b connects the master section to `/api/cutting-operations` with separately granted `cutting_ops:read`, `master_write`, `bom_write`, `bom_publish`. Canonical checks enforce factory/action and department (including department-sensitive position grants and explicit denies): production maintains material references and internal/outsourced cutting resources; engineering saves and publishes structured production BOMs; either can be granted read. Fixed positions do not automatically gain these permissions. Revisions and operation receipts commit atomically under a factory transaction lock, with expected-version conflict detection and actor/payload-bound idempotency. BOMs pin material versions, part quantities per set and per-set material requirements, stage/part applicability and a required-for-cutting flag; revised BOMs become new drafts while published history and original material names remain intact. Publication requires material evidence for every part, at least one required cutting material and currently active referenced materials. These are master references, not a duplicate global material master or stock ledger. No automatic stock, set-output, cost or date calculations exist. P1b defaults off (`CUTTING_OPS_ENABLED=false`); explicit migration `20261007_0131` adds masters/revisions/commands; no-op merge revision `20261009_0149` joins it with main `20261009_0148` without rewriting either history. Startup always excludes these tables from create_all. Existing business databases require verified backup/migration state before activation; no blind stamping, auto-migration or automatic grants. The other five sections remain informational. P1c order dispatch/receipt and procurement dates, BOM attachments/upstream integration, calendars and three-day boundary, inventory, costs and closing remain unimplemented. Tests use isolated SQLite; business-data and PostgreSQL concurrency acceptance remain pending. Huakang C's empty customer-import configuration requires confirmed customer mappings, without moving Huakang D history wholesale. Complete-set composition, completion versus handover/acceptance metrics, upstream/warehouse ownership, valuation/WIP, subcontract prices/taxes/acceptance, approvals/periods and historical cutover remain subject to confirmation. Fabric warehouse and cutting views must share authoritative movements; parts, sets, handovers and subcontract acceptance remain separate facts.

P1a/P1b acceptance remains open despite passing baseline regressions. Master forms and state-change reasons have discard confirmation for category/editor changes, cancellation and route exit. In-flight or uncertain saves block business navigation and preserve the original retry command; once an attempt is uncertain, subsequent retry rejection (including 401/403/409) cannot discard it. A 401 hides business forms and offers reauthentication in a new tab before retrying. Browser refresh/close raises beforeunload protection, but confirmed forced unload or a browser crash still loses component-local drafts and retries; business payloads are not persisted in browser storage. Material search accepts only the latest request in the current editor/query context, ignoring stale success and failure responses and clearing obsolete selections. Real login/IAM grant and revocation integration and target-business-database migration acceptance remain outstanding; isolated SQLite permission tests substitute the current-user dependency and do not establish full session authorization acceptance. Published BOM history exists, but effective-version selection and withdrawal rules must be settled before order integration.

### Shared Tool Center

The authenticated `/tools?factory=<factory-id>` route and the `公共工具栏` entry in the TOOLS sidebar provide ten asynchronous document operations: Word/PDF/Excel conversions, vector-preserving PDF splitting/cropping, and Word/PDF/Excel Chinese–English translation. Every active authenticated user may use the tools; sources, jobs, artifacts and corrections are owner-scoped. Factory context is metadata and does not grant access to another user’s files. The retired synchronous conversion endpoints remain absent; Huaxing PDF batch renaming is the explicit request-time exception described below.

The same toolbar opens `/image-translation/`, a separately built React UI backed by existing owner-scoped server document jobs. `image_translate` accepts PNG/JPEG/WebP/PDF and defaults to English→Chinese. Native PDF words retain measured coordinates; scanned content and large embedded images use Shinobu detection/OCR in a private Node 24 child. The rr-only document compositor replaces the manga bubble/erase/typeset stages: it protects numeric/model/color-code pixels before translation, translates readable colored captions using measured foreground colors, splits mixed OCR (including uppercase captions with numbers) only at observed gaps with verified re-recognition, bounds glyph cleanup against neighboring content and measures colored ink from the source box so mixed red/black averages do not leave red ghosts. Multiword colored captions on a light flat background also clear complete bounded neutral letter components, with limited horizontal padding for narrow multiword OCR boxes; dark-piece cleanup remains color-constrained so its outlines survive, preserves rules/artwork and retains uncertain or nonfitting text for review. OCR row metrics are separated at observed gaps; remaining conflicts can form a bounded paragraph of at most six neutral, high-confidence rows in the same column on a plain caption background, without crossing protected content. Duplicate caption readings favor a complete, equally confident reading, and ordinary headers can be re-recognized across dark colored table rules. Numbered title fragments merge only after independent recognition agrees on all letters, numeric token boundaries and protected tokens, with both readings meeting the normal 0.90 OCR floor; spelling-changing retries retain their stricter confidence requirement. Artwork edge components are protected before straight-rule detachment, including curved swatches entering caption padding. Cleanup removes a bounded two-to-four-pixel antialias surround, with protected artwork/rules restored, and constrained paragraphs wrap inside their original area. The UI offers zoom and per-region source/result/status localization. Its server-only stylesheet follows the Nexus light palette, compact controls, bordered workspace, queue selection and status styling; the standalone Shinobu stylesheet is not imported. The header returns to the shared tool center with factory context, and queue/preview/settings stack on smaller screens. Desktop settings use compact label/control rows so ordinary settings and the start action fit without panel scrolling; optional glossary and original-content protection details are collapsed by default. The rr Node CPU sessions use half the available logical CPUs, clamped to 1–4 intra-op threads and one inter-op thread; standalone Node defaults remain unchanged. The current four-CPU production host uses two intra-op threads per image worker; concurrent real-file throughput still requires load acceptance. Python uses the shared offline/online translator; a scoped manufacturing glossary improves offline labels, technical view captions, plush-pattern parts/materials and textile/embroidery terminology (including fleece, felt, satin stitch, French knot, thread mix and debossed). Exact-letter whitespace normalization and observed punctuation wrappers match complete known captions without guessing missing letters. Online English-to-Chinese image translation supplies manufacturing/textile/embroidery context and default craft terminology; a supplied user glossary replaces those defaults, and the provider controls its translation results. Offline long-sentence semantics remain unreliable and online service is not configured locally. Pattern-caption recovery additionally detects chroma strokes and touching serif letters, retries uncertain ordinary captions, unread colored strokes or one-character near-matches to Tail/Tricot/Plush/Guide in bounded short-caption forms (maximum 96). It never substitutes guessed letters: accepted retries require independent high-confidence recognition with unchanged protected tokens. Cut instructions and inline material codes are split at measured word gaps using original and bounded padded OCR crops; unverified mixed-code lines remain protected. High-confidence word corrections also cover verified color/BR/Tricot captions with unchanged color and code. Every accepted word split checks exact numeric/fraction token boundaries. Complete-letter whitespace normalization of Seam Allowance and known quantity-caption boundaries informs candidate counts without changing letters or values. Caption corrections require high-confidence word readings with unchanged quantities and protected codes; separator and known direction-arrow pixels are preserved, and known brand logos remain original. PDF inputs retain original page objects and text under a lossless difference overlay. The overlay includes three raster pixels of opaque edge padding and retains result RGB behind transparent pixels to prevent English glyph outlines or dark fringes at other zoom levels/renderers; padding excludes protected numeric words and horizontal/vertical rules, with a narrower guard for rules near descenders. Translated Chinese is graphical, underlying English remains extractable, and this is not redaction. Image inputs export lossless raster PDFs. Region patches create immutable revisions by merging only successfully retranslated areas into the parent, retaining its other pixels and review entries; the queue replaces the old version for ordered PDF aggregation through `/packages` (request body `format=pdf`). There is no DB migration. Users need neither browser model imports nor API keys; verified private weights remain on the administrator-installed read-only model volume and never enter web builds. Ordinary HTTP works without OPFS/WebGPU; standalone Shinobu browser mode remains separate and browser history is not migrated. Cancellation/lease loss reaps Node while waiting for translation; a running model/HTTP call finishes separately and late results are discarded. The image working-copy limit defaults to 24M pixels: uploads above it or a 12000-pixel edge are automatically resized during inspection and translation to at most a 4096-pixel edge and the configured pixel limit, with EXIF/alpha handling, retained original bytes, consistent preview/patch/output coordinates and a resolved resize notice. Decoding is still capped at 80M pixels and a 65535-pixel edge, and the upload byte limit remains enforced before resizing. PDF originals are not compressed by this feature. Default PDF raster and processing limits remain 80M total pixels and 1800 seconds; native PDF rendering can delay cancellation until a single render returns. Real-file regression uses the supplied 18-page specification PDF and 5100×3300 DECO artwork, with private inputs/results excluded from Git. Functional/layout verification does not establish semantic accuracy. A text-only comparison using the configured production qwen3-vl-plus provider verified improved craft terminology with domain context on two supplied product images, but exposed wrong interpretations of OCR fragments and ambiguous product names; this is not general semantic acceptance. Missing letters, stylized artwork text, protected mixed-value lines and ambiguous names still require review. Production employee sessions and load acceptance remain unverified; current production releases are tracked in the deployment section. Build/install/operating details are in `docs/document-tools/image-translation.md`.

The separate `协同填表` workspace at `/tools?factory=<id>&tool=collaborative-sheets` adds persistent Excel collection through `/api/tools/collaborative-sheets`. Only active, confirmed employees in their home factory may participate; this deliberately differs from the conversion tools' factory-as-metadata contract. Uploads begin as private drafts; owners assign existing same-factory accounts or departments to sheet/cell ranges, publish, close/reopen and download. Department membership is revalidated on access, participants see the whole authorized workbook but may edit only assigned ranges, and owners may edit the union of assigned ranges. Formulas and merged-cell interiors remain locked. Document revision compare-and-swap prevents stale overwrites; any document revision invalidates earlier completion confirmations. Audit evidence is append-only. Browser drafts survive conflict refresh and guard route/factory changes before the factory store changes.

The local collaborative workspace uses the Univer OSS 1.0.3 canvas by default; the original editor remains an optional fallback for image layout or loading issues. Only the active sheet is lazily mounted from the existing server manifest, and both editors share the workspace scalar draft, range/type validation and existing revision-aware save API. Native XLS/XLSX export remains authoritative; no Univer import/export or paid real-time service is integrated. Structure, style and formula edits are blocked. Formula cells show cached display text. The Univer canvas does not render images in cells; selecting an image anchor exposes an original-image preview and the original editor remains available. The default Univer editor is not deployed to production.

Valid XLSX column-format spans may extend to XFD even when the actual table is small. Such metadata does not count as populated cells or enlarge the browser grid; column widths are expanded only through the actual sheet extent, and full original column formatting is retained in downloads. Actual cells and allocation-driving merge/hyperlink/formula/comment ranges remain subject to the supported grid limits.

Department assignment supports checkbox multi-select, previews the union of selected same-factory accounts and expands one sheet/range into existing per-department grants. Exact duplicate grants are skipped; batches exceeding the 200-grant limit are rejected atomically. Account assignment remains single-select. Collaborative-sheet participant progress resolves the complete current same-factory active/confirmed account and department grant union through the canonical IAM identity resolver, including people without submissions; it never implicitly includes the uploader or crosses factories. Status distinguishes not started, saved but unconfirmed, confirmed at the current revision and stale confirmation. Save evidence aggregates the entire audit history, independently of the 50-event activity feed. The lightweight `/participants` endpoint preserves access checks and returns no workbook; 15-second frontend refreshes never overwrite the editing revision or drafts. The grid supports in-cell double-click/F2/type-to-replace, IME, Enter/Tab/arrows, Escape, Delete, save shortcut and atomic permission-checked TSV paste up to 500 cells. Internal single-cell clipboard data preserves scalar types and multiline text; formulas remain read-only and explicit saving is required.

Collaborative sheets retain immutable source bytes and store scalar cell overrides. Export patches native BIFF8 XLS or XLSX worksheet XML while preserving unrelated records/parts, original styles, merges and embedded content. Browser rendering covers common table formatting and WPS cell images; it is not full Excel rendering or a calculation engine. Formula caches remain unchanged until Excel/WPS recalculates the downloaded file; floating XLS drawings are preserved in download but not rendered. Unsupported protected/macro/signed/legacy structures fail explicitly. Uploads accept up to 100 MiB with an immediate client check and a 300-second upload-only timeout; the 160 MiB expanded ZIP and 5000-entry budgets are unchanged. This upload increase requires a frontend/backend production release. Limits are 100 MiB, 50 worksheets, 100,000 displayed cells, 5,000 rows and 256 columns, with allocation-driving ranges checked before spreadsheet loaders. The legacy `document_tools_enabled` gate and document storage apply. Migration `20261009_0121` retains its original `20261005_0120` parent; additive merge revision `20261009_0148` joins it with the published `20261008_0147` head without rewriting either history. Existing databases require an explicit backed-up Alembic upgrade before starting the new backend. This feature is deployed in the pinned production release described in section 7. Usage and constraints are in `docs/business/collaborative-sheets.md`.

Huaxing alone also exposes `/tools?factory=huaxing&tool=pdf-batch-rename` within the new tool center. Gate on the actual active factory, never the group-to-Huaxing production-factory fallback; changing factory clears the rename workspace and cancels pending requests, while switching tools preserves the separate conversion workspace. The authenticated `/api/tools/pdf-rename/{rules,preview,execute}` router returns no rules for other production factories and rejects their processing requests; group/unknown contexts are invalid. This is business-rule applicability, not additional user-role authorization. BuzzBee inspection reads Item No./Description and S/C to generate `#item-PO+PO.pdf`, preserving PO order and leading zeros. Caixing reads Batch no., the first five digits of Item number, P/O no., Quantity (Pcs), and DATE from the report body to generate `report-#item-PO-quantity-Y.M.D.pdf`; old filenames are not authoritative. BuzzBee inspection accepts complete, strictly validated identifiers without a blanket OCR review gate; local malformed PO reads retry the same region with RapidOCR, preserve complete PO entries and their order across all continuation lines, and never repair digits or separators. Caixing local OCR retains its review gate, while complete validated Qwen fields are ready to download. The BuzzBee印尼发票 rule reads the first-page red six-digit invoice number and generates `客发票--number.pdf` (for example `客发票--104753.pdf`), preserving leading zeros. It validates BUZZ BEE TOYS / INVOICE in the header, isolates visible red ink before either local or cloud OCR, excludes the black A prefix and ignores the scan's hidden text and original filename. Local invoice readings require review; complete validated cloud readings are ready. Missing red ink, malformed numbers and duplicate target names remain blocked. Manual filenames can release recognition exceptions after per-file and batch confirmation, but cannot bypass invalid PDFs, unsafe/duplicate names, or stale preview tokens. Renaming packages unchanged source PDF bytes without persistent jobs/storage and does not depend on the conversion worker or LibreOffice. When DOCUMENT_TOOLS_AI_MODE is not off and the existing document-tools Qwen key/endpoint are configured, all active rename rules use that connection and its layout model for labelled transcription of fixed first-page regions. Strict JSON, required field labels, report markers, identifier/date/quantity formats and ambiguity checks remain authoritative; provider errors are visible and do not silently fall back. The browser shows the configured mode and per-field actual recognition source. Unconfigured environments use local OCR. A bounded per-process 15-minute text cache reuses successful cloud readings for execute; explicit new previews invalidate source readings. Cache expiry, eviction, process restart or a different API worker can require re-recognition, and changed target names remain blocked by the preview token. PDF/image bytes and credentials are not cached or logged. Limits are 50 files, 20 MiB each and 200 MiB per batch; the rename-only production proxy location allows 205 MiB multipart requests and 900-second processing waits. The legacy `document_tools_enabled` gate still applies.

Document-tool batch, upload, conversion and package IDs use the shared `createRandomUuid` helper, whose secure `getRandomValues` fallback supports the production HTTP origin where `crypto.randomUUID` is unavailable. Localhost testing alone does not verify that deployment constraint.

Document task withdrawal atomically marks queued/running/awaiting-input jobs cancelled and revokes their lease; an offline or stale worker cannot publish afterward. Retry creates a new job. Owner-only deletion is logical removal from recent/my tasks, persisted as the reserved `options_json._deleted_at` tombstone, and also withdraws unfinished work. Deleted tasks cannot be reopened or retried through public job endpoints; repeated deletion is idempotent. Original files, artifacts, sources and correction provenance remain available to the owner and dependent revisions/packages. Deletion is not file erasure; no schema migration or source cleanup is required. The UI confirms removal and exposes both actions in recent tasks, the current document header and my tasks.

Migration `20260908_0103_docs` owns four persistent document-tool tables; merge revision `20260908_0105` joins it with the published carton `20260908_0104` branch without rewriting carton migration history. API and independent `app.workers.document_tools` share document storage; PostgreSQL workers claim with short SKIP LOCKED transactions, while SQLite has one locked worker. Lease tokens fence progress/final publication; retries create isolated attempt files and revisions retain prior outputs and correction provenance. Native Office extraction preserves authoritative content; LibreOffice with an isolated profile and UNO helper produces previews/print output. PDF.js uses normalized visible-page top-left point coordinates matching PDF geometry. Cropping changes the visible box and must not be advertised as secure redaction. Qwen OCR/layout use an explicit region/workspace-matched backend URL and secret; AI and obscured/ambiguous fields remain review candidates, never substitutes for reliable native/manual values. Tested-engine status is configuration-bound, not a generic accuracy guarantee. The current synthetic benchmark covers native, scan, geometry and Office print cases; complex layouts and source print truncation retain explicit review issues. Source/artifact files have no default expiry; configured expiry is enforced by content, download and structure reads, and no automatic source cleanup is enabled.

`backend/app/services/document_translation.py`, its model installer and `document_translation_*` settings are shared by quote translation and the document tool worker. Document translation uses `word_translate`, `excel_translate`, `pdf_translate`, with explicit offline/online selection and `zh_to_en`/`en_to_zh` direction. Office packages preserve formulas and formatting, translating selectable Excel sheets (empty selection means all, including hidden sheets); DOC/XLS normalization and PDF translation output require LibreOffice. PDF translation rebuilds a PDF plus editable DOCX and retains layout/OCR review notices. Translation results are read-only in the browser; download to edit or start a new translation. Offline mode also forces local PDF OCR. Online mode reuses the server Qwen connection with `DOCUMENT_TOOLS_TRANSLATION_MODEL` (default `qwen3-vl-plus`), sends text/terminology and optionally scanned PDF regions, and never silently falls back after translation failure. Strict output cardinality, nonempty text and numeric/identifier checks block detected changes but do not guarantee semantic accuracy. The shared online engine batches at most 12 text regions and normally 2400 characters (an intact single region may use the existing 6000-character limit). Incomplete, malformed or mismatched responses trigger ordered batch bisection, with at most one retry for a single region; completed batches are not resent during that recovery. Only complete JSON, optionally inside a complete code fence, is accepted. Network/service/refusal failures do not fan out into retries, and cancellation, numeric/code validation and all-or-nothing artifact publication remain enforced. API and worker require the same configuration and restart; configuration readiness is not live-provider verification. Quote translation remains offline. `document_tools_enabled` remains the shared legacy gate. The API/IR contract and runbook live under `docs/document-tools/`; real keys belong only in ignored backend environment configuration.

### Carton Mark and Indonesia Invoice

Carton-mark now has a factory-scoped persistent template library. The carton/PMC-warehouse upload path atomically stores the customer Excel, print-ready PDF, SHA-256 identities, versioned customer/PO/ITEM/contract metadata and the complete request-time Excel-to-PDF text-check result; duplicate document pairs are rejected and every check outcome is retained. A template is QC-ready only when its automatic result is `核对通过` or an active audited manual release exists. Manual release never rewrites the automatic result: only carton supervisors/managers or system administrators with `carton_mark:template_release` may release within the effective factory and carton/warehouse scope, a 5–500 character reason plus reviewer/time/source-status evidence is mandatory, and any later automatic recheck revokes the prior release. Carton-mark customer names are maintained in their own factory-scoped master and are never imported from the internal-quote customer list; existing template customer names were backfilled at migration, internal warehouse uploads must select a managed name, and only carton supervisors/managers or higher accounts with `carton_mark:customer_manage` may create, rename or remove options. The warehouse content-check form identifies a customer from current non-cancelled, non-deleted carton orders in its effective factory, using exact normalized contract and optional ITEM/PO filtering. Server-side source validation retains each Excel/PDF original's explicit order boundary, including deleted bindings, and reports conflicting contracts. Automatic selection requires one customer code and one normalized name; multiple codes or names require an explicit matching order choice. The UI shows order evidence, retains a manually chosen different customer only after confirmation, and clears prior automatic selections when metadata or fresh evidence changes. A recognized name missing from the separate carton-mark master remains a candidate; accounts with carton_mark:customer_manage may explicitly add it or preview and select up to 200 names from this factory's current carton orders. Initialization skips existing names, blocks ambiguous code/name identities, revalidates the current order names on write, commits atomically with actor audit, and never draws from internal-quote customers. Ordinary warehouse uploaders may recognize/select but cannot initialize names; QC does not get the order-recognition or initialization controls. Customer-name extraction from Excel/PDF text is a future extension; current identification uses order evidence. The same factory customer master is read-only in the QC carton workspace, including customers that do not yet have a QC-ready template; removing or renaming an option does not rewrite historical template names. The fixed carton manager position may operate carton-mark management, carton-procurement collaboration and QC carton verification across factories. Carton supervisors remain own-factory operators but may also perform every QC carton verification action in their home factory; carton warehouse keepers remain limited to the own-factory source-template workflow. Carton/PMC-warehouse roles may create and soft-archive records, while authorized carton/PMC-warehouse/legacy-QA/QC roles may list, inspect and download the original documents within their effective factory scope. Source Excel/PDF and QC front/side photos accept picker or drag-and-drop input; each selected QC photo may be rotated or cropped before request-time comparison. QC also offers separate front/side capture inputs using image/* and capture=environment, alongside the unchanged multi-file gallery selectors. Capturing appends to the selected side, preserves the other side and prior edited files, and does not upload until explicit verification; cancellation leaves the selection intact. Eligible warehouse template records link to QC with the exact factory/template ID; QC validates readiness against the current list, supports direct card selection and read-only refresh, preserves a later manual choice and its photos across refresh, and clears selections when a template becomes ineligible or the factory changes. Authorized QC readers can return to the warehouse content-check view. Camera behavior on actual phones and production activation of these workflow controls remain unverified. Creation, manual release, recheck and archive operations write metadata-only carton audit events, and archived records are removed from normal detail/download surfaces. QC's later print-PDF-to-site-photo comparison remains the existing permission-protected request-time PDF/OCR/photo service.

Carton-mark's warehouse workspace also has an independent factory-scoped source-file repository (`CartonMarkAsset`, initial local migration `20261005_0132`, published `20261005_0135`) alongside the checked Excel/PDF template library. Warehouse uploads may store PDF, Excel and static JPG/JPEG, PNG or WebP photos individually without mandatory customer, item or contract metadata; a warehouse batch accepts up to 50 files, 20 MB each and 100 MB total, retains successful files when another file fails, allows warehouse uploads with identical filenames or identical bytes as independent new copies, each with its own ID, binding, revision and creation audit. Uploading again never overwrites or restores an archived original. Original bytes live on the backend, with metadata-only lists and audited creation, binding and archive. A nonunique factory/SHA-256 index supports lookup; hash equality does not establish file identity. Since intentional repeats create copies, an interrupted upload must be checked in the list before retrying. Photos keep their original bytes and content type, are decoded and validated against the extension, and allow at most 40 million pixels; corrupt, mislabeled or animated images fail individually. Photos recognize only the filename contract, including numbered 正唛/侧唛/照片 suffixes and complete known contract tokens beside labels; image pixels and EXIF never provide order identity. Recognition for documents uses a contract-like filename, explicit contract labels and exact known local order contract tokens; conflicting candidates remain unbound, and PO/item/barcode substrings are not fallback contract identities. Optional contract associations are resolved against current non-cancelled, non-deleted orders in the same factory, so later orders with that contract automatically reuse the files. Contracts spanning different customer codes require an explicit order selection. Manual association and archive use optimistic revision checks; explicit order selection has factory-enforced foreign keys, and deleting its order preserves the original file without broadening the selection to another order. Internal carton order details display the associated source files read-only. Existing active template originals are backfilled once into this repository without altering their check results or release evidence; identical originals previously used for different contracts remain unbound. Saving a source file does not grant QC approval. The primary warehouse entry is carton order management: 箱唛资料上传 beside 新建纸箱订单 in the ledger header supports batch upload, and 箱唛资料库 is an order-management subpage. Supplier accounts use the right-header 箱唛资料库 entry; without an explicit factory query it defaults to 全部厂区, aggregating only current authorized service memberships through individually factory-scoped supplier API requests. A valid explicit factory query and the dropdown retain single-factory filtering; each aggregated file displays its factory and uses that factory for preview/download. Membership refresh removes revoked scopes, scope changes discard pending old results, and any failed factory request shows an error rather than a complete-looking partial aggregate. Both sides use the same keyword/customer/format-filtered source list, with internal precise binding states and upload-time/contract/customer sorting. Files normally group by factory, contract and customer identity; same-contract PDF/Excel/photo originals remain separate files with unchanged IDs and download scopes. Warehouse users can upload unnamed photos, search current non-cancelled/non-deleted local order metadata and manually select one order, enter a contract, or explicitly select up to 100 same-factory orders for multiple contracts of the same customer. Multi-contract bindings use carton_mark_asset_order_bindings with composite asset/factory and order/factory foreign keys; the original bytes and file ID remain single-copy. The manual_orders mode remains explicit even after every target is deleted, never reverting to automatic contract sharing or enrolling future orders. Soft-deleted/cancelled targets can be explicitly removed in the binding editor; a saved Excel/PDF pair with disjoint known order associations is rejected instead of relying only on the surrounding contract group. Binding, group binding and archive serialize under the factory business lock with revision checks; repeated saves retain unchanged relationship rows and audit the original selection. Photo-group merge forms retain the union of previous explicit targets for operator review; grouping never expands supplier access. The binding-order lookup requires factory-scoped carton-mark read plus upload permissions and exposes only order/contract/customer/item identities. Explicit photo groups contain up to 50 original images in one factory, retain per-file IDs/bytes, and can be saved unbound, merged, bound as a whole, previewed together or dissolved without losing the current associations. Group create/merge requires every active member of selected existing groups; every group change checks each member revision and commits or rolls back the whole operation including audit. Single-file binding cannot split a group; archiving a member clears its group ID; a later warehouse upload creates a separate ungrouped copy and never rejoins the archived original to a changed group. Internal filtered selection includes hidden siblings with a visible warning; supplier galleries include only individually authorized visible members in the same factory, and grouping never expands document access. Operation buttons occupy stable aligned columns, including an empty preview slot for Excel. PDF/photo preview and original downloads remain authenticated. The retired internal supplier-management page no longer offers generic per-order attachment uploads; legacy attachment evidence remains intact. Choosing stored Excel/PDF sources for the original template workflow reuses their server IDs and bytes, requires current factory read plus upload permissions, and still executes the original content check and release gate. The warehouse contract-group chooser presents all Excel/PDF candidates for that same factory/contract/customer group, including list-filtered files; it requires an explicit PDF/Excel pair, leaves multiple versions unselected for operator choice, excludes photos and carries both sources into the content-check form without submitting it. Existing individual-file selection remains available. Factory changes, manual file replacement, reset and submission invalidate pending source selections; concurrent Excel/PDF selection preserves both files and takes suggested metadata from the latest request. Photos are view/download sources and cannot enter the original Excel/PDF content-check or QC approval flow. Release-lineage forward migration `20261006_0138` (local applied `20261006_0136`) extends only the asset-kind CHECK constraint after published audience head `20261006_0137`; startup refuses the old constraint and downgrade refuses any stored photo, including archived records. Release-lineage forward migration `20261006_0139` (local applied `20261006_0137`) adds the nullable photo-group ID, image-only group CHECK and factory/group index after the photo-kind migration; startup also refuses a missing group field and downgrade refuses any retained group ID. Photo and group support are enabled locally and in production at published head `20261006_0139`, after verified backup and restored-PostgreSQL migration/startup rehearsal. The primary development SQLite retains its distinct already-applied local alias chain and verified backups; do not interpret absent published aliases as migrations to replay. Canonical forward migration `20261008_0146` follows immutable published head `20261008_0145`, adds customer ownership and removes source-hash uniqueness while preserving originals and existing assignments. It backfills an owner only for an existing singleton assignment; historical multi-person assignments retain their scope until an authorized manager designates an owner. Production activation and preservation evidence must be verified against the actual deployment. The supplier source-library API shares individual original PDF/Excel/photo files without requiring a paired template or QC release. Visibility requires the selected authorized service factory, current supplier ownership and a non-cancelled/non-deleted supplier-visible ordinary issued purchase snapshot; association uses its exact normalized contract, retaining full-factory customer ambiguity checks and explicit single/multiple-order boundaries. Multi-contract supplier responses contain only currently eligible own-order links and derive displayed contracts from those links; every download and check revalidates them, so removing the last eligible link revokes access without deleting original bytes. Supplier responses include only associated own-order customer/contract/PO/item metadata, and every preview/download rechecks visibility. Unbound, ambiguous, unissued, archived or other-supplier files remain hidden. The supplier carton-mark workspace allows canonical supplier read plus edit accounts to batch-upload Excel, PDF and static images together (50 files, 20 MB each, 100 MB total) into one explicitly selected authorized service factory. Contract recognition reuses the original filename/document parser and matches only current own ordinary issued snapshots. A unique own order is required for automatic association; an explicit own order/issue selection handles unnamed files or same-contract order collisions, while conflicting recognized contracts are rejected. Upload-order choices expose the issued purchase document number and business date to distinguish otherwise identical contracts/items. Selecting an upload order previews its customer, contract, customer PO and ITEM; every successfully stored/skipped file reports its actual authorized order binding, and supplier source-library badges describe purchase-order association rather than implying warehouse origin. Both upload binding previews and the check form show a missing customer PO explicitly rather than copying the contract number. Supplier mark batch uploads and both new-PDF and stored-pair checks explicitly use multipart forms despite the shared HTTP client’s JSON default; even a stored pair with no new binary file must retain the factory/order/issue/source/revision fields as form fields. Unmatched, ambiguous, invalid or conflicting files return individual failure messages and are retained in the client for correction/retry; valid files commit together to the existing warehouse source repository, each explicitly bound to its own order. Current session, canonical read/edit, service membership and issued order identities are revalidated under the factory business lock after recognition and before persistence. Creation audit retains supplier/order/issue provenance. Supplier factory/content-hash duplicates are reused only when an active copy is currently visible for that same chosen order; when several warehouse copies share a hash, every candidate is checked for that exact authorized association; inaccessible or archived originals are neither disclosed, rebound nor restored by supplier upload. Suppliers can also select an individually authorized Excel and PDF together when their authorized order associations overlap, including a multi-contract Excel with a PDF for one selected contract, with an explicit version choice when several files exist, or select Excel and upload one new print PDF (20 MB maximum) for the existing text check. Both library sources must authorize the same selected own order and current issue. If one Excel covers multiple own issued orders, an explicit order choice is required; the server derives customer, PO, ITEM and contract exclusively from that order’s current ordinary issued snapshot, without granting or populating carton-mark customer-master maintenance. Before parsing and again under the factory business lock before saving, it validates current session/permissions, supplier ownership, order state, issue ID and both selected originals’ revisions/bytes/identities, locking asset rows in deterministic ID order before saving. Every outcome atomically retains the original Excel/PDF pair, versioned result and supplier/order/issue/source provenance in the existing template creation audit. Internal readers and QC reuse that template record and existing readiness gate; supplier approve never grants manual release. The supplier’s own-origin check history and original-document endpoints include failed and review results, expose no internal reviewer/release-reason identities, and revalidate current supplier-owned non-deleted/non-cancelled issued-order identity on every read. Scope, account or authorization changes clear pending selections and stale responses; routine same-account session refresh preserves selected sources. Maintenance of existing source bindings, archive and customer masters remains internal; supplier upload creates only the validated initial single-order association. The supplier upload/check workflow production activation is recorded in the canonical deployment section; subsequent local changes require a separately verified release. The legacy approved complete-template API retains its earlier release and full issued-identity rules separately.

Migration `20260825_0083` persists carton-mark manual-release reason, reviewer, time and source automatic status, and refuses downgrade while approval evidence exists.

Migration `20260826_0084` retires the removed AI subsystem by deleting its operational-alert notifications and dropping all assistant conversation, task, artifact, guard, feedback, metric and action-confirmation tables. Its downgrade is intentionally blocked because deleted assistant data cannot be reconstructed; production requires a verified pre-migration backup.

Indonesia invoice reconciliation compares the supported Faith Jet and RRI PDF inputs for an authenticated user. It is also request-time processing rather than a persisted workflow.

### Injection-Scheduling Center

The current four-factory input template is RR_INJECTION_HUAXING_V2 (huaxing_template.py, templates/injection-plan-huaxing-v2.xlsx). It retains the Huaxing single plan sheet, row-3 A:AX headers, cyan machine bands, hidden helper columns, frozen rows/columns and horizontal day/night quantities. The factory-plan-authorized /templates/plan download offers 5/10/20 task rows per scoped machine (default 10), followed by a pending area, with current shared-mold and factory-machine lookup snapshots in hidden same-sheet columns. An empty factory register blocks download. Machine codes remain text, including meaningful prefixes and leading zeroes; full-width and case variants are normalized for factory-scoped uniqueness. Header dropdown changes refresh machine specifications, status and notes using prefixed text lookup keys, because native Excel COUNTIF otherwise equates 01 with 1. The visible factory must match the download binding and import context. A cleared machine-band label ends the preceding queue and blocks its unassigned tasks rather than inheriting the previous machine. Inserting an entire worksheet row is supported; copy only the A:DJ task region and formulas, not the hidden master columns. It never exports old orders or production or writes master data. Factory F2 and fixed planning origin I2 must match the selected import context. Machine bands and top-to-bottom task order determine persisted PLANNED runs; empty slots are ignored and pending-area rows remain unassigned. Operators do not maintain technical IDs or queue numbers. Stable factory-scoped IDs derive from mold/order/item/color/colorant/material; identical business identities must be a single row, and identity changes or split batches use the system/exchange workflow. Opening day/night shots plus the 31-day grid sum into M and are imported once as opening completed shots, never fabricated reports. Original quantity/weight/amount units remain, while the erroneous old week-number delta no longer delays dates. Excel dates are continuous-production references; the system uses its existing calendar, changeover and occupancy engine. Download-time master lookups do not replace independent machine/shared-mold/physical-asset maintenance. Apply retains transactional revalidation, duplicate/concurrent-change/production safeguards, no silent reassignment, preservation of omitted existing queues and no deletion by omitted Excel rows. Legacy Huaxing, flat RR_INJECTION_UNIFIED_V1 and standard exchange imports remain compatible. There is no schema migration.

Machine-board bulk start selects frozen persisted queue-head identities per machine, supports multi-select/select-all within current machine filters (including below-fold cards), and clears selections when the factory/filter scope changes. A read-only `/execution/start-preview` lists per-machine eligibility and reviewed resource/demand fingerprints; `/execution/bulk-start` requires factory report permission, explicit actual-start confirmation, reviewed factory CAS revision and the existing shared-resource write lock/idempotency receipt. Same-physical-asset selection conflicts require an explicit choice, never an arbitrary first winner. Each start reuses production execution validation within a savepoint; successful heads start at one timestamp, later queued runs remain planned, and the factory is recalculated once. Partial failures remain selected with individual reasons for re-review; changed heads are never silently substituted. Starting records no fabricated output. No migration is added, and no local business batch is started as part of validation. The operator procedure is in `docs/business/injection-scheduling-v3.md`.

The workbench's More menu provides a factory-wide plan-data reset with a read-only impact preview and one `确认清除` button; no reason entry, typed confirmation or extra checkbox is required. It includes imported and manually created demands regardless of filters, selections or pagination. Started/paused/transferred/finished runs or shift reports are explicitly listed as included in that same confirmation and still require both factory plan and report permission; ordinary resets require plan permission. Only the confirmation click submits the preview's factory confirmation text, an audit reason describing the user's clear action, and the preview's execution flag to the existing API. Cancel never writes; a failed/stale preview must be refreshed and confirmed again. The preview revision and affected-content fingerprint are rechecked inside the existing serialized, idempotent write transaction. Deletion of plan/import/history/execution rows and the complete before snapshot in `plan.clear` audit history commit atomically. Shared molds, machine specifications, physical assets, calendars, settings, saved views and other factories survive; only erased jobs' runtime occupancy/setup resets, preserving fault/maintenance/recovery facts. Clearing permits reimporting the same source file and invalidates stale frontend requests, selection and query context. Schedule undo cannot restore a reset, and reimporting cannot recover system-only manual or production records.

Injection Scheduling V3 is implemented and enabled locally from the user's read-only Huaxing 9.1 workbook and Codex specification. The single production card now opens the strict-permission full-page workspace `/modules/production/injection-scheduling?factory=<id>`; its real API is `/api/injection-scheduling`. Four scoped factories are `huaxing`, `huadeng`, `huakang-a` and `huakang-b`; the global factory catalog, including C/D, is unchanged. `/api/injection` still belongs to molding-sample production. Local schema enablement and Huaxing business-data import have been performed; this does not establish a production V3 backend/schema rollout.

The new `injection_v3_` domain separates shared mold masters, physical mold assets, factory machines/calendars, demands, immutable source evidence, planned runs and actual shift reports. Decimal calculations, deterministic compatible-machine scheduling, shared-asset occupancy, explicit oversized manual moves, queue pins and undo are persisted without an approval/publish step. Actual start/pause/resume/transfer/finish and cumulative report replacement drive real progress; FIFO/co-output allocation never duplicates physical shots. Unknown recovery keeps prediction dates unknown while retaining resource occupancy. The Vue/Pinia workbench includes virtualized TanStack tables, server-side whole-scope query/count/summary/export, Gantt, cards, shift entry, master data, source/history drawers and revision-aware five-second refresh that protects drafts.

The current local frontend uses three workspaces (planning, shift reports, master data), with Gantt/cards/full-field table inside planning. The page owns only display state (view, density, pool width, scroll, table layout); account or factory changes clear selected record context and cached display selections, while ordinary sync preserves them. At 1280px and above, one non-modal inspector docks beside the board; narrower screens use the same editable instance as a focus-managed overlay. Opening details automatically reduces the demand pool to a rail below 1600px. Editing and pending requests keep the existing dirty/busy navigation guards. Scoped injection tokens/components/motion styles, explicit Lucide icons and a self-hosted JetBrains Mono font apply only to this module; reduced-motion/hidden-page preferences stop decorative animation. Factory parameters use typed forms with an advanced JSON fallback and preserve untouched unknown keys, null/zero/false and array order. Multi-order run details read each demand through the existing factory-scoped detail endpoint; report feedback retains failed-row drafts. These are presentation changes, not a scheduler, API, permission, database or publication workflow revision.

The supplied real plan workbook was Huaxing: 76 machines, 280 candidate demands, 1,449 numeric shift cells and all 4,739 formula nodes were reconciled against the independent evidence. Original cached N totals and the N360 gap remain source evidence; operational remaining is L minus M. Import reads only the selected plan/exchange sheet, uses explicit identities and three-way field comparison, exposes ambiguous/conflicting rows, and preserves historical quantity baselines. Unchanged or conflict-only reimports preserve saved run times, sequence and revisions; only applied business changes trigger recalculation. Export includes factory-specific plan, stable exchange rows and quantity definitions with window-before totals. Huakang A/B equipment-only source profiles have been verified; imports of their original plan layouts and live PostgreSQL execution remain unverified. Future plans use the common Huaxing-style template. Current editable defaults and actual pending capability assumptions are documented in `docs/business/injection-scheduling-v3.md`.

The user's master-data requirement is a persistent shared mold library and factory equipment register maintained independently of plan imports, with the existing forms unchanged. Huaxing's 76 existing machine records have been reconciled to the separate `华兴机器设备.xls`, sheet `机器设备明细`, rows 4–79: `老车间` maps to existing `旧1`–`旧39` / `旧车间`, and `新车间` to `新1`–`新37`. The six rows in `报废机台` describe replaced historical equipment and must not overwrite current machines occupying those positions. A and clamp tonnage come from explicit source columns; explicit negative core-pull notes set `CORE_PULL=false`. Unspecified capabilities retain existing values, which are not newly verified facts. Brand, equipment/auxiliary model, year, power and original measurements are retained as semicolon-separated text in the existing single-line equipment notes field. Machine IDs, current status/setup and production history remain intact. The legacy Huaxing parser still has a missing-machine auto-create fallback. The four-factory unified-template path never creates machines, shared mold masters or physical assets; it requires pre-existing scoped master data. The independent reviewed equipment registration now holds Huakang A machines 1–55 and Huakang B machines 1–69 plus B1–B27. Source profiles read only machine header rows from the supplied A 8月 and B 计划表; source SHA/row/cells are preserved in raw_header_cells. They do not infer workshop names from prefixes or A/tonnage from order rows, and absent core-pull/heating capabilities remain unset. B14/B16 have no verified tonnage. Existing machine records are never overwritten by this registration. Source-only unspecified machine-family/speed fields retain the existing HORIZONTAL/NORMAL form defaults and are flagged as not source-verified. A machines 1/20 were initialized DISABLED and 19/29/35 FAULT from historical source labels, with status-stop calendars; current availability needs site verification through the existing equipment-status action. Original plan assignment conflicts A51/A117 and B303/B304 are retained for user review, and A229–241 have no machine. No source orders, production or physical mold assets are created by equipment registration.

Huakang A's existing 55 equipment records are now reconciled to the separately supplied `华康A普通机与高速机周期目标产量.xls`, sheet `机台A、吨 2026-4-14` rows 2–56, through an audited local parameter update rather than plan import. A and tonnage use explicit B/D values; 1/2/3/20 are 50/60/55/50T. Source-explicit arm type replaces conflicts, retaining existing axis counts only when the type agrees; blank arms preserve existing values, with 42/43 still unknown. The sheet explicitly identifies 17 high-speed machines, including 35/36; brand names alone do not establish speed, electric drive or machine family. `改螺杆` supplements equipment/model notes but never implies exclusive-resin restrictions or present availability. Its row 7 conflicts with machine 42: the dated equipment sheet and existing record are 18A/200T, while the screw sheet says 32A/BS320-11 and modification in progress. 18A/200T remains pending field verification, with the conflict visible in notes. Existing status/calendars, material restrictions, capabilities, setup, IDs and production remain intact. The 2019 23-hour cycle/output ranges are reference-only and must not become single machine/mold day targets or overwrite scheduling settings. The source evidence retains previous provenance. The existing master-data form and independent registration's no-overwrite behavior are unchanged.

The local shared mold catalog has been rebuilt independently from `华兴啤机日排版表9.1.xlsx` sheets `单价` and `机安`, replacing prior plan-derived editable values while preserving the 197 referenced mold IDs. Its source catalog contains 5,132 exact normalized mold codes, not 5,132 confirmed physical assets. The user explicitly chose `机安` as authoritative for A and equipment requirements when the two sheets disagree; `单价` V is a fallback only when `机安` G is absent. `单价` W supplies daily shots, Z the whole-shot price, and P whole-shot net weight; repeated component rows are never summed, and conflicting nonempty values within one source leave the corresponding default unset. `机安` M engineering weight is not established as gross weight and stays in notes. Explicit source zeroes remain distinct from missing values. Internal-space variants stay separate and are not invented aliases; source row numbers and discrepancies remain traceable. Physical assets, machines, demands, process snapshots and production records are unchanged by this catalog replacement. Source-only master replacement is a local maintenance operation, not a production rollout or a new import UI. Automation status and removable fixtures (suction cups, clamps and tooling) are production-preparation reference data, never scheduling eligibility or grouping constraints. Original assignments, automatic scheduling, manual moves and grouping share this rule, including legacy AUTOMATIC/fixtures capability-list entries; source values remain intact and existing schedules are not automatically rewritten when code updates. Core-pull/heating, machine family/A, mechanical-arm configuration, material restrictions and resource calendars keep their separate checks. Raw positive machine restrictions such as `只能排新34机` require manual review because the existing structured form has no allowed-machine whitelist.

Frozen additive migration `20260905_0100` follows `20260904_0099`, creates 16 domain tables and seeds four scoped permission codes. Startup checks migration readiness before `create_all`; it never upgrades a live database implicitly. The separate checkout SQLite `D:\RR\royal-regent-nexus\backend\data\royal_regent_nexus.db` is at revision `20260914_0116`, with customer-due-date, stocktake, document-tool, physical-position, carton master, supplier settlement, explicit quantities, customer PO and advanced spray schemas plus the 30 UV and eight order-ledger tables present. UV upgrade backups and restored-copy rehearsal evidence are retained under `D:/RR/maintenance-backups/uv-local-enable-20260914/`; integrity, foreign keys and unchanged contents of all 166 pre-existing business tables were verified before service restart. Earlier upgrade evidence remains under `D:/RR/local-startup-repair-20260912/`. The configured local V3 database is the business store. Imported plans and production records can be cleared by the explicit reset flow, while its persistent machine and mold data remain protected; none of these are disposable QA fixtures. Other early document-only databases labelled `20260908_0103` require the schema-verified branch-identity correction documented in `docs/document-tools/README.md`; the numeric marker alone does not prove carton positions exist.

Migration `20260912_0111` joins the customer PO branch `20260912_0108` and spray allocations `20260911_0110` without rewriting either migration. Database paths and migration states are checkout-specific. Earlier carton-activation documentation refers to a separate repaired SQLite state and must not be used to infer the current configured database. Always inspect the actual database before applying migrations. The published migration chain remains unchanged; divergent historical revision collisions require schema-equivalence checks and restored-copy rehearsal rather than blindly upgrading or stamping. Empty SQLite migration, real IAM/API, Excel round-trip and PostgreSQL DDL compilation have isolated tests. Production PostgreSQL upgrade from `20260911_0110` to `20260912_0111`, restored-copy startup and old-image service rollback have passed live rehearsal; this does not establish a fresh replay of the entire historical PostgreSQL migration chain.

Migration `20260903_0095` permanently drops both `injection_schedule_*` and `injection_scheduling_*` table families, removes their three generations of permission registrations/grants, invalidates affected authorization previews and removes module IAM seed state. It checks external dependencies before deletion and refuses downgrade because lost records require a verified backup. Historical migration scripts remain immutable for version-chain continuity; they have no dependency on removed application code.

The user previously authorized purging the old local scheduling database at `20260903_0095`. Historical 3D upgrade evidence is documented below; the current local V3 schema state is recorded above. Unrelated records, accounts and shared system authorization audit snapshots remain protected. The verified pre-purge backup under `D:\RR\maintenance-backups` is recovery material, not active application data. Source inputs were not erased. Local purge and V3 enablement do not establish production migration.

Production 3D repairs and hourly telemetry optimization are part of tracked main; current release identities and preservation evidence are maintained in section 7.
