They address memory-heavy historical statistics, cloud-record inventory classification, analytics timeouts and browser translation of 3D business text. Telemetry summaries must initialize only with a compatible writer. Additive covering indexes and hourly rollups at `20260929_0130` were verified on populated isolated PostgreSQL and SQLite databases, adding no business rows. Reset rollup initialization before reverting to an older telemetry writer so a later redeploy cannot reuse stale cache. API/Web replacement must preserve PostgreSQL and the separate `rr-three-d` printer Connector, private read-only translation models, reconciliation cutoff and production flags. Routine code rollback must retain the compatible database and never restore an old database over new work. Physical printer commands and inventory adjustments require separate business evidence; an offline dashboard is not proof a physical print stopped.

### Huakang A 3D Printing Management

3D printing management is a connected Huakang A-only production capability:

- The production card routes to `/modules/production/three-d-printing?factory=huakang-a` and uses strict page-entry permissions even while the legacy global authenticated-read policy remains enabled elsewhere.
- The 3D workspace's visual refinements are module-local under `.tdp-theme` (`styles/polish.css` and `styles/motion.css`). They keep the existing 216/175px sidebar, 1100/700px machine-grid breakpoints, two daily-record toolbars, table columns and business requests. `TdpButton` wraps the shared Button; busy states follow actual requests. The selected-nav indicator measures the real active button and allows manual horizontal scrolling. Native dialogs retain content until exit completes, then release temporary image display state and restore focus; the printer detail uses a body Teleport with a complete exit. Reduced motion bypasses movement without delaying close or data work. Device badges and progress reflect only fresh telemetry. The 3D workspace and its dialogs use translate=no so browser translation cannot rewrite device brands, product names or reactive UI text.
- Migration `20260729_0040` adds the original 3D domain; its permission seed retains explicit SQL casts for PostgreSQL bind parameters. `20260729_0041` reassigns the complete domain/IAM scope from Huakang B to Huakang A. Additive `20260904_0098` extends these same tables with provenance, frozen costs, inventory idempotency/reversals and command leases plus eight infrastructure/migration tables. Additive `20260904_0099` introduces typed operations resources with factory/kind/key constraints, revisions and audit receipts; destructive downgrade is refused. Startup requires 0099 and its table before create_all; import never upgrades schema. The local business SQLite and production PostgreSQL report `20260917_0117`; additive `20260917_0117` adds `three_d_printing_printer_connections.record_reconcile_enabled` (server default true, so existing connections keep recording) and bumps the startup schema guard. The last full reconciled legacy migration has an app_state cutoff of 2026-09-09 15:39:07 +08:00 and includes materials, inventory and settings. Print history has since been supplemented from the supplied `3d-server(1).zip` SQLite/WAL snapshot through 2026-09-17 10:24:33 +08:00: production received 282 missing records (261 active and 21 tombstones), 128 related products, 128 images and seven date rows after restored-copy PostgreSQL rehearsal. Existing records are preserved, including two source records subsequently tombstoned in the legacy snapshot but still active in Nexus; production therefore has 3,443 stored records, 3,265 active and 178 tombstones. Local development also has all 3,443 source records (3,263 active and 180 tombstones), with 1,471 related products and 1,169 images. This is record supplementation, not a full resync of materials, inventory, settings or prior edits. All eleven printer configurations, ownership and live collection remain unchanged. Later onsite changes are not automatically copied.
- The permission family is `three_d_printing:read|operate|image_upload|export|printer_control|audit_read`. 3D positions and the production-supervisor position receive scoped business permissions; only the administrator role receives `printer_control`. Production managers and general managers receive no 3D permissions by default. Migration batch/row-error/reconciliation GET APIs additionally require an active administrator binding and Huakang A audit access; explicit denies remain effective. Their output is an allowlisted projection, never raw source/connection JSON, and no HTTP migration-write endpoint exists.
- The production cloud Connector continuously observes all eleven Heyuan printers in cloud-observer mode; all eleven production connections are enabled for observation. Local development still has all eleven connections disabled. All endpoints and certificate fingerprints come from the actual onsite report, and cloud TLS probes matched all eleven. cloud-observer acquires a collector lease and stores fresh printer status, and its ability to write production records is now governed by the connection's own `record_reconcile_enabled` flag rather than by ownership: `record_reconcile_allowed()` in `backend/app/services/three_d_connector.py` permits recording for an enabled connection owned by either `cloud-connector` or `cloud-observer` when the flag is on, so an observation-only printer can complete daily records while hardware control still requires `cloud-connector`. `enable_observer.py` sets the flag to false on the mode switch, `deploy/three-d-printing/record_switch.py` plans and applies per-machine changes with a connection-revision bump, and the optional `THREE_D_RECONCILE_SINCE` guard keeps runs observed before that instant from being recorded retroactively. It cannot create, claim or dispatch printer commands even if global control switches are enabled. Explicit reconciliation returns observation_only while the flag is off, and legacy Edge pushes cannot overwrite cloud-observed status. Because observation-only therefore never auto-creates or auto-closes a daily record unless its record switch is on, the legacy standalone server's transition guards are ported into `backend/app/services/three_d_run_reconciliation.py`. A run that never received its terminal frame is settled by the periodic state sweep `sweep_open_runs`, driven by the FastAPI lifespan task and the settings `three_d_reconciliation_sweep_seconds` / `three_d_reconciliation_terminal_grace_seconds` / `three_d_reconciliation_stale_open_seconds` / `three_d_reconciliation_same_file_window_seconds`. That sweep examines only a connection whose record switch is on, enabled, stale-free and quiet beyond the grace window: `FINISH` closes the run as succeeded with the `closed_by_state_sweep` quality flag, `FAILED`/`ERROR` as failed, and every other end state including `IDLE` closes it as unknown with `completion_evidence_missing` and reconciliation pending; a run belonging to a superseded `device_job_key` is additionally flagged `device_job_changed_without_end`. The same lifespan task first runs one boot/reconnect settlement pass (`actor=system:boot-sweep`, `ignore_state_since=True`) that closes open runs straight from the full status the Connector pushes on connect, without waiting for a quiet window. Run creation additionally resumes an already recorded run of the same machine and gcode file whose end is missing or within the reprint window, instead of inserting a second record for one physical print, flagging it `device_job_changed_same_file`; an identical file printed after that window is a real second run. Product resolution strips the Bambu plate marker (`_plate`, `_plate_N`, and a doubled-marker separator) before matching, because printer file names carry it while no product row does, and falls back to the legacy contains match only when exactly one candidate qualifies; the exact-name query still refuses to guess between duplicates. When the resolved product already has a hand-entered record for the same machine and business day, the observation attaches to that record (`observed_run_linked_to_existing_record`) instead of adding a second empty one, using plate-normalized containment with a minimum length so unrelated short names never merge. Later telemetry retries the product match for a run still holding the placeholder name and fills a missing material from the device, flagged `material_source_device`, but the backfill never posts inventory on its own, so a run created before its material was known cannot double-consume stock. Telemetry backfill refreshes frozen quote inputs and totals when it fills product/material facts, using only captured rates and recording before/after snapshots in the audit; missing historical material prices remain unknown. An unchanged operator save of a current Nexus/cloud record repairs stale snapshot inputs left by older backfills. Imported historical snapshots keep their existing correction rules. Cross-midnight closure needs no special case: an open run is located by device identity, never by "today's" record. Settlement is settled from observed state only, so it never reverses or re-applies inventory, never reopens a deleted or already closed record, and is a no-op for observer-owned or unmarked connections; no operator-visible stale-open flag is exposed through the API yet. `backend/scripts/replay_three_d_runs.py` reconstructs runs from stored state events and writes a read-only diff against Nexus records and a legacy `data.json`, classifying each run as safe to insert, needing review, or already owned by the old writer; it applies nothing. enable_observer.py activates one printer idempotently without asserting that the old writer stopped. Old local software and Bambu Studio can coexist with observation; formal cloud-connector control transfer still uses the separate cutover procedure. Only the cloud service has the separate device-secret files mounted; local development keeps its Connector disabled with an independent token. The accepted path is cloud Connector -> Tailscale -> always-on Windows subnet router -> printer LAN. windows-site.ps1 handles installation/login, merging existing route advertisements, and TCP/TLS discovery without MQTT login. Windows PowerShell 5.1 entrypoints resolve their own directory after parameter binding and pass explicit child-script paths, so Chinese installation folders and System32 launch directories work; parameter defaults must not call Join-Path on PSScriptRoot. tailscale_doctor.py consumes the Windows report, then probes actual cloud routes, peer availability and pinned TLS without requiring a Linux VLAN or nftables snapshot. The cloud network timer is active every 30 seconds and reports healthy. Its probes do not authenticate MQTT. Tailscale probes allow 10 seconds per socket operation for the observed DERP relay; the systemd job timeout is 75 seconds, and host Python 3.10 uses timezone.utc. Unknown exposure evidence no longer causes a network failure on its own; explicit unsafe exposure, failed probes and over-90-second-old health remain abnormal. Device evidence older than 30 seconds is stale, and network loss never ends a job. Cloud-observer acquisition, session recovery, heartbeat projection and fresh telemetry are independent of site-wide diagnostic health: one failed printer probe must not disconnect the other observers. Server-written observation_only metadata keeps dashboard and SSE projections consistent; actual device silence/disconnection still becomes stale at 30 seconds. Network diagnostics continue to gate hardware control, and observer command claims return empty before that gate. Device serial/access codes remain outside the business database and Git. Material telemetry resolves external spool 254 and AMS trays across partial MQTT reports, preserving physical spool metadata across jobs and resetting it on reconnect; 255 means no selected tray. live_material stays empty when the device reports no material type, rather than guessing from temperature or job names. The onsite fleet currently reports external spool 254 with empty tray_type on all eleven printers; actual material display requires setting the loaded filament type on the device.
- PR-06 now includes the standalone Node cloud worker at `services/three-d-printer-connector/src/main.mjs`, bounded MQTT/pinned TLS and referenced secrets, discovery/heartbeat, per-printer local monotonic lease deadlines, ordered bounded event queues and command dispatch/ACK integration. Database leases are 30 seconds and command leases 8 seconds; dispatch permission is granted once, unknown sends are never replayed, and session/job/control epochs invalidate obsolete intentions. Reconciliation namespaces true device subtask IDs by printer, creates/consumes/freezes once on RUNNING, continues the same run after reconnect, and ends only on explicit matching terminal evidence. Download/preparation telemetry (PREPARE, PREPARING, DOWNLOADING, SLICING) is normalized to PREPARE rather than RUNNING; daily/machine cards and printer details show 准备中 and the reported filename without reusing print-progress percentages. Preparation alone never creates a production record or consumes inventory; actual RUNNING remains the start evidence. Release the API accepting PREPARE before the matching Connector; this preparation-state change requires coordinated API/Connector/frontend release and real-device download acceptance. Ambiguous products, missing identity and unresolved legacy runs require review; network loss never settles a run. Heartbeats mark expired devices/open runs pending. Internal/control flags default off, a distinct service token protects fixed Huakang A/Heyuan scope, and firmware-verified machine numbers are separately allowlisted. Only explicitly cloud-owned devices participate; old Edge cannot update/claim those devices. SSE uses committed PostgreSQL NOTIFY plus bounded snapshot queues, database refresh recovery, Last-Event-ID snapshot resets and recurring session/permission checks. The existing page applies live printer snapshots and falls back to polling after stream loss. It reopens a silent SSE stream after 15 seconds, retries on network/focus recovery, ignores retired streams, and preserves newer live snapshots over older dashboard responses. Periodic queries do not overlap, but post-save refreshes always run and superseded query results cannot overwrite them. Local acceptance covers two real Node workers with 11 TLS/MQTT simulators, logical 30-minute outage recovery, no duplicate runs, command evidence and SSE; actual PostgreSQL row locking/SKIP LOCKED and notification commit/rollback are tested in a disposable schema. Real VPN, firmware session fencing, old Edge disconnect and field cutover remain unaccepted; a paused operating system cannot be physically fenced by lease timers alone. The old Windows Edge entrypoint is retained for explicit legacy ownership; code completion is not a production switch.
- The operating UI preserves legacy workflows and the left navigation/daily printer-card/table layout, but colors must use the Nexus global semantic theme tokens (deep teal primary, cool-white surfaces, muted table headers). Do not copy the old standalone application blue/yellow theme. The desktop navigation stays pinned to the viewport with a separately scrolling menu; the footer retains sync, a factory-preserving home link and the shared AccountMenu with the actual signed-in account. Small screens use a sticky compact navigation strip and accessible home/account controls. Modal record/product/material/schedule/maintenance editing, separate material and warehouse pages, product image tables, date filtering and Excel export. The Vue pages remain under `src/features/three-d-printing` behind the existing route, with server-counted pagination (first/last page and typed page jump), bounded record rendering, remote product lookup, printer timelines and a reconciliation center under secondary navigation. Daily records expose keyword search across all historical dates or the selected day, include production dates in results, and retain search filters across pagination. Product-library rows open related print history by product-name keyword; exports remain explicitly scoped to the selected date. Product dialogs accept native Ctrl+V image paste anywhere in the form, preview the pending image and allow cancelling it before Save; local-file selection remains available. Both inputs use the existing JPEG/PNG/WebP 5MB upload path and upload only when saving the product. Native paste works on the existing HTTP deployment without the secure-context Clipboard read API. Live cards expose layer count and target temperatures from optional Connector telemetry fields without changing connection ownership. The printer-detail drawer presents Chinese current-state guidance, task/progress/temperatures, Beijing timestamps and consecutive state groups by state/file/error/session with a 90-second gap boundary; it labels the bounded recent-observation window rather than claiming full job history. Equal-time live/detail snapshots preserve stale evidence, while newer telemetry can recover. Record and product dialogs expose per-piece automatic quote calculation with a 12-hour electricity/labor basis and once-per-record design fee; record edits prefer frozen snapshot rates. When those rates are missing or the selected material differs, the quote editor offers a clearly labelled reference from the registered current material price and current billing settings; changing pricing inputs or clicking the current-price recalculation applies it to the quoted price. Opening/re-rendering never changes the saved quote, manual overrides remain sticky, and missing current material prices stay unavailable with a specific explanation. This does not backfill historical costs or alter inventory; backend frozen-cost evidence remains unchanged. The current-rate re-quote UI is local only until separately released. Opening or unrelated rendering preserves saved quotes; pricing-input edits recalculate unless manually overridden. These detail/quote changes were deployed with a183d2b on 2026-09-17. Daily-record dialogs now additionally support pending image paste, file selection and drag/drop with preview/cancel, a responsive information/image layout and a fixed Save footer. Clicking an existing or pending record photo opens a separate zoomable preview (up to 4x); replacing it remains a distinct file-picker action. Shared legacy dialogs handle only their own cancel events, so cancelling a nested file picker cannot dismiss the editor, and closing the photo preview returns to the unchanged draft. Each record owns its image through product_snapshot_json.record_image; edits and cost freezing preserve that metadata, and the list falls back to the product reference image only when no record image exists. Record images use the existing asset volume and image permissions; uploading increments the record revision without changing frozen costs or inventory. Record-save success followed by image failure retains the saved ID/revision and pending file for retry, avoiding duplicate new records. Image retries replay the original idempotency key before any further record edit; a newer-than-expected response version requires reopening the record instead of overwriting concurrent edits. This record-image change is local only until separately released. SSE retains polling recovery. PR-09 resources cover physical spool/AMS balances, approved exact-filename/version bindings, immutable content-addressed files, direct demand planning/completion proofs and manually confirmed run-quality/spool evidence. Request department scope requires an actual matching active operation grant, independently of the wider legacy position-role permission composition; operators can generate a plan directly from a submitted demand without supervisor approval. Legacy approval actions remain compatible; cancelling a scheduled demand also cancels its plan. Name-based pickers link records, spools, attachments and compatible products; completion record searches filter by product and success state, and trace output is structured for business users. Save/action notes are optional in the UI with generated action descriptions. Advisory scheduling preserves assigned machines first and reserves time/spool capacity only in memory. Missing network or setup data permits explicitly labelled offline estimates; known incompatibility, insufficient registered stock and fresh device errors affect candidates. Applying a recommendation saves only the versioned plan assignment and never dispatches hardware commands. Telemetry analytics use exact, rebuildable hourly summaries (migration 20260929_0130) for closed hours and stream only the first/current partial hours through the ix_3d_event_analytics covering index (20260929_0129). Cache entries retain boundary observations and full error-code counts so merging preserves the 120-second gap rule and window clipping. Accepted Connector frames invalidate their hour in the same transaction; duplicates and failed transactions do not invalidate it. Refresh locks the summary row so concurrent ingestion cannot lose invalidation. A separate background task refreshes at most two closed dirty hours per minute; requests rebuild dirty hours when needed. Deploy the new writer before running backend/scripts/rebuild_three_d_telemetry_rollups.py --apply; the initializer marks historical buckets dirty and exposes the cache only after backfill completes. Uninitialized or unmigrated installations retain the previous PostgreSQL grouped-counter / SQLite streaming path. Before rolling back to an older writer, clear the rollup-state initialized_at marker, and reinitialize before enabling the cache again. Original events and business records are retained. All raw event reads select narrow fields with streaming configured before execute; production-record fields and maintenance/profile queries are also bounded or batched. Statistics retain a 75-second database timeout, an 85-second proxy timeout and a 90-second client wait with a visible busy state and duplicate-request protection. OEE remains an explicitly estimated metric, with quantity-scaled standard hours and clipped cross-window intervals; observed pause/wait intervals exclude telemetry gaps, and maintenance uses actual completed intervals and deterministic error/temperature rules. Camera/ML and semi-automatic dispatch remain conditional future work.
- Product images and versioned attachments are stored under `THREE_D_ASSET_DIR`; the production Compose asset volume is `three-d-assets`, and attachments use content hashes below `operations/`. Authorized downloads are forced attachments. Product save and image upload are separate awaited operations. File mappings are frozen when a connector run is created; later edits do not rewrite historical evidence, and physical spool snapshots require operator confirmation rather than inferring history from current AMS slots.
- Authoritative legacy input is SQLite `app_state.id=1` plus `product_images` BLOBs, including committed WAL state. Capture operates on disposable frozen/ZIP copies or SQLite online backup; private snapshots/manifests stay outside Git. Missing WAL requires completed-checkpoint attestation, active-frame corruption blocks capture and obsolete checkpoint tails are accounted for. The CLI defaults to read-only analyze; analyze/dry-run never initialize Nexus DB. Explicit import/reconcile use the installed v2 schema, source-bound batches, site leases, transactional row checkpoints and validated image assets. Same-snapshot replay rechecks actual targets before returning already_reconciled; business rows/ledger/assets remain unchanged, while verification audit events may be appended. Incremental snapshots update only source-owned, unedited targets; failed attempts retain prior ownership, images can retry, and missing prior source rows block reconciliation. The retained explicit JSON importer is not permitted for this cutover.
- `backend/scripts/supplement_legacy_three_d_records.py` provides an append-only alternative for missing print history. It validates a captured snapshot/manifest, deduplicates by factory and legacy ID, and inserts only missing records and required uniquely resolved product/image/date dependencies in one transaction. Existing business rows and inventory effects are preserved; replay adds no duplicate rows. Its batch stays `imported`, not `reconciled`, because unrelated entities and existing source edits are not synchronized. A later full importer can resume after an existing full-migration baseline; the initially empty local target retains unowned settings and needs a separate plan before a full cutover. Private source snapshots, backups and identity reports remain outside Git.
- Migration preserves tombstones, raw material names and historical record snapshots. Legacy running is not evidence of a live job; unclosed runs use unknown plus pending reconciliation. Inventory imports current balances through opening/adjustment movements; historical stock-ins use affects_balance=false and aliases never merge balances. Historical fields are not backfilled from current products. Imported legacy-v1 cost snapshots label source-snapshot rates as diagnostic rather than proven historical cost; master/rate or non-cost record changes do not reprice them. Source corrections with the same material use the originally captured rates; a different material without historical price leaves cost null and marks cost_snapshot_requires_review. PR-04 source now removes product-history cascading (only pending schedules follow a rename), keeps raw inventory buckets stable on material rename, and uses full consumption or explicit material_shortage without flooring. Edits/deletes/day-off batches append reversals linked to original consumption; restores check stock again. Active factory supervisors/admins may explicitly approve negative stock with a reason. Required retry keys, revision checks, a factory write lock and transactional audit receipts protect manual ledger operations; receipts replay without duplicating business writes. New records freeze nexus-v1 rates/results and profile hashes; record totals retain those snapshots, including allocated labor, and mark missing historical costs instead of current-rate repricing. The restored daily/monthly operating view uses summary.legacyDisplay with one full labor charge per production day and excludes marked rest days; it does not overwrite frozen record costs. Default summary fields retain per-record allocation for existing callers. Nexus and cloud-connector production records without a legacy identity share the receipt-based inventory correction and restore path; source labels alone never turn a current cloud run into migrated history. Insufficient stock still preserves the record as pending without forced negative stock, and retries remain idempotent. Imported historical corrections use history-only mode plus audit access and never replay opening balances; the legacy edit dialog selects that mode automatically for imported records; unproven material prices remain null. Deleted-history queries and recovery also require audit access. The migration has restored-copy PostgreSQL rehearsal and live production count/image reconciliation; legacy operations have browser verification on the isolated database. Backend/frontend must load the matching contracts together; physical printer control acceptance is still separate.
- The operational cutover, rollback and edge acceptance procedure is recorded in `docs/three-d-printing-deployment.md`. The actual Heyuan site has Windows PCs, no Linux gateway and no onsite network specialist; the user is remote. The onsite report captured at 2026-09-09 15:28 +08:00 shows the Windows gateway connected to Tailscale and all 11 LAN TCP/TLS probes successful. Cloud probes independently verified all 11 routes and matching TLS fingerprints. Actual addresses, fingerprints and source reports remain outside Git. MQTT authentication and fresh live full-status reception are verified for all eleven printers. Physical serial-number matching and actual pause/resume command acceptance remain unverified. Existing PR-05 Linux nftables/systemd/XFRM and disable-SNAT templates must not be applied to Windows. The old 3d-server remains running with active print jobs; all eleven printers are observed by the cloud while control remains disabled. Onsite TCP evidence showed Bambu Studio connections rather than the presumed node server.js connection. The old firewall-based disconnection tools are not required for observation and were not successfully applied.

### QC Department Inspection Operations

The current target business requirements are `docs/business/QC验货运营中心业务需求.md`, reorganized from the user's `QC完整业务流程.docx` and latest explicit direction. The target removes report renaming from this center, moves temporary-order creation into an optional modal/drawer action within the total inspection schedule, adds customer filtering there, and supports checkbox-based bulk import of selected preview rows with unselected rows left pending. The frontend now implements these entry changes in QcOperationsCenterView/QcScheduleWorkspace: the old center, standalone manual-order page and rename page are removed. The four top-level entries are total schedule, schedule details, problem handling and reports. Total schedule is now an import/manual-add overview, without an inline order table. The standalone `/schedule-details` table includes only confirmed SCHEDULE_IMPORT orders that are unfinished and not cancelled; unscheduled and IN_PROGRESS/PENDING rows remain visible, while completed results (including FAIL) leave the pending table. It loads all factory weeks but opens on the current Asia/Shanghai calendar day. Pending schedules are grouped into one full table per effective inspection date, with previous/next/today, date-range, all-date and undated views; no day is split across row pagination. Customer/date/status/agency filters and optional current-business-week filtering are supported, plus revision-checked schedule editing. Result writers can select rows, an entire day or every filtered row (including days not yet expanded) and batch-register a shared explicit result and actual date. Filter changes clear selection. The confirmation dialog lists orders, requires a new-event type and note, preserves existing pending-event details/type, and saves each order independently with stable retry request IDs. Successful results leave the pending view; partial failures remain visible for retry. Event create/update accepts an optional expected_pending_order_revision guard, which enforces the imported-pending scope and checks order revision before mutation; the existing result-write and factory checks remain authoritative. No actual inspected quantities, sampling standards or dispositions are inferred for new quick-entry events. Manual orders and completed/cancelled history remain accessible through the separate `/order-records` table; source and completion filtering does not delete records. Import selection spans pages and filters, supports explicit KEEP/SKIP as well as CREATE/UPDATE, and submits at most 1000 decisions sequentially using returned revisions; unselected rows stay pending and existing current-week batches can be resumed. Legacy add links open the schedule modal; rename links redirect to reports. Persistence and historical attachments are preserved. This is not a claim that every target report layout or future configuration screen is implemented. The earlier `docs/business/qc-complete-business-process.md` remains a reference for historical implementation and technical contracts; its conflicting product-direction statements are superseded by the new requirements. Reporting week boundaries, customer/market mapping, staged inspection dates and metric definitions remain explicit business confirmation items.

QC schedule imports use the explicit `qc-review-unshipped-v1` rule: read only `正单评审表`, only rows with order type `正单` or `正式PO` above its standalone `取消单` section marker. The user defines that region as unshipped orders. Never fall back to `接单表`, ITEM sheets, cancelled rows or shipped sections; missing review sheet, order-type header or cancellation boundary blocks preview. No automatic calendar-week filtering is applied to this source region; the selected business week is assigned only on explicit QC confirmation. Header aliases cover `客名`, `Contract No.`, `P/O#:`, `产品编号`, `數量`, `验货日期` and `客要求走货期`. Customer/market names and national standards are not inferred as export country codes. Preview retains original Sheet/row and mapped source-cell values, preserves identifier leading zeroes, and flags missing PO or multi-stage date text for correction/skip rather than guessing. Formal orders require explicit confirmation of selected rows; bulk confirmation never includes unselected rows. Effective planned inspection dates use the explicit customer/planned date first, then shipment date when the former is blank; only two blank dates remain unscheduled. Raw planned dates and import source cells are preserved instead of backfilled, so later shipment changes automatically update fallback schedules. Frontend display, planned-date filtering, status/counts and ascending order, backend order sorting, and newly generated schedule XLSX period selection/date cells share this rule. Nonempty invalid or multi-stage text remains a validation issue, never a missing-date fallback. Existing orders apply the rule without migration; prior report snapshots remain immutable.

The canonical QC workflow and implemented business rules are recorded in `docs/business/qc-complete-business-process.md`. The module is registered at `/modules/qc/inspection-operations`, uses `/api/qc-inspections`, and is backed by Alembic revision `20260824_0082` (following the P0 revision `20260812_0067`). Its persisted core includes customer configuration, schedule-import batches and rows, inspection orders, multiple inspection/reinspection events, event PO/item lines, defects, test results, explicit dispositions, inspection report packages/documents, inspection problems, schedule decisions, generated report snapshots, report-renaming batches/groups/source files, audit events and idempotency records. The implementation is present in the repository but must not be described as deployed until the migration is applied and the application services are restarted and verified. QC schedule imports accept `.xls`, `.xlsx` and `.xlsm` workbooks up to 35 MB per file; the API streaming read and the import service both enforce the same authoritative cap.

Every inspection order has a system-generated factory-scoped unique inspection number. Sales contract plus customer item number is candidate matching only; zero matches may create a new order, one match may be compared, and multiple matches require a user decision with no silent overwrite. Temporary orders require a PO. Inspection results are entered on the order detail or through selected pending orders in the daily schedule’s batch-result dialog; both persist inspection events. Initial `PENDING` is not a submitted result and creates no problem; submitted `FAIL`, `REJECTED`, `CONDITIONAL_PASS` or `CANCELLED`, or a manual problem flag including PASS plus that flag, creates a deduplicated problem draft. Problem description, return reason, corrective action and resolution are maintained only in the problem workspace.

The QC permission family has 12 codes: read, schedule write, order write, result write, problem write, report export, rename preview, rename execute, customer management, audit read, factory summary and group summary. Inspector permissions include all normal factory operations, including rename execution and own-factory report export. Supervisors and managers additionally receive customer management, audit read and factory-summary permissions. `qc_inspection:group_summary` is separately granted, requires global factory scope, and is not inherited by the QC manager role. Backend factory checks are authoritative.

Current reports are manual XLSX outputs for customer summary, weekly statistics, Huaxing customer weekly detail, Huaxing weekly aggregate and group summary; PDF, CSV and scheduled generation are not implemented. Customer-summary Sheets are derived from the factory's active customer configurations, retaining an empty formatted Sheet for configured customers without completed inspections while also including unconfigured customer names that have reportable data; the historical 12-name sample list is not hard-coded. Inspection orders model packing, carton count, report status and production department; problems model first and second responsible persons. The report builder reads only those authoritative fields and leaves missing historical values blank. The Huaxing weekly detail column for non-return rework/AOD remains blank because no authoritative field exists and must not be inferred from results or problem text. Report renaming is removed from this frontend, including its API client and permissions references. Its historical backend remains for compatibility and retains explicit groups and immutable source uploads, preview-before-execute, audit and idempotency, and produces a new ZIP rather than mutating originals. Non-Caixing names are `COUNTRY_customer-item_PO_YYYYMMDD`; Caixing names are `report_customer-item_PO_digits-only-quantity_YYYYMMDD`; multi-image JPGs use `_01`, `_02`, `.jpg` and `.jpeg` inputs are accepted, illegal filename characters normalize to `_`, and a missing PDF/JPG or any group error leaves the whole group unchanged while other groups continue. A batch is capped at 39 groups, 25 files per group, 25 MB per file and 250 MB total; production gateways must also enforce a request-body cap no higher than 250 MB because application validation cannot replace ingress and temporary-disk protection. Multi-PO/multi-item naming remains unresolved and must not be inferred.

The V3 QC requirements baseline is evidence-driven from the read-only 2026-08-24 review of the user's QC archive (7,950 files, about 8.06 GiB). It distinguishes structured source data, system-generated statistics, immutable externally issued reports, and evidence/adjacent quality registers. V3.1 implements the P1 core transaction chain: an inspection order can keep multiple execution/reinspection events; each event has multi-PO/item lines, defects, tests and explicit return/rework/AOD/concession dispositions; saving an event synchronizes the order's latest result without overwriting event history; a per-event XLSX inspection report contains conclusion, PO/item, defect, test and disposition sheets and creates a generated report package/document index. External original-file upload/migration, OCR, field-level raw-data lineage, cross-report automatic reconciliation and final-report approval remain requirements only. P2 still covers department nonconformance, product testing, approved samples, training and factory-audit registers only if ownership is later confirmed.

The V3.1 report catalog preserves the five current manual XLSX outputs and implements weekly schedule, daily inspection ledger, weekly problem detail, weekly return summary, inspection pass-rate, annual statistics, product-quality ledger and inspection-document index XLSX outputs, plus a per-event inspection report. Report snapshots record explicit WEEK/MONTH/YEAR/EVENT scope, `qc-metrics-v1`, source revision SHA-256 and artifact SHA-256. New return reports count only explicit `RETURN` dispositions; FAIL/REJECTED do not imply return, CONDITIONAL_PASS does not imply AOD, reinspection does not overwrite the first event, and zero denominators emit zero rather than spreadsheet errors. The metric dictionary still needs business sign-off. Full evidence archiving must later support PDF-only, image-only, mixed/multi-PDF packages plus read-only historical DOC/DOCX/XLS/XLSX attachments using immutable originals, relative source path and content hash. This broader archive requirement does not reactivate the retired rename UI; the retained historical backend still uses explicit one-PDF-plus-JPG/JPEG groups with the existing size/count limits and single-PO naming; multi-PO/multi-item naming still requires business sign-off.

### Spray Production Management

The rebuilt module is an independent `SprayOps*` / `spray_ops_*` domain under `/modules/production/spray-production` and `/api/spray-operations`, using the read-only Royal-Regent-Nexus_Spray_Codex_Pack contract. Both `VITE_SPRAY_OPS_ENABLED` and `SPRAY_OPS_ENABLED` default to false; the production-department card stays an inert placeholder until enabled. Four explicit execution factories (`huaxing`, `huakang-a`, `huakang-b`, `huadeng`) are supported, without group/invalid-factory fallback. All auth modes enforce current factory/department/action authorization. Fourteen `spray_ops:*` rights are cataloged but not automatically added to fixed positions. New migration `20260922_0119` creates 52 normalized tables after customer-pricing migration `20260922_0118`; existing databases must be migrated explicitly before enabling, and automatic downgrade is blocked. Applied legacy migrations, old spray data and original source files remain preserved; no automatic old-table import exists. Internal-quote painting workflows remain separate and active.

Core facts are actual material batches, versioned DAG routes, finite-capacity plan previews, immutable confirmed reports, separate employee hours/payroll, handover acceptance events, original-cost material lots and partially allocated settlements. Per-factory transaction locks, entity versions and actor/action/payload-bound operation receipts protect writes. Quantity/allocation columns conserve independently; a seven-person team producing 560 units produces 560, not 3920. Unconfirmed rules allow trials only. Unknown prices/costs stay null, savings do not reduce material expense twice, payroll uses deterministic cent remainders, and historical confirmed prices/results do not change with current rules. Unknown material costs can be confirmed once in open periods using separate evidence events; existing unknown consume events remain unchanged. Physical returns and financial credits are distinct. Closing a period preserves original currencies and does not establish a consolidated foreign-currency profit.

Source imports support OOXML and BIFF8 signatures plus PDF evidence, retaining hashes, coordinates, formula/cache/error evidence and manual mapping decisions. Twelve profiles use explicit per-document atomic confirmation; complex original layouts are not fully automatically mapped. Opening balances and historical replay require mutually exclusive cutoff policies. Seven workspaces share user/factory/auth context fences, dirty navigation protection and an order passport. Nine frozen XLSX export types include a split production/employee/day-payroll report, with download authorization rechecked. Local synthetic business paths, SQLite migration/concurrency tests and several viewport sizes are verified; production migration/activation, PostgreSQL execution, source-exact print-template goldens and the complete accessibility/200% browser matrix remain unverified. Contracts and current evidence live in `docs/spray-production/`; source workbooks must remain outside Git. These limits must not be reported as full field or financial acceptance.

### Huakang A UV Printing Management

UV printing is rebuilt as the independent `uv-operations` domain, restricted to explicit `huakang-a / production`. The production portal links to `/modules/production/uv-printing`; new APIs use `/api/uv-operations` and `/api/internal/uv-agent`, models/tables use `UvOps*`/`uv_ops_*`, and 18 `uv_ops:*` permissions require canonical authorization even in legacy/shadow mode. Fixed positions receive no automatic grants. `UV_OPS_ENABLED` defaults off and startup never creates its 44 tables; revision `20260929_0127` follows `20260928_0126`, adds batch planning/execution/reversal ledgers and preserves old rows and SQLite CHECK constraints. The actual local business SQLite is at `20260929_0130`, including 0127; the UV runtime switch remains off pending real-device, IAM and production acceptance. Historical `uv_*` tables, migrations and audit data remain protected; do not resurrect the removed old routes, permissions or implementation. Source contract and handoff are in `docs/uv-operations/`.

Runs are read-only evidence; only confirmed production/quality/handover ledgers affect physical quantities. Tasks freeze process/file/price/wage versions. Rework binds a parent batch, gang allocations share one physical fixture per pass and allocate Run cost once. Decimal strings, explicit unknown values, separate currencies, immutable period snapshots, historical-date balance checks and current-authorization receipt/download projection are required. The outbound Windows agent uses DPAPI and APSW WAL/FULL with durable per-event ACK; simulator and synthetic CSV prove software only. Native print/pause/cancel dispatch is unsupported.

Software and synthetic business acceptance now cover manual unknown-cycle estimates, independent sub-batch/machine planning with physical-fixture exclusion, manual start/finish including cross-shift completion, reasoned immutable correction chains, full linked exports and shift/month close. Inventory rejects dates before existing source/target movements. Live snapshots poll every two seconds, share business facts for at most one second while reauthorizing/projecting per request, and identify current Runs from the current source cursor event. A 30-day/6000-plan/100000-Run service-query benchmark meets the suggested 500 ms steady p95 (376.10 ms); cold connection p95 is 682.47 ms and HTTP/production SLA is unverified. Current evidence is docs/uv-operations/SOFTWARE_ACCEPTANCE_20260929.md. Real-device/SCM/session-zero, Nginx release, actual price/wage/opening-stock/user-grant configuration and final business signoff remain deferred; synthetic acceptance does not enable production.

Migration `20260922_0120` retires the 13 exact legacy permission codes and their grants, updating affected authorization versions while preserving business data, access requests and audit events. The local `D:/RR` SQLite includes this cleanup in its applied Alembic chain; other databases require the retirement migration during their release. Alembic autogeneration excludes reflected `uv_*` tables absent from runtime metadata so retained history is not proposed for deletion.

### Group Operations Dashboard

The homepage (`/`, name `dashboard`) is a presentation surface over the static `overviewMetrics`, `factoryHeatmap`, `crossFactoryItems` and `moduleHealth` sample arrays in `src/data/enterpriseMock.ts`. It has no homepage API, and its `本周` and `导出报表` header actions remain unbound, so they render as genuinely disabled buttons next to a visible `示例数据 · 筛选与导出暂未接入` note; do not add counters, filtering, export or fabricated live status to it.

Its default Jade visual layer is `src/components/dashboard/styles/dashboard.css`; the dashboard additionally opts into the local Prism V4 layer described below. The original tokens and selectors remain namespaced under `.dashboard-page[data-dashboard-ui="jade-v2"]` and never declared on `:root`, `body` or `.app-shell`, so the shared `PageHeader`, `SectionPanel`, `ProgressMeter`, `StatusPill`, `enterprise-panel` and `surface-subtle` definitions stay reusable by other pages. The `data-active` factory highlight and the `activeFactoryId` prop of `FactoryHeatmap` are display-only: `group` highlights no factory, a factory without a sample card is not highlighted and gets no invented row, and switching stays in the global top bar.

A container query can only match a descendant, never the container element itself. The homepage `@container dashboard-page` breakpoints therefore require `container: dashboard-page / inline-size` on the ancestor `.dashboard-page-container` node in `DashboardView.vue`; putting it back on `.dashboard-page` silently disables the 4-metric row and the two-column main area while still passing source-text contract tests. The dashboard's metrics card, factory card, timeline and module-health component classes are currently used only by the homepage.

### Portal UI System (Jade Atlas)

The seven department homepages (`/modules/:department`, name `modules-department`) all render `ModuleCenterView.vue` and share one portal system. `src/components/portal/portalPresentation.ts` holds presentation-only per-department config (accent, accent soft surface, motif, permission note, todo empty text) and is computed from the route parameter, not read once on mount, because the same component instance is reused when the department changes. Motif artwork lives in `PortalMotif.vue` and the identity area in `PortalHero.vue`, which wraps the existing `PageHeader` so its `title` / `description` / `actions` contract and the factory title assertions in `moduleCenterFactoryScope.spec.ts` keep working. `src/components/portal/styles/portal.css` and `portal-shell.css` retain the default Jade appearance and are imported by `AppShell.vue` and all their ordinary selectors are namespaced under `.rrn-portal[data-portal-ui="jade-v3"]` or `.app-shell[data-portal-shell="jade-v3"]`, never on `:root` or the shared component defaults.

`src/lib/portalRouteScope.ts` is the only switch for portal appearance. It matches by route name plus `isModuleDepartmentId`, and must not use path prefixes or `meta.fullPage`: `/modules/:department/:module` is the module detail page and keeps the default appearance. `AppShell.vue` puts `data-portal-shell="jade-v3"` on `.app-shell` only for `dashboard`, `modules-department` (valid department), `workbench`, `people-directory` and `shared-tool-center`; the two `fullPage` permission pages use their own local `data-portal-region` hooks. Navigation theming only targets the named hooks (`.topbar-surface`, `.topbar-search-slot`, `.topbar-factory-button`, `.sidebar-surface`, `.sidebar-group-label`, `.sidebar-nav-link`, `.sidebar-nav-icon`, `.sidebar-health`) and reads sidebar selection from `aria-current="page"`.

Shared components stay default-first and opt in explicitly: `PermissionMatrix` and `TodoQueue` take `appearance="portal"` (used by the department homepage) while `ModuleDetailView` keeps the default markup and styles, and `IamNavigation` takes `appearance="portal"` only from `IamRoleTemplatesView`. `MemberRow` / `MemberDirectoryList` portal styling is scoped by `[data-directory-variant="default"]` so the member drawer is unaffected. Department homepages do not use `reveal-grid`; V4 separates list movement, a temporary reveal wrapper and article hover, and disables the old article entrance animation. `ModuleCard` keyboard activation only fires when the event target is the card itself, and the fixed strings `router.push(module.route)`, `:role="module.route ? 'link' : undefined"` and `@click.stop` are pinned by `productionModuleEntry.spec.ts`. Backend entry labels such as `共 128 条` and `在线服务 23 / 24` are hardcoded demonstrations; the portal only marks them as sample context and never presents them as live figures.

The dashboard and seven valid department homepages additionally opt into `data-home-experience="prism-v4"` through the strict `getHomeExperienceScope` helper. Existing Jade markers remain; business details, workbench, directory, tools, IAM and authentication pages do not opt in. `homePrismPresentation.ts` contains only the eight visual identities; `HomePrismArtwork.vue` contains finite decorative SVG motion. `home-prism.css`, `home-prism-motion.css` and `home-prism-shell.css` scope their content/shell overrides to that V4 marker. See `DESIGN.md` section 15.5 for this explicit exception to the quieter global design baseline.

`useHomePresentation` searches and resolves preview IDs exclusively against the existing final `visibleModules` pipeline; module data, supplier route remapping, factory rules and authorization still belong to their original sources. Search preserves order, supports IME and safe text highlighting, and provides separate no-result/no-module states. Preview supports route-only, internal href and external href actions; targetless placeholders remain inert. Hover waits 140ms; explicit preview pins the target. Account, raw factory context (including group), resolved factory or department changes clear query/pin state; revoked or filtered targets disappear immediately. Mobile uses one Reka dialog preview and restores trigger/search focus.

`useHomeAppearance` shares local presentation preferences through AppShell. Only schema version plus density/motion enums are persisted per account; guest choices stay in memory. System reduced motion and hidden documents override expressive motion. Sidebar and department indicators measure DOM geometry and retain positioning transforms with animation off; V4 production uses only MAIN as the canonical current sidebar item while preserving the module-configuration alias. TopBar measures its real height for sticky offsets and exposes a narrow-screen factory select through the unchanged switch logic. The department layout queries a distinct `home-page` ancestor (640px two cards; 1000px main plus 320px aside), while the dashboard retains its existing ancestor container queries. The new homepage does not add backend APIs, business KPIs or business-editing flows.

### Notification Work Center

`/notifications` and the top-bar bell use `src/stores/workCenter.ts`, one application-level `WorkCenterHost`, and `/api/work-center`. The current-source adapters cover molding-sample approval/production/problems, internal-quote department work and version-specific batch review, registration/password-reset requests, supplier shipment receipt/correction, and identity information. Other modules are not included in these counts. A business task ends only through its original domain workflow; personal read/snooze/pin/follow/archive actions never approve, receive stock, or complete production. Supplier-only portal sessions do not start this internal center.

Canonical responsibility keys include source, stage and cycle. Source reads and current IAM remain authoritative even before projection backfill. Shared notification `read_at`/`handled` is historical evidence, not personal read state or proof of business completion. Personal states are isolated by user and employment epoch; observed-version reads are monotonic. Source changes update replayable projection/events inside the original transaction. Read snapshots use one transaction and bounded SQL pagination; failed source adapters expose partial health instead of a false zero. Legacy notification endpoints overlay personal reads and reject manual handling of active responsibilities.

Existing databases must explicitly apply additive migration `20260928_0126` after backup. Startup refuses an incomplete work-center schema. `backend/scripts/reconcile_work_center.py` requires an explicit database URL, defaults to dry-run, and preserves source rows and personal states; repeat apply is idempotent. Roll back the UI with `VITE_WORK_CENTER_ENABLED=false` while retaining the new tables and compatible backend. Migration downgrade refuses existing personal state/preferences. PostgreSQL runtime concurrency and production acceptance remain unverified; local verification uses isolated SQLite databases. See `docs/work-center/IMPLEMENTATION.md` for the delivery evidence and migration runbook.

### Module Catalog and Placeholders

Several cards and dashboards in the module catalog remain planning, design or demonstration surfaces. Their labels, counts and sample rows are not proof of backend implementation. Each module must be classified from its registered route, API client, backend router, model and tests before changes are planned. The PMC / warehouse catalog no longer includes `订单事实收件箱` or the `入库与月结` placeholder, and the production catalog no longer includes `啤机部订单收件箱`. This removes their cards, search results and previews in every factory; existing order-inbox routes, APIs, permissions and records remain available.

## 7. Deployment and Data Safety Constraints

Production on 47.115.217.27 runs clean Git main `e34fc123e5afb02a39c956fbdbee62b52cb7d337`. The server-local `.deployment-prod-release.yml` pins Web to `royal-regent-web:multiselect-e34fc123-20261009`, and API/document-tools worker to the unchanged `royal-regent-api:quote-60a4fb1f-20261009`. Backend, shared runtime and deployment configuration sources are unchanged between `60a4fb1f` and `e34fc123`, so this release replaces only Web. PostgreSQL remains at `20261009_0148`. Collaborative spreadsheets support multiple department selection alongside same-factory rosters, progress colors, inline editing and original-format downloads.

Pin the production server's ED25519 host key to `SHA256:S72/ypSEg5gVBVHYE+YSfyxvMGW93xtMCEJOtR5Lf2I`. The user verified this fingerprint through the Aliyun console. Verify subsequent changes through an independent trusted channel before sending credentials.

API, Web and document worker are healthy. The current Web manifest verifies all 291 HTTP static assets; public health, tool-page/core asset hashes and anonymous collaborative-sheet access rejection pass. Backend, document worker, database and printer connector container IDs/images remain unchanged across the Web update, and production environment bytes match the saved checksum. The original collaborative-schema rollout verified 306 pre-existing business/identity tables and retained full database/asset backups. Real employee browser acceptance remains separate from synthetic and public release probes.

Existing runtime environment bytes, IAM identity writes/scheduling, authorization modes, document/model/assistant/3D volumes and the physical-printer connector are preserved. Compose always includes `.env.production`, `docker-compose.prod.yml` and `.deployment-prod-release.yml`. Frontend build retains `/api`, spray operations, work-center and identity UI flags. The connector remains `rr-three-d-connector:82b3424-20260922`; no printer-control operation or business data reset is part of this deployment.

Current Web rollback configuration, image metadata, unchanged-container evidence and receipt are in `/opt/royal-regent/backups/multiselect-e34fc123-20261009`; the package, manifest and scripts are in `/opt/royal-regent/builds/multiselect-e34fc123-20261009`. The Web rollback entry is `bash /opt/royal-regent/builds/multiselect-e34fc123-20261009/deploy.sh rollback`, restoring `royal-regent-web:quote-60a4fb1f-20261009` without restarting the backend or restoring data. Earlier full database and asset backups remain in `/opt/royal-regent/backups/collaborative-20261009`; the database dump checksum was reverified before the Web update. No new schema migration or database restore is part of this release. Retain current database/assets; never restore an old database over newer user work. Re-enter controlled maintenance and reassess recovery before later rollback.

Images reuse verified existing Python, Office, Node/canvas/ONNX and offline-model layers with unchanged production dependency manifests; `olefile 0.47` was checked in the runtime. This does not establish a clean dependency/image build. The host has limited free disk after keeping verified backups; monitor capacity and plan scoped archival or expansion without deleting business volumes or rollback evidence.

- Never edit an applied historical Alembic migration. Add a new forward migration for every schema change.
- Take and verify a complete database backup before applying schema migrations or destructive maintenance. The user clarified that carton cleanup means order information only: preserve customer rules, delivery rules, paper/specification options, customer/supplier masters, warehouses/bins and permissions; do not infer inventory or configuration deletion. The broad local reset script is disabled. Local carton test-data resets must retain the code-owned HEYUAN-DONGKANG fixed supplier configuration; the order form cannot maintain it and removing it blocks order creation.
- Preserve existing users, sessions, permissions and business records during normal deployments.
- Do not reset the database, delete the database volume, reseed production or grant broad cross-factory access as a deployment shortcut.
- Database startup guards intentionally refuse service when required migrations or schema contracts are missing. `backend/tools/repair_local_migration_state.py` is a read-only-by-default repair for the specific SQLite supplier-branch state whose pricing and quote-alternative tables already exist without their migration records. It compares those tables with immutable original migrations, refuses schema drift or foreign-key damage, and restores only missing canonical indexes and snapshot triggers before atomically adopting proven branches and running the real remaining Alembic chain. Applying requires an offline local API and a new verified full backup; never blindly stamp head or use the tool for an unrelated partial migration.
- Production updates must be based on an explicit repository revision and a clean, fast-forwardable tracked worktree.
- Deployment should preserve rollback evidence and verify application health after database and service
  changes. The production Web container waits for a healthy API and its own health check exercises the
  Nginx-to-API `/health` proxy path, so a static homepage alone is not considered deployment health. Frontend release acceptance must also fetch generated JS/CSS assets and compare their hashes; extracted static directories must be traversable by the Nginx worker (directories `0755`, files `0644`) rather than inherit a restrictive backup umask. If a
  running container's image object has been pruned, export a checksummed rootfs archive plus container
  metadata and import it as the rollback image instead of failing before cutover.
- Migration `20260723_0027` merged per-factory raw-material masters into the global master. Its data transformation is irreversible without a pre-migration backup.
- Migration `20260723_0028` introduced production-factory dispatch and factory-scoped inventory behavior. Its preflight rejects ambiguous Huakang C/D production history and unscoped inventory; rollback requires a backup.
- Migration `20260727_0037` removes the rebuilt injection-scheduling tables, permissions, IAM markers and PostgreSQL audit trigger. Its downgrade is intentionally blocked; recovery requires a backup from before removal.
- Migration `20260727_0038` adds an empty optional standalone hair section and immutable initial revision to every historical internal quote; downgrade is allowed only while those migrated hair sections remain untouched.
- Migration `20260728_0039` creates the new injection-scheduling backend, permission family and immutable audit contract. Its downgrade refuses to run after an import batch exists; production deployment requires the normal backup and migration preflight.
- Migration `20260731_0042` irreversibly removes the `injection_scheduling_*` backend, its data and permissions before redesign. Downgrade is blocked; recovery requires a verified pre-removal database backup.
- Migration `20260731_0043` rebuilds only factory-scoped injection-scheduling machine, mold and versioned rule master data, seeds conservative per-factory defaults and the eight canonical permissions, and refuses downgrade after master data or custom rule revisions exist.
- Migration `20260731_0044` adds factory-scoped orders, plans, tasks, append-only ShiftReports, immutable plan revisions and published snapshots, audit-event polling and database guards for published-plan immutability and one active running task per machine. Downgrade is refused after any Phase 3 business or audit row exists.
- Migration `20260731_0045` adds factory-scoped Excel import batches and append-only row issues, request/payload idempotency, source-file/sheet/row lineage on tasks, confirmed-batch and published-lineage immutability guards, and refuses downgrade after any import preview or imported task exists.
- Migration `20260802_0046` adds the independent password-reset request state machine, reviewer and notification linkage, scope metadata, issuance count and expiry/completion timestamps. Its downgrade refuses to run after any password-reset request exists.
- Migration `20260819_0079` adds the nullable unique SHA-256 claim hash used by the same-browser password-reset flow. Existing rows remain claim-less legacy requests, and the migration can remove and restore the claim column and unique index without rewriting existing request state.
- Migration `20260804_0047` irreversibly removes the current injection-scheduling tables, data, permissions, IAM markers and database guard functions. Recovery requires a verified pre-removal database backup.
- Migration `20260804_0048` rebuilds the injection-scheduling V2 Phase 0 backend after the removal boundary, restores the eight canonical permissions and execution/import guards, adds raw and numeric A-class fields plus normalization/process metadata, and refuses downgrade after V2 business data exists. Application startup refuses to let `create_all` bypass this migration.
- Migration `20260804_0050` adds factory-scoped auto-schedule runs and assignments, transition rules, machine availability calendars and automatic-plan provenance fields. Applying a preview remains revision- and snapshot-guarded; application startup refuses service when the Phase 3 schema is incomplete.
- Migration `20260804_0051` adds the Phase 4 requested/actual solver, solver status, fallback reason, scenario group/name/alternative and replay-source metadata to auto-schedule runs. Application startup refuses service when these columns are missing.
- Migration `20260804_0052` adds Phase 5 ERP/device integration cursors and events, cycle observations and speed models. Its downgrade refuses after any Phase 5 history exists, and application startup refuses service while the Phase 5 schema is incomplete.
- Migration `20260805_0056` adds factory-bound, versioned injection-scheduling import Profiles and their governance permission. Its downgrade rejects custom, changed, rebound or referenced Profile state.
- Migration `20260805_0057` adds plan-aware takeover and successor-lineage persistence plus append-only reconciliation/progress evidence. Its downgrade rejects takeover data and prevents a cascading downgrade from partially crossing the protected 0056 boundary.
- Migration `20260807_0058` adds public planning bindings, recoverable import artifacts and signed-export audit evidence. Its downgrade refuses to discard those artifacts, and SQLite/PostgreSQL guards reject export-audit updates and deletes.
- Migration `20260809_0059` adds demand-order import state and the company/shared-mold plus factory physical-asset model; it intentionally seeds company membership only and no commercial rates. Migration `20260809_0060` adds conservative rollout policies for all six factories and physical-asset assignment lineage. Compatibility migration `20260810_0061` follows the remote customer-order `0059` plus injection branch and conditionally supplies the customer-order audit columns missing from already-migrated local databases. Local SQLite includes the compatibility migration; its current head is documented in the carton collaboration contract. Production still requires its own verified backup, migration and real-workbook reconciliation.
- Repository configuration examples are not proof of the live production authorization mode, secrets, migration state or running revision. Verify live state before any production action.
- Production Compose injects only the three required `POSTGRES_*` variables into PostgreSQL; the API
  alone receives the complete untracked `.env.production`.

## 8. Active Known Issues

- Authenticated read-only page entry is globally enabled in the frontend policy. Whether this is the permanent product rule or a temporary rollout policy is not yet settled.
- The 3D API/Web and standalone cloud Connector image have been deployed. The HTTP randomUUID white-screen fix is live. Cloud preparation resides in /opt/royal-regent/three-d; the Windows field bundle is D:/RR/3D现场配置包.zip. Tailscale enrollment, Windows routes, cloud TCP/TLS verification and network collector activation are complete. All eleven printers now have sustained read-only MQTT telemetry in the new project, including real running, completed and failed job states. The prior complete legacy snapshot is reconciled, and missing print history is supplemented through 2026-09-17 10:24:33 +08:00; the latter is an append-only partial batch. Subsequent changes in the still-running old writer are not automatically copied. Physical serial identity and production-control ownership transfer remain pending. The old writer remains active; no printer control command has been sent. DERP relay latency can vary. Old-writer handoff and real device pause/resume verification remain field work.
- Bambu LAN control behavior can vary by installed firmware, so remote pause/resume must remain an administrator-only, field-accepted capability.
- Customer Order Center has an independent historical schedule migration preview/confirmation entry, but real business cutover is not yet posted or accepted. The 24 supplied schedules have only undergone read-only dry runs; customer owners must verify sections, selected rows, duplicate delivery identities and opening quantities before posting. Special delivery/return rules and automatic warehouse shipment feedback remain pending; production-feedback detail is outside the agreed order-center scope.
- Customer-order warning thresholds shown by the frontend, including day-based exception thresholds, are not yet confirmed as authoritative business rules.
- Explicitly identified Indonesia customer-order contract and schedule processing is outside the current BuzzBee parser contract; WMU identifies a supported mainland Walmart US contract and must not be treated as Indonesia.
- Many module cards and dashboard metrics still use demonstration data and need explicit replacement plans before they can be treated as operational.
- The repository alone cannot confirm the live production `AUTHZ_MODE`, permission-write posture, database head or deployed application revision.
- The former AI subsystem is retired. Historical migration files remain only so existing databases can advance safely to `20260826_0084`; application code must not import or recreate the removed routes, models, services, tables, provider settings or UI surfaces. The checked-in `Dockerfile.backend` no longer copies removed `docs/ai` files or runs the retired AI `SkillRegistry`; it validates application syntax with compileall. A complete clean container build still needs verification in an environment with Docker and model-download access. A deployment that reuses a verified existing dependency layer with unchanged dependency manifests and replaces application source from a pinned Git tree does not establish that the standard clean build works.
- Injection Scheduling V3 is enabled locally at `20260905_0100` with Huaxing business data and separately maintained equipment and shared mold catalogs. Live PostgreSQL migration and a production V3 rollout remain unverified and require their own backup, rehearsal and authorization. A merged PR does not deploy or modify production data.

## 9. Current Next Steps

The smallest unresolved decisions that require product or operational confirmation are:

- Decide whether authenticated users should permanently retain global read-only page entry, or whether page entry must return to permission-gated behavior.
- All eleven printers have live read-only MQTT observation. The supplied snapshot is reconciled; obtain a newer snapshot for subsequent old-writer changes and arrange an idle-device window for actual production-control handoff. Cloud TCP/TLS and recurring network health are verified; physical pause/resume remains unverified.
- Use Customer Order Center historical migration previews to choose and reconcile the first customer cutover batch, including explicit statuses and opening quantities; then agree exceptional delivery/return rules, the Indonesia schedule phase, and any future automatic warehouse feedback contract. Preserve existing PO mappings while extending the ledger.
- Confirm the intended production authorization mode and IAM-write rollout before enabling permission configuration changes.
- Complete the remaining injection-scheduling master-data capability gaps and verify other factories' real workbook contracts before expanding use. Preserve the existing V3 business records and keep production migration/release separately authorized.
- Inventory the remaining demonstration module cards, then prioritize each as an implemented integration, a deliberately retained placeholder or a removal candidate.

When one of these decisions becomes an implemented, verified long-lived fact, update the relevant section in place and remove the corresponding unresolved item.
